When staging exists (D-2, `owner`), the owner (or engineering with the owner's access) runs `show timezone;` and `select current_setting('TimeZone');` as the API roles on the staging database and records the result here; a value other than UTC is not an error, but any difference from CI's UTC is written down so test results transfer. Engineering never touches the live project `jwmfxrmougneeceldhsa` for this.

### ADR-069 — CGR-35: a move of a flagged past visit stores that its responsible member was absent
**Status:** PROPOSED · **Task:** AN2 first change (issue #205): CGR-35 (PO decision 2026-10-23 23:15 in `docs/agents/briefs/ABS1.md`; `cra` 22:30, advisory S3, PS-28) with ABS1-C7 and DAY-1-R1–R3 in the same PR. Migration 0056, pgTAP `057_an2_cgr35_moved_keeps_absence` (24), unit tests (HAND1 line, singular text), e2e text update.
**Context:** a past planned visit flagged by CGR-32 (ADR-065) is most often moved forward ("Folgeeinsatz") with `reschedule_visit`. The `moved` row read as an ordinary planning change; once the absence was shortened or withdrawn, the fact that care due on the original date was not given because the responsible member was reported absent was lost. ADR-066 closed the same gap for `cancel_visit`. Same answer (product authority, documentation integrity, not a clinical rule): store what NAVORA already knows; no new reason code, no extra tap.
**Decision:**
- `visit_plan_changes.responsible_absent boolean not null default false`, check: only a `moved` row can carry true. The table stays append-only (0014 trigger), `authenticated` keeps select only, `reschedule_visit` has no parameter for it. Existing rows get false.
- `reschedule_visit` (patched on its current definition; every find must match exactly once; idempotent through a marker) reads, before the update, `planned` and dated before today (Europe/Zurich) and `private.visit_responsible_absent` (0053), and stores the result on its `moved` row. The read sits inside the ABS1-C5 subtransaction, so a refused move writes neither the row nor its audit.
- Audit: `visit_plan_changes` had no audit trigger and its id is a bigint, so the generic `write_audit_event` (entity_id uuid) does not fit. A dedicated insert trigger `audit_visit_plan_moves` (only `change = 'moved'`) writes one `audit_events` row with entity = the visit, table `visit_plan_changes`, `changed = {change, responsible_absent}`, command and idempotency key of the calling command. It also audits the `moved` rows written by `accept_cover` and `withdraw_cover` (always false); cover moves are out of CGR-35's scope.
- Readers of a `moved` row: HAND1 `private.handover_entries` adds `responsible_absent` to the detail of plan-change entries; `/team` `team_cases().upcoming[].last_change` returns it. The app shows the fixed text "nicht durchgeführt: zuständige Person abwesend gemeldet" as a line of the HAND1 "Einsatz verschoben" entry (`entryLines`) and after the "Verschoben …: <reason>" line on `/team`. OPS1 lists no moves, so nothing changes there. No reason for the absence exists to show.
- ABS1-C7 (decides ADR-067 *Open*): `pastPlannedNotice(1)` reads "1 vergangener Einsatz ist noch offen: bitte im Team entscheiden"; n ≥ 2 unchanged.
**Consequences:** the stored fact survives a later withdrawal of the absence (asserted in 057). AN2 measure 8 counts moved flagged visits from this column. Whether missed care must be made up, and whether a move counts as making it up, stays `clinical` (D-28, D-31).
**Rollback:** re-run the definitions of `reschedule_visit` (0052 patch on 0050), `handover_entries` (0054) and `team_cases` from before 0056, drop the trigger and its function; the column can stay (default false).
**Open:** none for engineering. `cra` look at 0056 requested with the PR.

### ADR-070 — AN2 part 2: Pilot-Hypothesen as one read model; AN1 organisation-level suppression
**Status:** PROPOSED · **Task:** AN2 (issue #205, `docs/agents/briefs/AN2.md`), part 2; with the AN1 condition of the PO review 2026-10-22 17:15 (due "in engineering's next PR that touches `pilot_kpis`"; 0043 and 0048 touched it without it: recorded deviation, closed here). Migration 0057, pgTAP `059_an2_pilot_hypotheses` (57), fixtures of pgTAP 027, 030, 032, 033, 034, 048 adjusted, unit `analytics-hypotheses`, e2e in `analytics.spec.ts`.
**Context:** the brief asks for about a dozen measures, each from existing events, per team and organisation, never per nurse, with AN1's access and suppression rules; its open questions: one function or one per measure; can measures 1 and 10 reuse `exception_events`.
**Decision:**
- **One function** `public.pilot_hypotheses(p_month date)` returning `(organization_id, team_id, team_name, measure, detail, value, base, too_few, suppressed, note)`, `stable security definer`, `search_path = ''`, execute for `authenticated` only. Access exactly as `pilot_kpis` (coordination roles: organisation + all teams; members: their teams). One function keeps one access rule and one suppression rule; a measure is a CTE. `detail` carries a fixed code only (J5 reason codes for measure 7).
- **Period** = the visits dated in the month (calendar dates, Europe/Zurich by construction of `visit_date`), with their later events up to now. One cohort for every measure, so a figure never mixes "facts created in October" with "visits of September".
- **Every measure in every scope**: a catalogue cross-joined with the scopes, so a measure without events shows base 0 ("zu wenig Daten") instead of disappearing.
- **Suppression (AN1 rule, one definition):** `private.pilot_team_people` (distinct performers of completed visits in the period, AN1's `team_people`) and `private.pilot_org_suppressed` (fewer than 3 persons in the organisation, or the persons of all hidden teams together number 1 or 2). AN2 rows of a hidden team or organisation show **neither value nor base** (`suppressed = true`, note "Zu wenig Personen …"): the base is an inference channel too (the PO's AN1 review named it). `pilot_kpis` gets the organisation rule, patched on its current definition (every find must match exactly once); its existing behaviour (base shown on suppressed rows) is otherwise unchanged; `open_p1_exceptions` stays visible ("Stand jetzt", shown in OPS1 anyway, no person behind it).
- **Measures in this part:** 3, 5, 6, 7, 9, 11, 12 (sources and definitions in the migration header and on the page). Choices: measure 3 "coordination role" = the CGR-26 `coordination` label (admin/coach/operations of the organisation, not in the visit's team); measure 5 counts withdrawn correction drafts as versions, and "signed again with no change" requires the same snapshot hash **and** the same summary (the summary is not in the snapshot; a summary-only change is a change); measure 6 re-derives the `report_correction_open` window from the effective `report_not_final` threshold (0047), ends at final (signature or countersignature) or withdrawal; measure 7 (AN2-M7) keeps `below_minimum` on its own row with the fixed note and leaves facts whose *only* reason is `below_minimum` out of the total and of the days-to-billable median (a fact with another reason as well stays in the total); measure 9 "P1 threshold" = planned end + `visit_stuck` amount + its P1 escalation hours; measure 12 "visit end" = the last `completed` event with time corrections (AN1's `ended_at`).
- Thresholds are read as they are now (`private.effective_exception_rules`); no history of rule values exists, so a changed override re-colours past months. Stated on the page ("Schwelle wie heute eingestellt (provisorisch, D-28)").
- **UI:** `/analytics` section "Pilot-Hypothesen", one block per scope (`role="group"`, so AN1's region names stay unique), one card per hypothesis (the brief's number, a German one-liner, the challenge row it tests by title, the source), one line per measure with value, "Basis", period. At phone width the sidebar is hidden and AN1 tables scroll in their own box; the page never scrolls sideways.
**Consequences:** AN1's organisation rows are now hidden in small pilots (the seed shows "zu wenig Personen" everywhere). Six existing pgTAP files got a fixture (three more synthetic performers in two small teams, or a reshaped first team) so their organisation figures stay visible; pgTAP 032's last assertion ("the organisation figure stays") is replaced by the PO's condition, which says the opposite for that fixture.
**Not built (PLANNED, next part):** measures 1, 2, 4, 8 (with AN2-M8a), 10. Measures 1 and 10 will reuse `exception_events` with the derived exception keys, as `cover_without_operations` does (answer to the brief's second question: yes).
**Rollback:** drop `public.pilot_hypotheses(date)`; re-run `pilot_kpis` from before 0057 (0048 state); drop the two helpers.
**Open:** none for engineering. The `cra` 02:26 proposals (minute distribution around the minimum duration; planned to actual start) are not in the brief and not built: PO.

### ADR-071 — AN2 part 3: measures 1, 2, 4, 8 (AN2-M8a) and 10 replayed from logs
**Status:** PROPOSED · **Task:** AN2 (issue #205, `docs/agents/briefs/AN2.md`), part 3; completes the brief's measure list. Migration 0058, pgTAP `060_an2_pilot_hypotheses_part3` (48), pgTAP 059 count 17 → 35 (catalogue grew; no assertion weakened), unit `analytics-hypotheses`, e2e `analytics.spec.ts` (12 hypothesis cards, AN2-M8a row).
**Context:** the five remaining measures need the *history* of an exception, a responsible member or an absence, while exceptions are derived from current state (ADR-035) and only their handling is logged.
**Decision:**
- **Same read model** `public.pilot_hypotheses(p_month)` (ADR-070), re-created with the same signature, access, suppression and period; one CTE per measure. Every 0057 measure is unchanged (copied verbatim; the diff adds CTEs, catalogue rows and notes only).
- **Measure 1** replays the responsible member from `visit_plan_changes` (`released`, `taken`, `returned`; before the first change its `before`, else today's value). The `visit_uncovered[_hospital]` exception starts at the first moment inside its window (planned start minus the effective threshold; kept after the start, 0038 PS-2) with nobody responsible, and ends with the next change of the responsible member. "Taken by the team" = that change is a `taken` (ABS1 "Offen im Team") with no OPS1 `take`/`close` on the key before it: one definition with AN1 `cover_without_operations` (the brief's second question: yes, `exception_events` and the derived keys are reused).
- **Measure 2:** `follow_up_planned` decisions (0045) with no visit of the same case *created* within 48 h (a `planned` row in `visit_plan_changes`, or a new `care_visits` row). Decisions younger than 48 h without a visit are not judged yet. A moved existing visit does not count (the brief says "created"); the row says so.
- **Measure 4:** `moved` rows, and `released`/`taken`/`returned` rows that change the responsible member, less than 24 h before the start **as planned at the change** (the row's `before` slot), per 100 visits of the month. Absence releases are included (the brief names all responsible-member changes).
- **Measure 8:** absences whose start (Europe/Zurich date) is in the month; team rows count the team's rows, the organisation row each `absence_group` once (ABS1-C3). "Revised within 48 h" = shortened (the `ends_at` audit row with an old value) or withdrawn within 48 h of recording, or followed within 48 h by a new absence of the same member adjacent to or overlapping the period as first recorded. Past flagged visits are replayed as CGR-32 saw them at recording time (planned, no visit event before the recording, dated before the recording day, naming her, interval as planned then — a later move's `before` slot — inside the absence as first recorded); their first ending is one fixed code: `started_late`, `cancelled` (CGR-34 mark), `cancelled_without_mark`, `moved` (CGR-35 mark), `moved_before_capture`, `still_open`, `other`.
- **AN2-M8a:** a move counts as `moved` only when its row stores `responsible_absent = true`. 0056 recorded no time, so 0058 stores a capture start in `private.an2_capture_starts` (RLS on, no grants): the earlier of the migration time and the first stored `true`, a safe lower bound. A flagged visit moved before it shows `moved_before_capture` with the note "vor Erfassung (nicht gezählt)", never "nicht abwesend".
- **Measure 10:** `visit_overdue` (one per visit: planned start + effective threshold, while still planned) and `visit_stuck` (one per status change into on the way, started or paused, key `visit_stuck:<visit>:<event>`, reference as in 0038). Ended by the next visit event (a command) or closed by hand first (`exception_events` `close` on the key). Base = every such exception in the period, also those still open. Since CSR1 (0044, CGR-06) these escalating exceptions cannot be closed by hand, so "closed by hand" measures the time before that rule and confirms it after.
- Rows that depend on a threshold carry "Schwelle wie heute eingestellt (provisorisch, D-28)" (1, 10), as in 0057.
- **AN2-M9a** (PO 2026-10-24 07:10, with ADM1-R): the measure-9 row `visits_open_past_p1` names the value it used, from the organisation's effective `visit_stuck` rule today: "Schwelle heute: geplantes Ende + <n> Std. + <m> Std. bis P1. Wie heute eingestellt (provisorisch, D-28)."
**Known limits (stated, not hidden):** thresholds are today's values (no history; with CGR-39 open, a lowered look-ahead override such as 0 h for `visit_uncovered` would shrink measure 1's window); historical suspension and cover state are not replayed for measure 1; a visit removed from the plan has left `care_visits` and every measure (its history row stays); measure 8's flag uses today's responsible member, and the moved slot only from the first move after the recording.
**Not built:** nothing from the brief's measure list remains. The `cra` 02:26 proposals (minute distribution, planned-to-actual start) stay with the PO.
**Rollback:** re-run `public.pilot_hypotheses(date)` from 0057; drop `private.an2_capture_starts`.
**Open:** none for engineering.

### ADR-072 — ADM1-R (CGR-39): a direction per exception rule; an override is never later than the default
**Status:** PROPOSED · **Task:** ADM1 changes (issue #262, `docs/agents/briefs/ADM1.md`, PO 2026-10-24 07:10, ADM1-R1 to R6); finding CGR-39 (`cra` 2026-10-24 06:30, blocking S1); remediation plan `docs/agents/clinical/remediation/CGR-39.md`. Migration 0059, pgTAP `061_adm1_cgr39_direction_aware_range` (49), pgTAP 035 (the inverted assertions replaced, count unchanged), unit `admin-rules`, e2e `admin.spec.ts`. Amends ADR-046 (safe ranges).
**Context:** ADR-046 bounded every threshold override from 0 to the default (P1) or twice the default, as if a larger amount always meant a later signal. Five readers use the amount as a **look-ahead window** (larger = earlier signal), so the bound was inverted for them: the P1 hospital-discharge rule could be set to 0 h and never fire.
**Decision:**
- `public.exception_rules.amount_direction` ∈ {`look_ahead`, `delay`}; a check constraint makes it required exactly when the rule has an amount, so a new amount-bearing rule cannot be added without a direction. Values with the reader lines that prove them are in the migration header: look-ahead `visit_uncovered_hospital`, `visit_uncovered`, `order_expiring`, `team_cancellation_late`, `cover_without_access`; delay `case_not_ready`, `report_not_final` (also times `report_correction_open`), `visit_overdue`, `visit_stuck`.
- **Never later than the default** (until D-28 sets a clinical band): look-ahead from the default up to 4× (cap 720); delay from a floor up to the default. Floor 0 (0 means "at once" in every delay reader), except `visit_stuck` with 1 h: at 0 every visit would be "stuck" the moment it starts, so the rule would no longer mean anything. 4× and the floor are engineering bounds against floods and meaningless rules, not clinical values. Priority never lower and escalation never later: unchanged.
- One check, `private.exception_override_in_range`, used by `set_exception_rule` and by the one-time reset; `private.exception_rule_range` and `public.admin_exception_rules` return the direction and the lower bound.
- **Existing overrides** outside the new range are deleted (= back to the platform default) by `private.adm1_reset_out_of_range_overrides()`, one `audit_events` row each (`command = 'adm1_range_reset'`, old priority, amount, escalation, who set it and when; actor null = maintenance). The migration raises the count as a NOTICE. On a fresh database it is 0 (the seed has no override and runs after the migrations).
- `/admin` shows a fixed general text ("Sicherheitsausnahmen melden sich nie später als der Standard"), the range per rule ("7 bis 28") and which way is stricter ("Früher melden = mehr Tage Vorlauf" / "weniger Stunden Wartezeit"); the client check mirrors the database and names both.
- `private.effective_exception_rules` returns `setof public.exception_rules`, so it is re-created with the new column; every reader and every default keeps its result (ADM1-R6).
**Not chosen:** a table-level guard on `exception_rule_overrides` (only the security-definer commands write it; pgTAP 047 writes one directly as the owner to test a reader, and R6 forbids editing it); clamping overrides inside `effective_exception_rules` (would silently change what an organisation sees configured).
**Rollback:** re-create `exception_rule_range`, `set_exception_rule` and `admin_exception_rules` from 0035, drop `exception_override_in_range`, `adm1_reset_out_of_range_overrides`, the two constraints and the column (re-creating `effective_exception_rules` from 0035). Reset overrides are not restored (their old values are in the audit rows). Rollback would reopen CGR-39 and needs a PO decision.
**Open:** the clinical band per rule (D-28, `clinical`); team-level overrides (D-28 option C) out of scope.

### ADR-073 — COORD5: the watcher asks a person only when the decision package does
**Status:** PROPOSED · **Task:** COORD5 (issue #240, PR #271, `docs/agents/briefs/COORD5.md`, PO 2026-10-24); process `docs/agents/DECISION_PACKAGES.md` (owner, 2026-10-24). Amends ADR-039 (coordination watcher). Files: `scripts/agents-coordination/parse.mjs`, `plan.mjs`, `run.mjs`, `scripts/agents-check.mjs`, `.github/workflows/agents-coordination.yml`; tests `tests/unit/agents-coordination.test.ts`, `tests/unit/agents-layer.test.ts`.
**Context:** the watcher assigned every open `owner`/`legal`/`clinical` decision to the owner on creation (29 issues). Since 2026-10-24 the agents first prepare a package; `decisions/INDEX.md` is the authoritative package state.
**Decision:**
- **Where INDEX is read (brief's open question):** in `parse.mjs`, next to the three official files, as an optional input of `readLayerTexts` (`index`, `packageFiles`). INDEX is **not** added to the official layer: it stays PO-owned under the decision-package process, and the planner stays pure (run.mjs does the file I/O). INDEX has no `## ` heading, so a heading-less first-table reader (`parseFirstTable`) is added; the status is the link text of the *Package status* cell (`[awaiting-approval: owner](D-22.md) (…)`) or its first word.
- **Routing for owners `owner`, `legal`, `clinical`:** `awaiting-approval: owner|legal|clinical` **and** a linked package file → labels `agents`, `human-decision`, assigned to `OWNER_LOGIN` (until README *Participants* names other GitHub accounts); the body links the package (GitHub blob URL on `recovery/main`) and quotes its *Owner approval required?* section (capped at 4000 characters). Every other state (`not-started`, `inputs-requested`, no INDEX row, an unknown value, a status without a linked file) → `agents`, `agent:po`, `decision-package`, nobody assigned. Fail-safe direction: when in doubt, the agents keep the item; nobody is pulled in by a malformed row (the validator turns it red instead).
- **`po-decided`/`decided` while the row is still in *Open*:** stays a `decision-package` issue and the validator warns (brief, negative criterion). The issue closes only when the row leaves *Open* (moved to *Decided*), with the existing close reason. This reconciles the two criteria "po-decided → closes" and "po-decided in Open keeps decision-package": closing waits for the record in `OPEN_DECISIONS.md`, which is what the README requires anyway.
- **Existing issues:** the diff now also compares whether the owner is assigned (decision issues only). An update removes **only** `OWNER_LOGIN` from the assignees (others stay) or adds it; `decision-package` is a managed label, so it is swapped with `human-decision` both ways; `claimed` and other unmanaged labels survive. The German comment is posted once, on the sync that moves an issue from `human-decision` (label or owner assigned) to `decision-package`; the next sync finds nothing to change.
- **`po`/`eng` decisions:** unchanged (labels `agent:po` / `agent:eng`, nobody assigned).
- **Validator:** every *Open* decision owned by `owner`/`legal`/`clinical` has an INDEX row; status in the DECISION_PACKAGES.md list; `awaiting-approval:*` and `po-decided` need `decisions/<ID>.md`; an INDEX ID must be in *Open* or *Decided*; `po-decided`/`decided` still in *Open* is a warning. `--dry-run` prints the package status per decision issue and one summary line.
- The workflow also runs on pushes to `docs/agents/decisions/**`, so a status change reaches GitHub at once, not only at the hourly reconciliation.
**Not chosen:** reading the package's front matter (`status:`) instead of INDEX (two sources of truth; DECISION_PACKAGES.md names INDEX); closing an issue as soon as INDEX says `po-decided` (the row would still be open and nobody would see the missing record).
**Rollback:** revert the PR; the next watcher run re-labels every human-owned decision `human-decision` (no data lost; the comments stay). The old diff does not compare assignees, so the owner is **not** re-assigned on existing issues by a revert; re-assign by hand if needed.
**Open:** assigning legal advisers and the Clinical Owner directly once README *Participants* names their GitHub accounts (PLANNED, needs those names, owner).

### ADR-074 — SIG1: a P1 safety signal is a delivery to named people, written by the server
**Status:** PROPOSED · **Task:** SIG1 (issue #246, `docs/agents/briefs/SIG1.md`, PO 2026-10-24); finding CGR-37 (`cra` 2026-10-24 02:26, advisory S2); remediation plan `docs/agents/clinical/remediation/CGR-37.md`. Migration 0060, pgTAP `062_sig1_safety_signal_delivery` (54), pgTAP 019, 039, 059, 061 (expected values for the new rule and measures), unit `signals-rules`, `analytics-hypotheses`, e2e `signals.spec.ts`.
**Context:** every visit safety signal was derived on read (`private.derived_exceptions`); nothing wrote a row when a signal reached P1, so nobody received it unless a screen was open. A delivery needs a writer and a record.
**Decision:**
- **Episode, not exception:** `public.safety_signals` holds one row per continuous P1 period of an exception key (`visit_overdue:<visit>`, `visit_stuck:<visit>:<event>`, …). A partial unique index allows one open row per key; when the key is no longer P1 (cause gone, or the rule changed) the row gets `ended_at`; the same key later is a new episode (SIG1-2). P1 is computed exactly as `team_visit_alerts` does, from `private.effective_exception_rules` (no second copy of a threshold, SIG1-9). P2/P3 are never delivered.
- **Deliveries:** `public.signal_deliveries`, unique per (signal, recipient), with kind, visit, recipient, reason (`responsible`, `team`, `operations_fallback`, `untaken`) and time; no name, no free text. Recipients: the responsible member (`responsible_user_id`, or the covering member of a covered visit, CGR-21) if still in the owning team or the covering member of the same organisation; every member of the owning team; nobody absent now (ABS1); if that leaves nobody, the organisation's `operations` users (admins only when it has none). A coach is never a recipient by role.
- **Writer (brief question 2):** `private.evaluate_signals(org, force)`, `security definer`, serialised per organisation by `pg_try_advisory_xact_lock` (a concurrent run is skipped, it would write the same). Called every minute by pg_cron (`navora-sig1-signals`, where the extension exists) and by `public.my_signals()` on each page poll (throttled: at most once per 15 s per organisation). CI tests it by calling the writer with `force` inside pgTAP; no clock is faked.
- **Polling, not realtime (brief question 1):** `/field`, `/team` and OPS1 poll `my_signals` every 30 s through a server action. Realtime would need a publication on the delivery table and RLS-checked channel joins on every phone; polling reuses the RPC and RLS already tested and costs one small request per 30 s while the page is open. A new P1 appears within at most ~45 s (poll + throttle).
- **Take:** `public.take_signal(signal, key)`; only a recipient; `duplicate` for the same key and for any tap after the first; audited (`taken_by`, `taken_at`, command, key). It never ends the signal (CR-041).
- **Untaken (SIG1-5):** rule `signal_untaken` in `exception_rules` (P1, 15 `minutes`, direction `delay`): the ADM1-R range guard already allows only stricter values (0 to 15). Past it, an untaken open signal gets `untaken` deliveries to Operations once (`escalated_at`).
- **Record integrity:** no foreign key to `care_visits` (a removed planned visit must not be blocked by its record, and the record survives it); guards refuse any change other than setting `ended_at`, `taken_*`, `escalated_at` once, and any change or delete of a delivery; audit triggers on both tables (system writes carry command `evaluate_signals`, actor null).
- **RLS:** a person reads only deliveries addressed to her and the signals behind them; no write privilege for any user role; column grants only.
- **AN2 measure 10 (SIG1-6):** `signal_taken_share`, `signal_minutes_to_take` (median minutes from P1 to taken) per team and organisation; AN1 suppression via the existing final select.
**Not chosen:** Supabase realtime (see above); an Edge Function scheduler (one more deploy unit; pg_cron is already used by PLAN2 and COVER1); a trigger on visit changes (P1 is reached by the passing of time, not by a write); storing the threshold in a new table (ADM1 already has the safe-range guard).
**Rollback:** drop `my_signals`, `take_signal`, the writer functions, the cron job, both tables, the type and `private.signal_evaluations`; delete the `signal_untaken` rule (and overrides); re-create `pilot_hypotheses` from 0058 and `after_threshold` from its previous body. Deliveries are lost on rollback (they are in `audit_events`). Reopens CGR-37; needs a PO decision.
**Open:** delivery outside the app (SIG2, D-32/D-33); response duties and on-call (D-28, `clinical`); whether an absent responsible member should still receive the signal (deviation, PO); `cover_without_access` delivery (PO).

### ADR-075 — ORD0: "doctor informed" is its own audited record; an open-ended B order is a P3 exception that closes with its cause
**Status:** PROPOSED · **Task:** ORD0 (issue #247, `docs/agents/briefs/ORD0.md`, PO 2026-10-24 03:10 and 07:10); findings CGR-36, CGR-38 (`cra` 02:26, advisory S2), CGR-40 (`cra` 06:30, advisory S3); remediation plans `docs/agents/clinical/remediation/CGR-36.md`, `CGR-38.md`. Migration 0061, pgTAP `063_ord0_gp_informed_and_open_end_order` (53); pgTAP 019, 059, 061 expected counts updated (one more rule, two more measures).
**Context:** the `inform_gp` task had no completing command and closed only with the case; an order `present` without an end date raised nothing. Both are product decisions of the PO; deadline, content, order duration and billing effect stay D-22 (`legal`, `clinical`).
**Decision:**
- **Separate command (brief question 1):** `public.record_gp_informed(case, informed_on, channel, key)` writes `public.case_gp_notices` and closes the task in one transaction. `complete_case_task` keeps refusing `inform_gp`, so the only way to close it is the typed record (date and one of four channel codes) — a generic "done" tap would lose exactly the fact D-22 needs. Results `ok`, `duplicate` (key replay, via the audit row as every command), `unchanged` (no open task), `rejected` with a reason (`not_team`, `case_closed`, `future`, `before_case`).
- **Dates (CGR-40):** Europe/Zurich days; not after today, not before the case's creation day.
- **Record integrity:** append-only (guard trigger refuses update and delete, also for the owner), audited (`write_audit_event`), no write grant; RLS read: the recording team, the case's current team, coordinators of the organisation; no free text, no health data.
- **No reopening (needed for ORD0-2):** PLAN2 refreshes the plan on every `/team` load and would reopen `inform_gp` at once. Planning and PLAN2 therefore skip the task for a case that has a record. When the team must be asked again is **Q12 (`po`)**; a returning client gets a new case and a new task as before.
- **Exception without a new table (brief question 2):** `order_without_end_date` is one more branch of `private.derived_exceptions` (P3 rule row, no threshold). The condition lives in one function (`private.order_without_end_date_holds`). It is added to the three places that make an exception close only with its cause (`exception_closes_with_cause`, `operations_exceptions` `closable` and visibility filter) and to `team_visit_alerts` (with `visit_id` null for a case alert). "Running" = lifecycle `active` with a team; a paused case does not prompt.
- **Evidence (ORD0-6):** the exception is derived on read, so its history cannot be replayed reliably from audit rows. `private.order_open_end_episodes` is maintained by triggers on `cases` (order status, end date, lifecycle, team) and `care_plan_items` (active, category) and records how each period ended; cases already in that state are backfilled as open from the migration time. AN2 measure 10 gains `order_open_end_ending` (share per ending, base = periods started in the month, open ones included) and `inform_gp_done_within_7d_share` (tasks created in the month, closed by a record within 7 days of creation; not by case close).
**Not chosen:** extending `complete_case_task` with a typed payload (one command with two shapes, and the `cra` test "still refused" would be void); refusing `present` without an end date at intake (the `cra` alternative; `lca` reads open-ended B orders as possible in some cases, so the PO chose a prompt); replaying measure 10 from `audit_events` (several tables, lifecycle not audited the same way).
**Rollback:** drop `record_gp_informed`, `case_gp_notices`, the episode table, its triggers and functions; delete the `order_without_end_date` rule (and overrides); re-create `plan_visit`, `generate_case_plan`, `derived_exceptions`, `exception_closes_with_cause`, `operations_exceptions`, `team_visit_alerts`, `handover_entries` and `pilot_hypotheses` from their previous bodies (0060 and earlier). Records are lost on rollback; tasks closed by a record stay `done`.
**Open:** Q12 (when `inform_gp` opens again); D-22 (deadline, content, billing effect, order duration, issue date and GLN/ZSR of the order).

### ADR-076 — SIG2: outside delivery by web push, written by the database, sent by the app server with its own key
**Status:** PROPOSED · **Task:** SIG2 (issue #283, `docs/agents/briefs/SIG2.md`, PO 2026-10-24 11:09); D-32 (po, option B narrow variant) and its addendum for CGR-41 points 1-3; finding CGR-41 (`cra` 10:26, advisory S2); remediation plan `docs/agents/clinical/remediation/CGR-41.md`. Migration 0062, pgTAP `064_sig2_outside_delivery` (82), pgTAP 059 (two more measures), unit `push-webpush`, `push-rules`, `signals-rules`, `csp`, `auth`, e2e `push.spec.ts`.
**Context:** SIG1 (ADR-074) delivers a P1 to named people only inside an open page. D-32 allows one fixed, content-free web push through Google (FCM) and Microsoft (Windows push) endpoints only, opt-in, within planned working time.
**Decision:**
- **Who decides, who sends (brief question 1):** the database decides every outside result in the SIG1 writer's transaction (`private.outside_after_evaluate` after `evaluate_signals`): `pending`, `not_active`, `outside_working_time`; the app server sends. The VAPID private key lives only in the server's environment; the database holds no secret. The sender claims due rows through `push_dispatch_claim` and reports through `push_dispatch_report` (skip locked; both executable by `service_role` only, so the endpoints and keys are never readable by a signed-in user). Entry points: `POST /api/push/dispatch` (bearer `PUSH_DISPATCH_SECRET`, timing-safe compare) for a one-minute scheduler, plus an in-process round after every SIG1 page poll and after an opt-in (test message). The service-role key is used in this one module only (`src/features/push/dispatch.ts`).
- **No library:** RFC 8291 (aes128gcm) and RFC 8292 (VAPID ES256) on `node:crypto` (`src/features/push/webpush.ts`, ~100 lines); the npm registry is not reachable from the engineering sandbox and the lockfile only changes through CI. Unit tests decrypt as the browser would and verify the JWT with the public key. **Not verified against a real push service** (CI never calls one): UNKNOWN until staging (D-2).
- **Fail closed:** a row not reported within 2 minutes is `failed` (`timeout`); a signal whose rows are all decided and none `sent` records `outside_none_at` and goes to Operations at once as untaken (CGR-41 point 1). With the sender not configured, every P1 of an organisation that switched on therefore reaches Operations at once in the app.
- **Vendors (brief question 3):** `public.push_vendors` holds the hosts: exact `fcm.googleapis.com`; suffix `.notify.windows.com` (WNS hosts carry a region prefix). An endpoint must be `https://<host>/…` with no port or user info; anything else (Apple `web.push.apple.com`, Mozilla `updates.push.services.mozilla.com`, look-alikes) is not stored. A vendor counts only while its Swiss-U.S. DPF entry is recorded as `active` with the date read; **this migration records none** (the official list is a JavaScript application the engineering tools could not read: UNKNOWN), so nothing is accepted and the channel cannot be switched on until a reviewed migration records the entries. A changed host is noticed as `not_available` on opt-in and as "nicht aktiv" on `/team`; it is never accepted silently.
- **Active:** a subscription whose last send succeeded (the test message or a signal), stricter than "did not fail": a device that never received anything is not counted as reachable. A 404/410 from the push service deletes the device.
- **Working time (D-32 item 2):** the span from the planned start of her first to the planned end of her last visit (responsible or covering) that Zurich day, any status except cancelled; the responsible member always; Operations whenever opted in (SIG2-7).
- **Watched hours (brief question 4):** a shifted window inside `derived_exceptions` (one `or` on the hospital-discharge uncovered branch, `private.watched_raise_at`), not an early raise in the writer: the writer ends any episode whose key is not in `derived_exceptions`, and `/team`, OPS1 and the signal must agree. Earlier only by construction (the original condition stays).
- **Records:** `signal_outside_deliveries` keyed by the SIG1 delivery id (the SIG1 row stays immutable); result once from `pending`, tap once; audited (system writes: command `evaluate_signals` / `push_dispatch`, actor null). `safety_signals.outside_none_at` is set once (guard extended). The tap is recorded when `/field?von=push` opens (the message carries no ID, so the caller's sent, untapped rows of the last 24 h); display is never claimed.
- **Payload (SIG2-8):** `{"text": "NAVORA: Ein Sicherheitssignal wartet auf dich.", "url": "/field?von=push"}`; the service worker shows the same constant and ignores the payload, so a message can never show other content.
- **Tests (brief question 2):** pgTAP plays the sender as `service_role` (claim, report) and moves `decided_at` back with triggers off for the timeout; `PUSH_TRANSPORT=test` makes the app sender accept without network; no clock is faked.
**Not chosen:** a `web-push` npm dependency (see above); an Edge Function sender (a second deploy unit and the key in another place); pg_net calling the app (a secret in the database); payload-less push (the brief names the payload); realtime.
**Rollback:** drop the routes and the push module; drop `push_dispatch_*`, `register_/unregister_push_subscription`, `my_push_state`, `team_push_status`, `record_push_tap`, `set_outside_push`, `set_watched_hours`, the private helpers, the four tables and `safety_signals.outside_none_at`; re-create `evaluate_signals`, `guard_safety_signal`, `derived_exceptions`, `pilot_hypotheses` and `my_signals` from 0060/0061. Outside results are lost (they are in `audit_events`).
**Open:** the DPF entries (eng or owner, a reviewed migration); D-2 (owner): VAPID key pair, `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`, `PUSH_DISPATCH_SECRET`, the one-minute scheduler, staging verification against FCM and WNS; D-33 (owner): Safari and Firefox; D-28 (clinical): night response.

### ADR-077 — ORD0-C2 and SIG1-C1: `inform_gp` opens again on an event; a care assistant's take of a P1 no-show does not stop the step to Operations
**Status:** PROPOSED · **Task:** *Next up* 1 (ORD0-C1, ORD0-C2, SIG1-C1 in one PR; `docs/agents/briefs/ORD0.md` 15:10 and 16:20, `docs/agents/briefs/SIG1.md` 15:10; issue #309 claimed for the queue); findings CGR-44 and CGR-43 (`cra` 14:27, advisory S2) with the `cra` note under CGR-43 (18:26: SIG2's immediate step too); remediation plans `docs/agents/clinical/remediation/CGR-43.md`, `CGR-44.md`. Migration 0063, pgTAP `065_ord0_c2_sig1_c1_conditions` (49), pgTAP 063 (+4, ORD0-C1), pgTAP 064 (one fixture made clock-independent), unit `signals-rules`.
**Context:** ADR-075 kept `inform_gp` from being reopened by every plan refresh, so a case with a record was never asked again (CGR-44). ADR-074 let any recipient's take stop the SIG1-5 step, so a care assistant's take of a P1 no-show ended the escalation (CGR-43). The PO decided both as product rules (Q12 (c) widened to (b)+(c); SIG1-C1 (a)-(e)); the clinical rules behind them stay D-22, D-28, D-19.
**Decision:**
- **Events, not refreshes (ORD0-C2):** two `after` triggers call one function `private.open_inform_gp_after_event(case, Zurich day)`: a care-plan item of category A or C becomes active (inserted active, re-activated, or changed to A/C while active), and a needs-assessment visit reaches `completed`. It opens one task unless the case is closed, the order is `present`, a task is already open (the existing one-open-per-kind index), or a record is dated on or after that day. Planning and PLAN2 keep the 0061 rule (first opening only), so a refresh never reopens. Each reopening is a new task row, so AN2 measure 10 counts each episode.
- **Every take is a row (SIG1-C1):** `public.signal_takes` (append-only, audited, no grants; read through `my_signals`) records taker, her qualification in the visit's team (the cover team for the covering member) and `stops_escalation`. `safety_signals.taken_by` stays the first taker, so the guard trigger and AN2 measure 10 (time to the first take) are unchanged. One predicate, `private.signal_taken_stops_step`, replaces `taken_by is null` in both steps (`evaluate_signals` SIG1-5 and `outside_settle` SIG2-5), patched in place with a match-count check.
- **Who stops the step:** kinds other than `no_show` and `visit_interrupted`: any take (as built, SIG1-C1 (c)). Those two kinds: a registered nurse of the visit's team, the covering member if a registered nurse in her cover team, **or a person the step would reach** (`signal_operations_users`: Operations, or admins when there is none). The last case is an engineering reading, not in the brief: the step exists to tell those people; when one of them has taken the signal, sending it to them again only marks it `escalated`. Recorded as a deviation for the PO.
- **Second take:** `take_signal` records a later take by somebody else while no take stops the step (a registered nurse after a care assistant); a take by the same person, or after a stopping take, returns `duplicate` as before. It returns `operations_follows`.
- **Reader:** `my_signals` gains `taken_by_qualification` and `taken_awaits_rn`; the taker shown is the stopping take, else the first. `taken_by_me` now means "I am the taker shown". The banner keeps such a signal at the top for everyone else, with "übernommen von <Name> (Pflegehilfe), Meldung an Operations folgt".
- **Backfill:** existing takes become rows judged by the same rule (protective; synthetic data only).
**Not chosen:** a second column on `safety_signals` (`rn_taken_by`): the guard trigger would need widening and a third taker would have nowhere to go; reading `team_memberships` at escalation time instead of storing the qualification (a later qualification change would rewrite history); reopening `inform_gp` on every plan refresh after a time limit (no deadline exists, D-22).
**Rollback:** drop the two triggers and their functions, `signal_takes` with its guard and helpers; re-create `take_signal` (0060), `my_signals` (0062), and `evaluate_signals` / `outside_settle` with `s.taken_by is null` (0062 bodies).
**Open:** D-28 (`clinical`): whether other kinds need a registered nurse, and whether Operations' take is enough; D-19 (no-show protocol); D-22 question 6 (deadline and content of informing the doctor).

### ADR-078 — SIG2 follow-up: the scheduled evaluator is seen to run; Operations outside the app only in watched hours; the look-ahead raise lies inside a watched period
**Status:** PROPOSED · **Task:** *Next up* 2 (SIG2-11, SIG2-12, SIG2-13 in one follow-up PR, `docs/agents/briefs/SIG2.md` 15:10 and 19:09; SIG2-14 from open PO PR #311, 20:09, built in the same PR); findings CGR-42 and CGR-45 (`cra` 14:27, 18:26, advisory S2), `lca` 15:05 (D-32 addendum); remediation plans `docs/agents/clinical/remediation/CGR-42.md`, `CGR-45.md`. Migration 0064, pgTAP `064_sig2_outside_delivery` (82 → 106), unit `signals-rules`, `push-rules`, e2e `push.spec.ts`.
**Context:** 0062 had no way to see that the pg_cron evaluator stopped: `private.signal_evaluations` is also written by every page poll, so an open page hid a dead job, and `/team` kept showing "aktiv" (CGR-42). The send path started from a page poll, or from an external caller of `/api/push/dispatch` that nothing scheduled. Pushes to Operations left at any hour, which `lca` 15:05 reads as an expectation to react outside working time (Pikett). The look-ahead raise time was the end instant of the last watched period, which the inside test treats as outside (CGR-45). The 064 fixture depended on the hour the suite ran (SIG2-14).
**Decision:**
- **Liveness (SIG2-11):** `private.signal_scheduler_runs` (one row per organisation, no grants, not audited: a heartbeat every minute would flood `audit_events`, as `signal_evaluations` already is not) is written only by `private.evaluate_all_signals`, the job `navora-sig1-signals`. Page polls call `evaluate_signals` directly and do not write it. Live means a run at most 5 minutes old (the PO's operating limit). `public.signal_evaluator_health()` returns, for the caller's organisations in which she is Operations or administrator, the last run and `running`; `/operations` and `/admin` show "Signalprüfung läuft nicht" from it. `my_push_state.active` and `team_push_status.active` are true only while the evaluator of that organisation is live. Delivery decisions (`push_active` in `outside_classify`) are unchanged: they run inside the evaluator, so a dead job decides nothing.
- **Send path (SIG2-11 (3)):** each scheduled run ends with `private.push_dispatch_kick()`: when a channel is on somewhere and pg_net and Supabase Vault exist with `navora_push_dispatch_url` (https) and `navora_push_dispatch_secret` (at least 32 characters), it POSTs to `/api/push/dispatch` with the bearer secret (pg_net is asynchronous, so the evaluator does not wait). Dynamic SQL keeps the function compilable where neither extension exists (CI). Anything missing → no call; pending rows still fail after 2 minutes and the signal goes to Operations at once (fail closed, unchanged). The page poll may still kick. The job is re-scheduled by name where pg_cron exists (idempotent).
- **Operations within watched hours (SIG2-12):** one predicate, `private.watched_inside(org, t)` (Zurich wall time, start inclusive, end exclusive, `24:00` = end of day), is patched into `outside_classify` for the reasons `untaken` (SIG2-7 and the SIG2-5 immediate step) and `operations_fallback` (SIG1's fallback, also a push to Operations): outside, or with no watched hours recorded, the row is `outside_working_time`; the in-app delivery is unchanged. `not_active` still comes first. `/admin` says "Keine überwachten Zeiten erfasst: Operations erhält Signale nur in der App"; the member information on `/settings` says Operations is reached outside the app only during the watched hours.
- **Raise time (SIG2-13):** `watched_raise_at` returns, for a window opening outside, the later of the start of the last watched period that ends at or before it and that period's end minus 2 hours. It is inside by the SIG2-12 predicate, never later than the 0062 time and never later than the effective rule.
- **Clock-free test (SIG2-14):** the look-ahead comparison is one function, `private.lookahead_raised(org, now, window_opens)`; `derived_exceptions` passes `now()`. pgTAP asserts it at fixed instants (03:00, 12:00, 17:59, 18:00, 19:30, 22:30 Zurich); the integration fixtures use watched hours relative to `now()` (0063) or the whole day (SIG2-12).
**Not chosen:** reading liveness from `signal_evaluations` (page polls write it); a second pg_cron job for the dispatcher (two schedules to keep in step; the evaluator is the one that knows when rows become pending); calling the push services from the database (the VAPID private key would live in the database, ADR-076); counting a missing run row as live (a project where the job was never scheduled would look healthy).
**Rollback:** re-create `watched_raise_at`, `my_push_state`, `team_push_status` and `evaluate_all_signals` with their 0062/0060 bodies; re-patch `outside_classify` and `derived_exceptions` back to the 0062 text; drop `signal_scheduler_runs`, `signal_evaluator_live`, `signal_evaluator_health`, `watched_inside`, `lookahead_raised`, `push_dispatch_kick`.
**Open:** D-28 (`clinical`) for thresholds and response times; who may be an outside recipient outside watched hours (a provider-designated on-call person, owner, later item); the production home of the sender and its key (D-26, `owner`); the pilot project's pg_net, Vault entries and pg_cron job (owner executes, OWNER_RUNBOOK).

### ADR-079 — F6R: a registered nurse returns a signed report to its performer; the returned version stays, a new version is corrected, signed and countersigned
**Status:** PROPOSED · **Task:** F6R (`docs/agents/briefs/F6R.md`, approved by the PO 2026-10-24 19:09; D-10 sub-question R decided by po under conditions (a)-(d), `cra` 18:26 in open PR #306, `lca` 11:55). Migration 0065, pgTAP `066_f6r_return_report` (82), `059` count 41 → 43, unit `field-report`, e2e `field.spec.ts` (F7 test extended).
**Context:** a report awaiting countersignature is frozen (0040) and only a `final` report can be corrected (0016, 0044), so a registered nurse who sees a wrong time had to countersign it first: the wrong version became `final`, the team's record and a billing basis (J5) until a correction round ended (C-7).
**Decision:**
- **Command:** `return_report(report, reason, key)`: allowed to a registered nurse of the report's team who is not the performer, only on the **latest** version while it is `signed` with `countersign_required`. Reason 10–500 characters (10: brief; 500: engineering bound, as `correct_visit_time`). Refusals are named: `not_team_member` (another team, an admin or coach without membership, operations, a client or partner account), `own_report`, `rn_required`, `not_latest`, `not_awaiting_countersignature` (draft, final, withdrawn, or no countersignature required), `reason_too_short`, `performer_not_in_team` (CR-048 interim, D-30: only she can sign again, so the report would strand). Idempotent and audited (`begin_command`/`end_command`).
- **Never in place:** signed versions stay immutable (0016 guard unchanged in substance). A return appends (1) a row in `visit_report_returns` (report, version, snapshot hash, reason, performer, returner, time): RLS for the performer and the team's registered nurses, no insert/update/delete grant for any app role or the service role, a trigger refusing update, delete and truncate for every role, audit without the reason; and (2) a new version (`status = 'draft'`, `supersedes_id` = the returned version, `return_id`, `correction_reason` null, the **next** version number: the brief's open question, answered like withdrawn corrections in 0049, so a returned number is never reused).
- **Who changes what (F6R-3):** while the latest version is a returned draft, `correct_visit_time` and `record_observation` accept only the performer (`private.visit_open_return_performer`); summary and signature were already performer-only. `countersign_report` now refuses a version that is no longer the latest (`not_latest`), so the returned version can never become `final`.
- **A return is no correction:** it cannot be withdrawn (`returned_report`; the guard also refuses it), it does not raise `report_correction_open`, and AN2 measure 5 does not count it. `report_not_final` is not touched: no version is final, so it keeps raising with the same threshold (CR-053).
- **Never stranded (F6R-5):** `field_returned_reports()` lists the performer's returned drafts on every date at the top of `/field` with the reason; `field_countersign_queue()` keeps them for the team's registered nurses as "zurückgegeben" until they are signed again; `remove_team_member` counts a returned version as not signed (`has_unsigned_reports`).
- **Reason as health data (F6R-7):** read only through RLS and four read models (`field_reports`, `field_returned_reports`, `field_countersign_queue` without the reason, `report_returns`), which null it for anyone but the performer and the team's registered nurses. pgTAP checks that no other function and no view reads the table.
- **Evidence (F6R-9):** AN2 `report_returned_share` (countersign-required first versions of the month returned at least once) and `countersign_latency_hours` (median from the first signature to the countersignature that made the report final, returns included), per team and organisation under the AN1 suppression rule. AN1 `pilot_kpis` takes the first final version's time (a returned version was never final; identical without returns).
**Not chosen:** moving the signed row back to `draft` (a signed version would change in place; its snapshot would survive only in the return row); a new status `returned` on the old row (every status reader would need it; "latest version" already expresses it); putting the reason in `visit_reports` (team-readable).
**Rollback:** re-create `field_reports` (0049 text), `field_countersign_queue` (0026), the guard (0049); re-patch `countersign_report`, `correct_visit_time`, `record_observation`, `withdraw_report_correction`, `derived_exceptions`, `remove_team_member`, `pilot_kpis`, `pilot_hypotheses` back; drop `return_report`, `field_returned_reports`, `report_returns`, `visit_open_return_performer`, `visit_reports.return_id` and `visit_report_returns` (only while no return exists: they are documentation records).
**Open:** who must countersign (D-10 A vs. B, `clinical`, after the pilot on the F6R-9 evidence); who corrects when the performer has left (D-30, `clinical`); a client's access extract including returned versions and reasons (D-4 gate r1, later item); H-04: a countersignature or a return attests only as much as the `registered_nurse` label is verified (D-29 real-data gate, unchanged).

### ADR-080 — RLS2: the four visit and case record tables are read only by the client's team and the active covering member; a read-path inventory with a catalogue check
**Status:** PROPOSED · **Task:** RLS2 (`docs/agents/briefs/RLS2.md`, approved by the PO 2026-10-24 22:10; D-3 real-data gate (iv), D-23 precondition 3 (a); `lca` 21:55; `cra` 22:26 in open PR #321). Migration 0066, pgTAP `067_rls2_team_scoped_records` (127), `012` and `017` adapted (below), inventory `docs/engineering/READ_PATHS.md`.
**Context:** 0015 (ADR-031) scoped the legacy care tables to the team. `visit_events`, `visit_time_corrections` and `case_tasks` kept `org_members_read` (every member of the organisation); `team_decisions` had been narrowed by 0017 to the deciding team plus every coordination role, with its free-text reason. `uncovered_visits()` returned every team's uncovered visits to every member. No screen reads any of the four tables directly (`src/`: no `.from()`); Operations, coaches and intake read them through `security definer` read models.
**Decision:**
- **Two predicates** (`security definer`, empty search path, executable by `authenticated` only for policies): `private.reads_visit(visit)`: member of the visit's team, or its active covering member (`covered_by` = caller, still in `cover_team_id` and the organisation, no `final` report: D-3 PO 2026-10-22 14:30 "until its report is final"); `private.reads_case(case)`: member of the case's assigned team. A cover never opens case-level rows.
- **Policies:** `team_scoped_read` replaces the old read policy on each table: `visit_events` and `visit_time_corrections`: org member and (member of the row's `team_id` or `reads_visit(visit_id)`); `team_decisions`: org member and member of the row's team; `case_tasks`: org member and `reads_case(case_id)`. **No coordination role** reads these rows through the table API (RLS2-2 c): every coordination task named in READ_PATHS.md (OPS1, SIG1, intake, J2 escalation, AN1/AN2) already uses a read model.
- **Free text (RLS2-3):** the row scope keeps `visit_time_corrections.reason` and `team_decisions.reason` inside the team (and the cover for the first). No column grant changes: `field_visits` is `SECURITY INVOKER` and reads the correction reason for the caller's own team. The two escalation readers (`public.matching_escalations`, `private.matching_escalations_for`) drop the decline `reason` and keep code, team and time; no screen calls them. pgTAP fixes the list of functions that read the reason columns.
- **Further organisation-wide path (RLS2-5):** `uncovered_visits()` is narrowed to coordination roles and the visit's team (its stated purpose was Operations, 0014:495). Coordination-role paths that would change a screen or need a product answer are findings F-1 to F-5 in READ_PATHS.md, proposed as RLS3 (decision Q13, `po`).
- **Catalogue check (RLS2-1):** pgTAP compares the tables with a `case_id`/`visit_id`/`client_id` column readable by `authenticated`, and the functions returning such a column, with fixed lists; no readable view; RLS on every readable table; a cross-team sweep calls every reader as a nurse of another team. Keyed on column names (the brief's open question): it needs no maintenance for unrelated tables, and a new linked table fails CI until it is listed.
- **Changed assertions (RLS2-6 vs RLS2-2):** `017` test 42 asserted that coordination roles read every decision row; it now asserts 0 rows (the rule RLS2-2 replaces). `012` tests 16 and 23 read `case_tasks` as Operations; they now read as the table owner (same facts), and 067 checks Operations' view through `case_funding_facts`. No assertion was removed; none checks less than before.
**Not chosen:** revoking `reason` from `authenticated` (would break `field_visits` for the team, or force it to `security definer`; the row scope already excludes everyone else); keeping coordination roles on `team_decisions` (no task uses the table API); scoping by the visit's current team only (the recording team would lose its own history after a team change; both are "the people who care for the client"); revoking `actor_id` now (F-4, belongs with the D-23 working-day proposal).
**Rollback:** re-create `org_members_read` on `visit_events`, `visit_time_corrections`, `case_tasks` and 0017 `scoped_read` on `team_decisions`; re-create `uncovered_visits` (0014) and the two escalation readers (0017 text); drop the two predicates. Widening access again needs the PO (D-3).
**Open:** F-1 to F-5 (RLS3, Q13, `po`); the cover read field list (D-3 b, `clinical`) and when a cover ends beyond the final report; the read log (D-3 gate i) and DSFA (gate ii); `lca` re-check of D-3 gate (iv) against this inventory.

### ADR-081 — DST1: one rule for Zurich local time → instant (repeated hour: earlier; missing hour: standard time); a pinned test clock
**Status:** PROPOSED · **Task:** DST1 (`docs/agents/briefs/DST1.md`, approved by the PO 2026-10-25 02:10 CET; `cra` CGR-52, advisory, open PR #341). Migration 0067, pgTAP `068_dst1_clock_change` (44), fixture fixes in 019, 023, 028, 029, 034, 039, 061, 062, 064, 065, 056 (written form), unit `field-time`, e2e `field.spec.ts` (F3), CI `db` job (pinned clock). Remediation plan `docs/agents/clinical/remediation/CGR-52.md`.
**Context:** on 2026-10-25 the `db` job went red on docs-only PRs, with a set of failing assertions that changed with the hour (CSR1 038, ABS1 046, SIG1 062, SIG2 064), and e2e F3 refused a correction as "in the future". Three earlier clock-dependent failures had been fixed one by one (AN2-C1, SIG2-14, DAY-1).
**Root cause (DST1-1), reproduced:** the pgTAP suite was run against the migrations of `recovery/main` and of this branch at fixed clocks (a PostgreSQL 16 server under a clock offset, migrations and seed applied, every file run; local, not CI; CI is the authoritative runner):

| Clock (UTC) | `recovery/main` | this branch, real-clock fixtures | this branch, pinned suite |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-10-25 00:35 (02:35 CEST, repeated hour, 1st pass) | **red**: 038 ×3 ("10 minutes after the planned start … P2", detail, "niemand zuständig"), 046 ×2 (CGR-15 released overdue visit), 062 ×5 (SIG1-9, SIG1-7, SIG1-5 ×2, SIG1-1 fallback) = the CGR-52 list | green | green |
| 2026-10-25 01:30 (02:30 CET, 2nd pass) | green | red: 038, 045, 062, 064 (one each) | see below |
| 2027-03-28 01:30 (03:30 CEST, after the gap) | **red**: 062 ×7, 064 ×21, 065 ×4 (wall-clock fixture arithmetic), 034 (calendar) | red: 034 only | see below |
| 2026-11-10 10:00 (ordinary day) | green | green | see below |

- **Production code (both, DST1-5):** every function turned a Zurich local date and time into an instant with `<timestamp> at time zone 'Europe/Zurich'`. PostgreSQL resolves an ambiguous local time to the **later** instant (CET): 2026-10-25 02:30 → 01:30 UTC. A visit planned in the repeated hour was raised overdue, escalated to P1 and stepped to Operations **one hour late**. The 00:35 failures are this: "10 minutes ago" is 02:25 local, read as 01:25 UTC, i.e. in the future. A non-existent spring time (02:30 on 2027-03-28) is read in standard time (01:30 UTC). The app's correction input `zurichLocalToIso` (F3) also resolved the repeated hour to the later instant, against its own comment.
- **Fixtures:** (a) several fixtures subtracted on the wall clock (`now() at time zone 'Europe/Zurich' - interval`), wrong whenever the interval crosses a transition (the 2027-03-28 failures and the 01:11 UTC failures of 062 test 15 and 064 test 69 on PR #331); (b) fixtures placed at "real now minus N minutes" cannot be expressed as a local time in the second pass of the repeated hour under any rule (02:20 local is either 00:20 or 01:20 UTC, and the planned visit means one of them): with the earlier rule they read one hour earlier (the 01:30 column); (c) 034 used the fixed date 2026-11-02 as a requested start, outside the accepted window from 2026-12-03 on (a calendar time bomb, found by the 2027 clock).
**Decision:**
- **Conversion rule (DST1-2, DST1-3), IMPLEMENTED:** `private.at_zurich(timestamp) → timestamptz`: an ambiguous local time → the **earlier** instant (CEST), so an alert is never late; a non-existent local time → read in **standard time (CET)**, i.e. what PostgreSQL already did, so its overdue signal is never later than the CET reading (it is the CET reading); every other local time unchanged. Implemented as "one hour earlier if that instant shows the same wall time", which holds for Zurich (transitions of one hour). `private.at_zurich(timestamptz) → timestamp` is the unchanged reverse. Refusing a planned time in the gap was **not chosen**: it would need a new refusal in every planning path (plan, move, cover, self-planning) and UI copy, for a case the rule already handles safely.
- **Applied everywhere, mechanically (as ADR-068 / 0055):** every function in `public`/`private` (extension functions excluded) is re-created from `pg_get_functiondef` with each `<operand> at time zone 'Europe/Zurich'` (also inside dynamic SQL strings) replaced by `private.at_zurich(<operand>)`; the overload keeps the direction, so instant → wall-clock conversions are unchanged. The operand parser takes an identifier chain with casts or a parenthesised group with its function name and **aborts the migration** on anything else. **One exception, from `cra` CGR-52 (4) (05:30 CET):** the end of a SIG2 watched-hours period (`private.watched_raise_at`) uses `private.at_zurich_end`, which takes the **later** instant for an ambiguous time, so a watched window is never shorter than its wall-clock length (`watched_inside` compares wall-clock times and was already right). Every other end (an absence or visit end in the repeated hour) also takes the earlier instant: one rule, a known effect on that one night (it ends one hour earlier). It is recorded for `cra` to confirm; no case was found where it delays an alert. Signatures, owners, grants, security and search_path unchanged.
- **Test clock (DST1-1, DST1-4), IMPLEMENTED:** `private.now()` returns `now()` unless the session setting `navora.test_now` is set **and** the private table `private.test_clock` holds its one row. Only `supabase/seed.sql` writes that row (local, CI); `supabase db push` never runs the seed, so on staging and production the setting is ignored and `private.now()` is `now()`. The table has RLS on and no grants to any API role; the function is `security definer` with `search_path = ''`. Every function body and the 49 column defaults that read `now()` now read `private.now()` (same value without the test row). A `language sql` `private.now()` is not inlined; with the setting unset it costs one `current_setting` call.
- **Pinned suite, IMPLEMENTED:** the pgTAP fixtures and `seed.sql` read `private.now()` (mechanical replacement of `now()`; 067 of RLS2, merged into this branch after 0066, gets the same pass, except 056 and 058, which test the written form and their own session-time-zone clock, and 068, which pins its own instants). The CI `db` job resets without the seed, pins the database clock to **10:00 of the Zurich day of the run** (`alter database … set navora.test_now`), then runs the seed (its first statement writes the test-clock row, so the seed rows are dated by the pinned clock too) and pgTAP, and removes the pin before the concurrency checks. pgTAP then gives the same result at any hour (midnight, repeated and missing hour). 058 pins `navora.test_now` to the real clock inside its transaction, because its own clock method (a session time zone chosen by the hour) needs the real hour. 068 proves the DST behaviour at fixed instants in both transitions. The e2e job keeps the real clock (no pin).
- **Fixtures (DST1-4):** wall-clock arithmetic replaced by explicit conversion (`(now() + x) at time zone 'Europe/Zurich'`), also in the two concurrency scripts; 034 relative dates; e2e F3 picks a number of minutes back that can be typed in the second pass (20 → 80, 15 → 75) with the same assertions. **No assertion of 038, 046 or 062 changed (DST1-5)**; 056 now checks the written form `private.at_zurich(private.now())` of the same Zurich-day rule.
- **Guards:** 0067 fails if any function body still holds a native Zurich conversion or a bare `now()`; pgTAP 068 fails when a later migration brings either back, or a `now()` column default. Later migrations write `private.now()` and `private.at_zurich(...)`.
**Rollback:** re-create the functions with `private.at_zurich(x)` → `x at time zone 'Europe/Zurich'` and `private.now()` → `now()` (same mechanical pass), reset the column defaults, drop `private.now`, `private.at_zurich`, `private.test_clock`; revert `time.ts`.
**Open:** UNKNOWN whether GitHub-hosted runners keep a changed system clock, so the four-clock proof (DST1-4) is the local run in the table above plus 068 in CI; a CI matrix job at the four clocks is PLANNED for this PR if the runner allows it (`sudo date`). Showing the repeated hour differently on `/field` or `/team` stays out of scope (brief). The thresholds stay PROVISIONAL (D-28). `cra` review of the production change requested (DST1-5).

**Addendum 2026-10-25 11:25 CET (DST1-C1 to DST1-C3, PO 10:12 CET `changes-requested`; `cra` CGR-53, CGR-56, open PR #358). Migration 0069, pgTAP `070_dst1_c1_c3_follow_up` (31), CI `db` step "Real clock before the seed (DST1-8 a)". Remediation plan `docs/agents/clinical/remediation/CGR-53.md`.**
- **Corrected (DST1-C1, CGR-53), IMPLEMENTED:** the sentence above "every other end … also takes the earlier instant" is withdrawn for intervals used in a safety overlap check. Rule now: **an interval used for an overlap test starts at `private.at_zurich` (earlier instant) and ends at `private.at_zurich_end` (later instant), so it is never shorter than its wall-clock length.** 0067 had turned the end of `private.visit_interval` to the earlier instant, so a visit 01:30-02:30 on 2026-10-25 occupied 23:30-00:30 UTC and an absence of its nurse from 01:00 UTC no longer overlapped it (ABS1 neither released nor flagged; before 0067 it did). The intervals and ends, checked one by one in every function body that converts a local time and compares ranges:

| Interval end | Used by | Now |
|---|---|---|
| `private.visit_interval` (visit with explicit end) | every absence check: `record_absence` (release, CSR1 past planned), `change_absence`, `private.release_visits_between`, `plan_visit`, `reschedule_visit`, `take_open_visit`, `private.visit_responsible_absent` (`responsible_absent` flag), `team_open_visits`, `private.member_working_span` (SIG2), `pilot_hypotheses` (AN2) | `at_zurich_end` |
| `private.visit_interval`, no end, planned minutes | same | unchanged: start + minutes is an elapsed duration |
| `private.visit_interval`, no start or no minutes | same | `at_zurich_end` of the next midnight (midnight is never ambiguous in Zurich; written for uniformity) |
| `record_absence` absence end (`e_at`) | the absence row and all checks above | `at_zurich_end` (start `s_at` stays `at_zurich`) |
| `change_absence` new end | same | `at_zurich_end` |
| `private.release_member_visits` (leave, by dates) | LEAVE/ABS1 by date | unchanged: ends at a midnight |
| `private.generate_case_plan` (slot window) | planning | unchanged: compares wall-clock times, not instants |
| CSR1 stuck reference (`private.derived_exceptions`, 0038/0058) | `visit_stuck` | **unchanged, earlier end** (an earlier reference raises the alert sooner; accepted by the PO, DST1 deviation (c)); asserted in 070 |
| AN2 `pilot_hypotheses`: planned end of a visit (stuck and overdue measures) | measures only, they mirror the CSR1 references | unchanged, earlier end: a measure must count what the alert did; not an overlap check |
| Cover: `cover_offers`, `my_covers`, `private.handover_rule` | COVER1 | unchanged: they build no instant interval from a local time (catalogue search: no function other than those above both converts a Zurich local time and compares ranges) |

- **Found while doing DST1-C1, fixed in the same function (IMPLEMENTED, a deviation in scope):** since 0048, a visit that starts in the spring gap and ends after it (2027-03-28 02:30-03:10) made `visit_interval` raise "range lower bound must be less than or equal to range upper bound" (start read as 01:30 UTC, end 01:10 UTC), so every absence command over that visit failed. Such an interval now covers both readings: from the end instant minus its wall-clock length to the start instant plus it (00:30-02:10 UTC for the example). Wherever the end instant is not before the start, the interval is `[start, at_zurich_end(end))` as above.
- **DST1-C2 (DST1-7), IMPLEMENTED:** writing the 2027-03-28 assertion showed that `private.watched_inside` compared wall-clock times while `watched_raise_at` reads the period end as an instant: a period 00:00-02:30 on 2027-03-28 was "outside" from 01:00 UTC although its end (DST1-3 reading) is 01:30 UTC, and the look-ahead then searched a week back. The statement above that `watched_inside` "was already right" is withdrawn. `watched_inside` is now true when **either** the wall clock **or** today's period instants (start `at_zurich`, end `at_zurich_end`) cover the time. On ordinary days both readings agree; on the two clock-change nights a window only gets longer. pgTAP 070: 02:30-06:00 on 2026-10-25 covers 00:45 UTC; 00:00-02:30 on 2027-03-28 covers 01:15 UTC and not 01:35 UTC; the look-ahead after it is 2 h before 01:30 UTC.
- **DST1-C3 (DST1-8), IMPLEMENTED:** (a) the CI `db` job pins the clock in its own step, then a step **before the seed** fails unless `navora.test_now` is set, `private.test_clock` is empty and `private.now()` equals `now()`; the seed is a separate step after it. (b) 070 asserts that `anon`, `authenticated` and `service_role` hold no table privilege of any kind (select, insert, update, delete, truncate, references, trigger) and no column privilege on `private.test_clock`, that no function they can execute names `test_clock` except `private.now()`, and that `private.now()` is a STABLE SQL function (it cannot write). A session can still `set navora.test_now` itself (any role can set a custom setting); without the row it has no effect, which (a) proves on an unseeded database.
- **Not in this addendum (own deadlines, PO 10:12):** DST1-C4 (CGR-54, the "Sommerzeit / Winterzeit" choice in the correction form; before 2027-10-31 or the first real client) and DST1-C5 (CGR-56, deploy check that the pin never reaches a shared database; before gate B). PLANNED.
- **Rollback:** re-create `visit_interval`, `record_absence`, `change_absence` with `at_zurich` for the ends and `watched_inside` with the wall-clock predicate only (0067 state); revert the CI step split.

### ADR-082 — SIGC: AN2 counts the take that stops the step; the banner and two lists say when Operations is in the app only
**Status:** PROPOSED · **Task:** SIGC (`docs/agents/briefs/SIGC.md`; conditions SIG1-C2, SIG1-C3 in `briefs/SIG1.md` and SIG2-C1 in `briefs/SIG2.md`, PO 2026-10-24 23:10; `cra` CGR-46, CGR-47, CGR-48, advisory). Migration 0068, pgTAP `069_sigc_signal_conditions` (59), count updates in 059 (43 → 44 measures) and 067 (one more reader in the catalogue and the sweep, 127 → 128), unit `signals-rules`, `analytics-hypotheses`, e2e `push.spec.ts` (390 px). Remediation plans `docs/agents/clinical/remediation/CGR-47.md`, `CGR-48.md`.
**Context:** 0063 (SIG1-C1) records every take in `signal_takes` with `stops_escalation`, but AN2 measure 10 and SIG2-10 kept reading `safety_signals.taken_at`, the first take (CGR-47). Outside the watched hours the step to Operations stays in the app (0064, SIG2-12), yet the banner said "Meldung an Operations folgt", and SIG1-8 is a manual check (CGR-48). Two take paths of `signal_take_stops` had no test (CGR-46).
**Decision:**
- **SIG1-C2, IMPLEMENTED:** one function, `private.signal_qualified_taken_at(signal)`: `no_show` and `visit_interrupted` → the earliest take with `stops_escalation`; every other kind → the first take (as built, D-28). `pilot_hypotheses` reads it for `signal_taken_share`, `signal_minutes_to_take` and `signal_taken_within_threshold_share` (string patch of the function's own definition, each pattern exactly once, marker `SIG1-C2 (ADR-082)`, like 0063). A take that does not stop the step counts as not taken. The first take of anyone stays as **`first_take_any_minutes`** (catalogue row, measure 10 group), whose note says it is not the qualified response; the three counted measures say which take they count. AN1 suppression is the existing final select. `safety_signals.taken_by` stays the first taker.
- **SIG1-C3, IMPLEMENTED (tests only, as expected):** pgTAP 069 (a)-(d) on the real commands; no code change was needed.
- **SIG2-C1 (1), IMPLEMENTED:** `my_signals` gains `watched_now` = `private.watched_inside(org, private.now())`, the SIG2-12 predicate at the reader's instant (no watched hours → false). The reader is polled, so the flag follows the clock. `takenText` shows "Operations nur in der App: ausserhalb der überwachten Zeit" instead of "Meldung an Operations folgt" unless `watched_now === true`: a row without the flag never promises the message (fail safe).
- **SIG2-C1 (2), IMPLEMENTED:** `public.visits_outside_watched_hours(p_own_teams)`: planned (`status = 'planned'`), not suspended visits of today and the next 6 Zurich days whose planned wall-clock interval is not wholly inside the union of the organisation's watched periods (`tsrange` `<@` `range_agg`, so adjacent periods count as one; the wall-clock reading is the one `watched_inside` uses). Interval: start-end; no end → one minute (as ABS1); end at or before start → across midnight; no start → the whole day. Scope: administrators their organisation on `/admin`; with `p_own_teams => true` (`/team`) and for every other member, her own teams. Columns: visit, organisation, team, date, start, end; no client, case or person. A shared component renders it on both pages; a load failure says so and is never shown as an empty list.
- **Reading chosen (protective), PLANNED for PO confirmation (Q14):** "whose interval lies outside every watched period" is read as "not wholly inside the watched hours", so a visit 19:30-20:30 with watched 07:00-20:00 is listed. The brief's four examples hold under both readings. Listing more never weakens SIG1-8.
- **Not built:** Operations does not get the list (the brief names `/admin` and `/team`); no planning warning (CGR-48 "optionally"); no named watcher (D-28, D-32).
**Rollback:** re-create `pilot_hypotheses` from 0067's state (drop the marker lines), `my_signals` from the 0063 body, drop `visits_outside_watched_hours`, `visit_wall_range`, `watched_wall_ranges`, `signal_qualified_taken_at`; revert `takenText` and the two pages.
**Open:** thresholds stay PROVISIONAL (D-28). SIG1-8 stays a go-live condition until the list is built and empty for the pilot plan (Clinical Owner). UNKNOWN until CI: the generated `database.types.ts` (edited by hand here; the types bot regenerates it).

### ADR-083 — RLS3: coordination roles read visit status notes, cover events and working-time facts only through read models; no `actor_id` through the table API; a two-level correction trail per visit
**Status:** PROPOSED · **Task:** RLS3 (`docs/agents/briefs/RLS3.md`, approved by the PO 2026-10-25; Q13 decided by `po` 08:10 CET; RLS3-4b added 10:12 and sharpened 12:12 CET from `cra` CGR-55 and `lca` 11:50). Migration 0070, pgTAP `071_rls3_task_scoped_reads` (81), `009` and `067` adapted (below), inventory `docs/engineering/READ_PATHS.md` (F-2, F-4, F-5 closed).
**Context:** After 0066 (ADR-080) three tables still let every coordination role read every team's rows through the table API: `visit_status_notes` inherited the `care_visits` policy (team + coordination, 0015), `cover_events` (0028) and `working_time_facts` (0022) named `private.is_coordinator`. Inside the team, `visit_events.actor_id` and `visit_time_corrections.actor_id` were selectable row by row (F-4, D-23 term b). The brief's first open question, answered from the catalogue: the only `SECURITY INVOKER` reader of these tables is `field_visits`, which joins the caller's own team memberships and reads no `actor_id`; every coordination read model (OPS1 `operations_exceptions`, SIG1, AN1/AN2 `pilot_kpis`/`pilot_hypotheses`, `billing_preview`, `ops_case_lifecycle`) is `SECURITY DEFINER`, so narrowing the table policies empties none of them.
**Decision:**
- **RLS3-1, IMPLEMENTED:** `team_scoped_read` replaces `scoped_read`: `visit_status_notes`: org member and `private.reads_visit(visit)` (team or active cover, 0066); `cover_events`: org member and (member of the visit's team, or `nurse_id` = caller); `working_time_facts`: org member and member of the row's team. No coordination role. The row's own `cover_events.team_id` (the covering nurse's team on `accepted`) is deliberately not used: it would widen the read to that whole team (the brief's generic "directly" wording; recorded as a deviation). **One read is added, as the brief requires:** the covering member reads the visit's status notes while her cover runs (before, no policy admitted her).
- **RLS3-3, IMPLEMENTED (tests only):** `care_plan_items` (team) and `case_lifecycle_events` (inherits `cases`: team + coordination) were already team-scoped for nurses of another team; 071 adds the negative and positive table-API cases. No policy needed narrowing. `case_lifecycle_events` stays readable by coordination roles (reason code only, no brief item narrows it).
- **RLS3-4, IMPLEMENTED:** the table-wide `select` grant on `visit_events` and `visit_time_corrections` is replaced by a column grant of every column except `actor_id` (the 0013/0015 pattern). RLS (team, cover) is unchanged. A member's identity together with an event or correction time comes only per visit from three listed readers: `field_reports` (J5 signing/countersigning: signer, countersigner, returner with the report snapshot), `report_returns` (F6R: returner with the returned snapshot) and the new `visit_correction_history` (RLS3-4b). 071 fixes the list from the catalogue (a new reader fails CI) and asserts none groups or counts per person.
- **RLS3-4b, IMPLEMENTED:** `public.visit_correction_history(visit)`, `SECURITY DEFINER`, one row per correction: event, recorded time, old value (previous correction or the recorded time), new value, time of the correction, `full_trail`, `reason`, `corrector_name`, `corrected_by_me`, and the report version the current (not superseded) billing fact is based on with its countersigner. **(a) full trail** for active members of the visit's team, the active covering member (`reads_visit`), and the report's author or countersigner while she is still a member of the visit's team or of its cover team (so a covering author keeps her report's trail after the report is final; a member removed from those teams reads nothing, as RLS2-7). **(b) billing view** for every other coordination role of the organisation (the readers of `billing_preview` through `teams_in_scope`): the same rows with `reason`, `corrector_name` and `corrected_by_me` null. Nobody else gets a row. No screen calls it yet (the brief changes no screen).
- **Changed assertions (RLS3-6):** `009` test 14 ("every event carries actor, command and idempotency key") read `actor_id` as the nurse; it now reads as the table owner, same assertion. `067` tests 65 and 70 (fixed lists of readers of `visit_time_corrections` with a reason, and of set-returning readers of visit events) gain `visit_correction_history`. No assertion removed or weakened.
