**Not chosen:** a `SECURITY INVOKER` correction reader (it would need `actor_id` granted back); returning the corrector's id instead of a name (an id is a join key for per-person lists, D-23 b); letting the report's author keep the full trail after she left the visit's teams (protective reading of RLS2-7; the PO can widen); a named "billing review" role (D-12, not decided: every reader outside the team gets (b)); narrowing `case_lifecycle_events` for coordination roles (not in the brief).
**Rollback:** re-create the three `scoped_read` policies from 0022, 0024, 0028; `grant select on public.visit_events, public.visit_time_corrections to authenticated`; drop `visit_correction_history`. Widening access again needs the PO (D-3).
**Open:** F-1 (DOOR1-11) and F-3 (D-4 or DSFA) stay open. `cra`: whether (b) serves CGR-55's billing purpose (PO 12:12). PO accepts the three listed identity readers and their tasks at review (RLS3-4). The `actor_id` of `visit_status_notes` is outside this brief. UNKNOWN until CI: `database.types.ts` (edited by hand; the types bot regenerates it).

### ADR-084 — FUP1: one visit length for the outside-watched-hours list, a banner flag that follows the delivery, `in_billed_version` per correction

**Date:** 2026-10-25 · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED in migration 0071 (PR open; CI decides) · **Brief:** `docs/agents/briefs/FUP1.md` · **Findings:** CGR-57, CGR-58, CGR-59 (`cra` 13:26, all advisory); remediation plans `docs/agents/clinical/remediation/CGR-57.md`, `CGR-58.md`, `CGR-59.md`.

**Context:** SIGC (0068) gave a planned visit without an end one minute in `visits_outside_watched_hours`, while ABS1's `private.visit_interval` gives it start + planned minutes A+B+C, else the rest of its day (CGR-57). `my_signals.watched_now` is judged at the reader's instant, not when the step to Operations is made (CGR-58). The RLS3-4b billing view did not say whether a correction is in the billed version (CGR-59, PO Q15 → RLS3-4c).

**Decision:**
- **FUP1-1, IMPLEMENTED:** brief question answered: `visit_interval` returns instants and the list compares wall-clock periods, so it cannot be used as is; the shared helper is **`private.visit_wall_range(care_visits)`**, which calls `visit_interval` for a visit without an end (or with end = start, as `visit_interval` reads it): wall start + the interval's elapsed length, and never earlier than the wall reading of its end instant. On an ordinary day both readings agree; on a clock-change night the longer one wins (the DST1 principle). The case split and the planned-minutes sum live only in `visit_interval`, so the two cannot drift. Visits with an explicit end keep the 0068 wall reading. The list reads watched periods up to two days after the visit date; anything beyond is "not wholly inside" (listed). The 3-argument helper is dropped.
- **FUP1-2, IMPLEMENTED:** a new reader flag, not a changed one: **`my_signals.operations_outside_app`** = `private.signal_step_outside_app(signal, private.now())`. Once the step is made (an Operations delivery with reason `operations_fallback` or `untaken` exists, or the signal is escalated) it follows the delivery: true only for an outside result `pending` (handed to the sender) or `sent`; `outside_working_time`, `not_active`, `failed`, no outside row or no Operations member → false. Before it, `watched_inside` at the instant the step is due (`after_threshold(created_at, signal_untaken)`, never earlier than the reading instant); no threshold → false. `watched_now` stays (069 assertions unchanged). `takenText` promises "Meldung an Operations folgt" only on `operations_outside_app === true` when the row carries the field; a row without it (a reader older than 0071) falls back to `watched_now`; null never promises.
- **FUP1-3, IMPLEMENTED:** `visit_correction_history` gains **`in_billed_version`**: `corrections.created_at ≤` the countersignature time of the version the current (not superseded) billing fact rests on, or its signature time when `countersign_required` is false; null without a fact. Same two levels (ADR-083); level (b) still without reason, corrector or `corrected_by_me`; no grouping or counting (071 catalogue checks re-run on the new body). #373 did not carry it.

**Not chosen:** re-defining `watched_now` (would change accepted SIGC assertions and the meaning of a column read elsewhere); returning the billed instant instead of a flag (a timestamp per correction next to the countersigner adds nothing billing needs; the brief allows either); a time margin before the end of a watched period for the not-yet-made step (a new operating rule, `po`).

**Rollback:** re-create `private.visit_wall_range(date, time, time)` and `visits_outside_watched_hours` from 0068, `my_signals` from 0068 and `visit_correction_history` from 0070; drop `private.visit_wall_range(care_visits)` and `private.signal_step_outside_app`. `takenText` then falls back to `watched_now` by itself.

**Open:** residual of FUP1-2 (UNKNOWN in practice): a step due within the minute before a watched period ends is classified by the scheduled evaluator up to one run later. What invoicing does with `in_billed_version = false`: D-18 and the invoice brief. SIG1-8 stays a go-live condition (Clinical Owner). UNKNOWN until CI: `database.types.ts` (edited by hand; the types bot regenerates it).

### ADR-085 — DOOR1 part (a): the door facts as columns on the no-show rows, a codes-only Operations line, the door notes through a visit-scoped reader

**Date:** 2026-10-25 · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED in migration 0072 (PR open; CI decides) · **Brief:** `docs/agents/briefs/DOOR1.md` (DOOR1-1, -5, -6, -7, -11; -8, -9, -10 for these parts) · **Findings:** CGR-51 (advisory, decided as DOOR1-11); remediation plan `docs/agents/clinical/remediation/CGR-51.md`.

**Context:** The access and language notes recorded at intake (0013, 0017) had no `/field` reader (H-03). A no-show stored only free text, so a phone attempt read like a door attempt (D-19 (2)); "Notfallkontakt informiert" carried no basis (`lca` 22:50). Operations read a 120/200-character excerpt of the note (F-1), which CGR-51 says may go only together with structured facts.

**Decision:**
- **Split (brief question 3):** this PR is part (a): DOOR1-1, -5, -6, -7 and **-11** (DOOR1-11 needs the facts of -5/-6 and must ship in the same PR as the excerpt removal). Part (b), DOOR1-2/-3/-4 (versioned category list, append-only values, display form with the code guard), follows in its own PR.
- **Storage (brief question 2): columns, not new rows.** `visit_status_notes.at_door boolean` (only on `no_show`) and `.disclosure_basis text` (only on a `no_show_outcome` with `emergency_contact_informed`; `recorded_consent`, `emergency`, `other`). The fact belongs to the one row it qualifies, the table is already append-only, audited and team-scoped (0024, 0070); a second table would need its own RLS and join for no gain. Check constraints bind each column to its row kind; a BEFORE INSERT guard requires them on new rows (rows recorded before 0072 keep null, read "nicht erfasst"). Both are added to the audit trigger's columns (codes, never the note).
- **Commands:** `record_no_show(visit, note, key, p_at_door)` and `record_no_show_outcome(visit, outcome, note, key, p_basis)`; the old signatures are dropped, so no caller can skip a fact. The new parameters default to null and are refused with 22023, so a client that omits them gets the validation error, not "function not found".
- **Operations line (DOOR1-11):** `private.no_show_ops_detail(visit)` composes it from codes; `derived_exceptions` uses it and replaces the interruption excerpt with "Notiz im Team". Removing the text at the source means no present or future Operations reader (OPS1, signals, AN1) can carry it. The team's own list (`team_visit_alerts`) restores the interruption excerpt through `private.team_alert_detail`, so the team text is byte-identical to before (045 unchanged).
- **Door notes (DOOR1-1):** `public.field_door_notes(date)` (SECURITY DEFINER) returns the two notes for visits the caller reads by `private.reads_visit` (RLS3: active team member, or the covering member while her cover runs, i.e. not after the final report). The brief names `private.cover_team_for`; that helper picks a cover team for an offer, while `reads_visit` is the RLS2/RLS3 rule for "covering member while the cover is active", so it is used here (nothing widened). Blank notes return null; the card shows "nicht erfasst".
- **Language notes (brief question 1):** kept as the existing note and shown (the PO's view; no second record, D-15).

**Not chosen:** a separate `no_show_facts` table (second RLS and audit surface for two codes); keeping the 3-argument command and adding an overload (the fact could be skipped); stripping the note in `operations_exceptions` only (signals and other readers of `derived_exceptions` would still carry it); showing the note to the covering member through `field_visit_status_notes` (out of scope; that reader stays team-only).

**Rollback:** re-create `record_no_show` and `record_no_show_outcome` from 0024, `field_visit_status_notes` from 0054, `private.handover_entries` and `team_visit_alerts` without the DOOR1 fragments and `private.derived_exceptions` with the excerpt (0047/0061 bodies); drop `field_door_notes`, `private.no_show_ops_detail`, `private.team_alert_detail`, the guard trigger and the two columns.

**Open:** (1) The P1 no-show exception closes with the outcome (C1, DOOR1-10 unchanged), so Operations' open row always reads "Ergebnis offen"; the outcome form is proven on `no_show_ops_detail`. Whether Operations keeps a closed no-show visible with its outcome: `po`. (2) The covering member has no `/field` card for a covered visit yet (COVER1 run-in-app waits for D-3); the reader serves her as soon as the card exists. (3) UNKNOWN until CI: `database.types.ts` (edited by hand; the types bot regenerates it), vitest, typecheck, lint, e2e (not run locally: npm install failed in this run).

### ADR-086 — DOOR1 part (b): a versioned, immutable category list; append-only values read only through a visit-scoped reader; a separator-aware code guard

**Date:** 2026-10-25 · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED in migration 0073 (PR open; CI decides) · **Brief:** `docs/agents/briefs/DOOR1.md` (DOOR1-2, -3, -4; -7 for the values; -8, -9, -10 for this part) · **Findings:** none for part (b) (Clinical Governance Report 2026-10-25 17:26 CET: "no `cra` gate unless the PR adds a category"; this PR adds only the organisational access arrangement).

**Context:** Part (a) (ADR-085, 0072) put the access and language notes on the card. D-17 (decided by po, product) asks for the option B form: ja / nein / unbekannt plus note, "unbekannt" first and distinct, who, when and source, append-only, display only, organisational part first, no clinical category before the Clinical Owner (D-17 part 3).

**Decision:**
- **Category list:** `public.safety_alert_categories` (code, version, label, class `organisational` | `clinical`, active from, source, help text, `refuses_codes`, `approval_ref`, `withdrawn`). Rows are immutable (trigger on update, delete, truncate, for every role); a change, including a withdrawal, is a new version. The active version of a code is the highest version that has started (Europe/Zurich date), unless it withdraws the category. A check constraint makes `approval_ref` mandatory for `clinical`; no API role has a write grant, so a category is added only by a reviewed migration. Ships with `access_arrangement` v1 (organisational, help text "Ort und wer den Schlüssel hat, nie den Code"), nothing clinical.
- **Values:** `public.case_safety_alert_values`, one row per recorded value, pinned to the category version it was recorded against. Append-only (trigger, every role), audited with case, category, value and source, **never the note**. "Latest wins" orders by an identity column (`seq`), not by `created_at`: two rows in one transaction, or under the pinned test clock, share `created_at`.
- **No table read grant:** `anon` and `authenticated` have no privilege on the values table. They are read only through `public.field_safety_alerts(date)` (SECURITY DEFINER, `private.reads_visit`: the team, or the covering member while her cover runs, the RLS3 rule used by DOOR1-1) and HAND1 (`private.handover_entries`, case's team only). This keeps DOOR1-9 provable by a catalogue check and avoids a new `actor_id` table read (RLS3 F-4).
- **Who records:** the case's team (`private.reads_case`) and, at intake, admin or operations of the case's organisation (source usually `referral`). The covering member reads but does not record (a cover is one visit, not the case, RLS2). Intake shows the record form only; it does not read the values back (DOOR1-9 names only the team and the covering member as readers).
- **Code guard (DOOR1-4, engineering proposal as the brief invites):** four or more digits in a row, **also with a single space, dot, comma, colon, slash or dash between them** ("4711", "47 11", "4-7-1-1"), refused with NV004 "Bitte keinen Code erfassen". Same pattern in `private.looks_like_code` (command and BEFORE INSERT trigger, so even the table owner cannot store one) and `src/features/door/rules.ts` (form, unit test). A plain run of four digits alone would let "47 11" through.
- **Form (DOOR1-4):** shared `SafetyAlertForm` on the `/field` card and at intake; "unbekannt" first and preselected; "unbekannt" shown with a dashed amber badge, "nein" with a neutral one; "nicht erfasst" when no value exists; who, when (Europe/Zurich) and source under the value.

**Not chosen:** RLS select policies on the values table (a second read path to keep in step with the reader, and `actor_id` row reads in the team); `created_at` ordering (ties); moving the language notes into the category table (brief question 1: kept as the existing note, D-15); applying the code guard to the free-text `cases.access_notes` (0013) in this PR (it changes intake validation of an existing field; proposed for `po` below).

**Rollback:** drop `public.field_safety_alerts`, `public.record_safety_alert_value`, `public.active_safety_alert_categories`, `private.active_alert_categories`, `private.guard_safety_alert_value`, `private.looks_like_code`, the two tables and `private.forbid_door1_change`; re-create `private.handover_entries` without the `safety_alert` block (0072 body).

**Open:** (1) PLANNED/`po`: the same code guard on `cases.access_notes` (intake free text, 0013), which today can still hold a code. (2) UNKNOWN until CI: `database.types.ts` (function entries edited by hand; the types bot regenerates tables and functions), vitest, typecheck, lint, e2e `door.spec.ts` (npm registry not reachable in this run). (3) Clinical categories: only with an approval reference from the Clinical Owner (D-17 part 3, G-1).

### ADR-087 — LEAVE1 part (a): an append-only access record, one restrictive policy per table, guarded read models and commands

**Date:** 2026-10-25 · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED in migration 0074 (PR open; CI decides) · **Brief:** `docs/agents/briefs/LEAVE1.md` (LEAVE1-1, -2, -3, -4, -7, -8, -10 in this part) · **Findings:** CGR-49, CGR-50 (LEAVE1-11, -12) are part (b).

**Context:** `remove_team_member` refuses while a member has open reports (0037, 0065 §8), and nothing else ends a person's access (`lca` 00:50). D-26: no service-role key on the app host, so the end must be enforced in the database.

**Decision:**
- **Record (brief question 1):** `public.member_access_events` (kind `ended` | `restored`, reason code, actor, time, identity `seq`), per organisation membership, append-only for every role (trigger on update, delete, truncate), audited by trigger and by the command (`audit_events`, `change_log`). Current state = newest event per (organisation, person). `private.access_ended(user)` is one index probe on `(user_id, organization_id, seq)`. Readable by the organisation's administrators and by the person herself (her notice); not by the team (the marker is part b), not by any analytics, billing, export or push function (catalogue assertion in pgTAP 074).
- **Reads (LEAVE1-3):** every `public` table that `authenticated` can read gets one RESTRICTIVE policy `leave1_access_open` (`not (select private.access_ended(auth.uid()))`, an init plan per query), so no permissive policy, present or future, returns her a row. The membership helpers `is_org_member`, `has_org_role` (and so `is_org_admin`), `is_team_member` and `cover_team_for` return false for her.
- **SECURITY DEFINER read models (brief question 2):** they bypass RLS, and two of them read memberships directly (`team_visit_alerts`, `active_safety_alert_categories`; found by the catalogue probe). Every set-returning SQL read model that runs as its owner and that `authenticated` may call (39) is rewritten mechanically as plpgsql with the same signature, result, volatility and body: `if access_ended then return; end if; return query <unchanged body>` (`#variable_conflict use_column` keeps the SQL rule that a column name wins). Row order is kept (`return query`). pgTAP 074 asserts that no such read model lacks the guard and that each returns zero rows to her.
- **Commands:** `private.begin_command` raises `access_ended` (42501). The eleven plpgsql commands that do not use it get `private.assert_access_open()` at their start; visit transitions go through `private.transition_visit` (begin_command); `my_signals` returns nothing. pgTAP 074 asserts that every volatile `public` function reaches one of these guards.
- **Signals and push (brief question 3):** her push subscriptions are deleted at access end (audited per subscription with reason `access_ended`); `push_active`, `signal_recipients` and `signal_operations_users` exclude her (an Operations user whose access ended does not count as Operations for the admin fallback). After a restore she switches push on again.
- **Commands `end_member_access` / `restore_member_access`:** organisation administrator only (the organisation is the one in which the caller administers the person; none → `rejected`, which covers another organisation and every other role), never the caller herself (`self`), idempotent, codes only. End refused while she is primary or deputy nurse of an open case (`has_cases`), has a visit on the way, started or paused as responsible, covering member or performer (`has_visit_in_progress`), or is responsible for or covers a planned visit from today on (`has_planned_visits`); never refused by her open reports. Nothing is deleted except push subscriptions; `auth.users` is never touched; restore gives back exactly what she had.
- `public.my_access_state()` gives her `{ended, ended_at}` (no reason code, names or counts) for the LEAVE1-6 screen in part (b).

**Deviations (recorded in the status row):** (1) **New reason code `has_planned_visits`**: the brief says "with the existing reason codes", but no existing code covers a planned visit (`remove_team_member` releases planned visits instead of refusing). (2) **Restore reason codes `employment_resumed`, `ended_in_error`** are an engineering default (the brief names none); `po` may rename. (3) **Per person, not per organisation, in effect:** an end in one organisation removes her access in every organisation she belongs to (privacy first; no person has two organisations in the pilot model). (4) **Last administrator:** the caller is always another administrator with access, so `last_admin` cannot be reached through this command; the guard stays as defence in depth and pgTAP asserts it in the source. (5) The 39 read models changed language (SQL → plpgsql); no signature changes; existing pgTAP suites pass locally. (6) pgTAP 067 and 071 now count permissive read policies only (the restrictive policy is on every table).

**Not chosen:** a column on `organization_memberships` (an update in place, no history); ending access through Supabase Auth (needs the service-role key on the app host, D-26); deleting memberships (would lose history, LEAVE1-4); patching each read model by hand (a new one could slip through).

**Rollback:** drop `end_member_access`, `restore_member_access`, `my_access_state`, every `leave1_access_open` policy, the guards in the commands and read models (re-create from their previous migrations), `assert_access_open`, `access_ended`, `leave1_org_for` and the table.

**Open:** (1) PLANNED part (b): LEAVE1-5 marker, LEAVE1-6 screen, LEAVE1-11, LEAVE1-12 (open-report counts in the command result and the list before the end), LEAVE1-9 gate line (which file holds the go-live list: `po`). (2) UNKNOWN until CI: `database.types.ts` (types bot), app, e2e. (3) D-30 (Clinical Owner) unchanged: who completes a leaver's report.

### ADR-088 — LEAVE1 part (b): a flagged return without a draft, one open-work helper, the marker as a state

**Date:** 2026-10-25 · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED in migration 0075 (PR open; CI decides) · **Brief:** `docs/agents/briefs/LEAVE1.md` (LEAVE1-5, -6, -11, -12 in this part; -9 waits for `po`) · **Findings:** CGR-49 (LEAVE1-11), CGR-50 (LEAVE1-12), both advisory; no remediation plan needed (the brief carries the remedies as criteria).

**Context:** after part (a) (ADR-087) a leaver reads nothing, but her open reports must stay visible to the team, a wrong report of hers must never become final, and the administrator should see her open work before ending the access. D-30 (who completes such a report) is the Clinical Owner's and stays open.

**Decision:**
- **LEAVE1-11:** `return_report` records the return whether or not the performer is available. `visit_report_returns.performer_unavailable` (new column, default false) is set when her access ended or she is no longer in the report's team; then **no draft opens**. The returned version stays `signed` (immutable, 0016): `countersign_report` refuses it (`returned_performer_unavailable`) and the report guard refuses signed → final for it (defence in depth), so it is never final, never billable (J5 reads final versions only), and `report_not_final` keeps raising. A version is returned once (0065 unique). The 0065 refusal `performer_not_in_team` on returns is gone (the brief replaces it); `field_reports.i_can_return` and `i_can_countersign` now agree with the commands in every case. One helper, `private.report_returned_unavailable(report)`, is the only new reader of the return table (no reason read).
- **Correction:** `correct_report` also refuses while the performer's access ended (`performer_unavailable`), for the reason 0044 refuses it while she is not in the team (CR-048 interim): only she signs a correction.
- **LEAVE1-12:** `private.open_reports_of(user)` is the one definition of her open work: `unsigned` (a completed visit she performed without a signed or final version: a first draft, or no version at all), `correction_draft`, `returned` (a returned draft, or a version returned while she was unavailable). It feeds `member_open_reports(user)` (kind, visit date, team; administrators and the team's RNs), `team_open_reports()` (a registered nurse's view of her teams: counts by kind and the access-end date), the `end_member_access` result (`open_reports` by kind; never a refusal, LEAVE1-2), the RN queue and HAND1.
- **LEAVE1-5 / LEAVE1-12 (3):** `field_countersign_queue` gains `kind` and `performer_access_ended` (a date): next to the reports awaiting countersignature and the returned ones, it lists the other open reports of a member whose access ended. HAND1 gains the entry `performer_access_ended` (open-report kind and date) for every team member who reads a visit of that client. `private.access_ended_at(user)` gives the time, never the reason code.
- **LEAVE1-6:** the protected layout reads `my_access_state()` and renders only "Ihr Zugang ist beendet. Bitte wenden Sie sich an Ihre Organisation." (and "Abmelden") when it is ended. The database already returns nothing (LEAVE1-3); the screen is a courtesy, not the control.
- **UI:** `/admin` shows per member the open-report block, "Zugang beenden" (codes only) with the open-report notice, the access state and "Zugang wiederherstellen"; `/field` shows the queue note and, for registered nurses, "Offene Berichte im Team".

**Deviations (recorded in the status row):** (1) The HAND1 marker is a **state**, not an event: it is shown on every visit of the client until the report is closed, not only "since you were last here" (a once-only entry would make the open report invisible after one visit). (2) A version returned while she was unavailable stays as it is after a restore (no automatic draft: that would choose D-30's route); a later return of another version opens the normal F6R draft. (3) `correct_report` gains `performer_unavailable` (not in the brief; same rule as 0044). (4) AN2 `report_returned_share` counts only returns that opened a version (F6R-7: no analytics function reads the return table). (5) e2e: the `/admin` list is exercised with an account without open reports (the seed has no report of a leaver); the list with entries is proven in pgTAP 075. (6) `team_open_reports` lists every team member with open reports, also while the access is open (the brief's "before the end"). (7) pgTAP 066 (two assertions on the old `performer_not_in_team` return refusal) and the reader catalogues of 066 and 074 are updated for the new helpers.

**Not chosen:** a new report status (`returned_unavailable`) on `visit_reports` (a signed version is immutable; the flag on the append-only return row says the same without touching the guard's state machine); opening the draft and assigning it to a team RN (would decide D-30); a once-only HAND1 event (see deviation 1).

**Rollback:** re-create `return_report`, `countersign_report`, `correct_report`, `guard_visit_report`, `field_reports`, `field_countersign_queue`, `handover_entries` and `end_member_access` from 0065/0074; drop `member_open_reports`, `team_open_reports`, `open_reports_of`, `open_report_counts`, `report_returned_unavailable`, `access_ended_at` and the column (only after no row has it true).

**Open:** (1) LEAVE1-9: answered by `po` 2026-10-25 22:10 (go-live gate index G-13, met by the PO). (2) UNKNOWN until CI: typecheck, vitest, lint, e2e (not runnable here: npm registry unreachable). (3) D-30 (Clinical Owner): who completes a report returned while its performer was unavailable.

### ADR-089 — LEAVE1-13: the access end releases her planned visits to the team by the one release rule

**Date:** 2026-10-25 · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED in migration 0076 (in PR #406; CI decides) · **Brief:** `docs/agents/briefs/LEAVE1.md` LEAVE1-13 (PO 2026-10-25 22:10 CET, product authority) · **Findings:** none new; the release rule and its exceptions are the ones `cra` reviewed for ADM1/ABS1 (CGR-09, CGR-10, CGR-34, CGR-35).

**Context:** part (a) (0074) refused `end_member_access` with `has_planned_visits` while she was responsible for or covered a planned visit from today on, so an administrator re-assigned a leaver's visits one by one. NAVORA already has one release rule for removal, absence and qualification change (`private.release_member_visits`, 0036/0046).

**Decision:**
- `end_member_access` (0076, full `create or replace`, same signature): after every refusal check, in the same command context, it releases her planned visits from today per team (teams taken from her visits in the administrator's organisation, so a visit of a team she already left is released too) through `private.release_member_visits(team, user, null, null, 'Zugang beendet', actor)`, as `remove_team_member` does: suspended included, in progress never, dated before today never. The released visits are uncovered, so the J3 flag, OPS1 `visit_uncovered` and SIG1 signals apply unchanged.
- The team-readable plan change row carries the fixed text "Zugang beendet" only; the reason code stays in `member_access_events`, `change_log`, `audit_events` (LEAVE1-8). Visit audit rows carry `end_member_access` and its key.
- The result gains `released` = `{team_id: n}` (`{}` when none); change log and audit row carry the same object. `/admin` shows "N geplante Einsätze sind jetzt offen im Team." after the end, and the form says so before.
- `has_planned_visits` stays only for a planned visit she **covers** (`covered_by`), while COVER1 is off. `has_cases`, `has_visit_in_progress` unchanged.

**Deviations:** (1) a restore (LEAVE1-7) does not give the released visits back; the team decides who takes them (same as after a removal). (2) pgTAP 074 test 32 now proves `has_planned_visits` with a covered visit (the responsible case is proven released in 076).

**Not chosen:** a separate release in the app before the command (two transactions; the access could end with visits still assigned); re-assigning to a named colleague (that is the team's decision, ADM1).

**Rollback:** re-create `end_member_access` from 0074 with the 0075 result patch.

**Tests:** pgTAP 076 (22): covered visit refuses and releases nothing; 3 visits in Nord (today, next week, suspended) and 1 in Mitte released, per-team counts in result and change log; past and colleague visits untouched; plan change rows with the fixed text, no reason code; uncovered exception for today's and tomorrow's, none for next week's; audited under the command; duplicate key releases nothing; restore leaves them released. Unit: `readAccessEndResult` sums `released`, notice texts.

### ADR-090 — AUD1: no table read of the audit log; one purpose reader per record; free text stays in the team; every read recorded

**Date:** 2026-10-26 · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED in migration 0077 (in PR; CI decides) · **Brief:** `docs/agents/briefs/AUD1.md` · **Findings:** `READ_PATHS.md` F-3; D-3 item 6 (b); D-4 provisional rule (po 2026-10-24 10:11, gate r3).

**Context:** `audit_events` was readable row by row by every administrator of the organisation through the placeholder policy `org_admins_read_audit` (0004, "Q4"). `changed` carries audited values, including free text such as `quality_holds.reason`. No screen reads the log; six SECURITY DEFINER functions derive facts from it.

**Decision:**
- **No standing table read (AUD1-2).** The policy is dropped; RLS stays on with no permissive policy, so `authenticated` reads 0 rows through the table API (the existing SELECT grant is kept so the answer is 0 rows, as the brief states); `anon` has no grant. The definer readers run as the table owner and are unchanged.
- **Value catalogue (AUD1-1).** `private.audit_value_classes (table_name, key, class)`: `code` or `free_text` for every key of `changed`, per audited table. Columns the trigger records whose type cannot carry text are classed `code` from the catalogue at migration time; text columns and the keys of the direct writers are listed by hand. Ten free-text keys: `quality_holds.reason`, `quality_holds.lift_reason`, `teams.coach_name`, `teams.name`, `teams.region`, `cases.region`, `matching_requests.region`, `partner_requests.region`, `member_profiles.display_name`, `member_invitations.email`. A key missing from the catalogue is withheld for everyone (fail closed).
- **Purpose reader (AUD1-3, AUD1-4).** `public.read_audit_record(p_entity_type, p_entity_id, p_purpose)`, SECURITY DEFINER: one record only; purpose `compliance` | `billing` | `safety_review` (D-4 provisional rule, no new code), anything else 22023; only rows of organisations where the caller is an administrator (another organisation or a non-administrator: 0 rows); access-ended callers refused (LEAVE1-3). A `free_text` value is returned only when the caller is an active member of the record's team (`private.audit_record_team`: holds, teams, cases, matching requests); otherwise the key stays with value null, `withheld_keys` names it and `text_withheld` is true. Time, actor, role and command stay visible. No grouping, counting or ranking.
- **Read record (AUD1-5).** `public.audit_read_log` (append-only by trigger, also for the owner; RLS on, no app grant): reader, time, organisation (null when nothing was returned), entity type and id, purpose, rows returned. One row per completed call; a refused call (bad purpose) rolls back and records nothing. `public.org_audit_read_log(p_organization_id)` returns an administrator her organisation's rows, newest first, no per-person aggregate.

**Deviations:** (1) Names, regions and the invitation email are classed free text although they are not health data: they are typed by people and have no closed set; the PO may reclass them (one catalogue row each). (2) Records without a team (member profiles, invitations, referrals) never show their free text through the reader. (3) A call that returns 0 rows is recorded with organisation null, so no administrator reads it through `org_audit_read_log`; it stays in the table as evidence. (4) Reading the read record needs no purpose code (administrator check only). (5) `quality_holds.reason` cannot change after the hold is set (guard trigger); its value enters `changed` when the hold is set. (6) pgTAP 076 now pins the test clock to 10:00 Zurich: unpinned, "tomorrow 09:00" was outside the 24 h threshold when CI ran between 00:00 and 09:00 Zurich (found while running the suite at 00:10; not AUD1 code).

**Not chosen:** revoking SELECT (the brief asks for 0 rows); masking inside `audit_events` itself (the definer readers need the values); a view (no purpose and no read record); a list or search reader (the brief allows one record per call).

**Rollback:** drop `read_audit_record`, `org_audit_read_log`, `private.audit_record_team`, `private.audit_value_classes`; keep `audit_read_log` (evidence); re-creating `org_admins_read_audit` would reopen F-3 and needs a PO decision.

**Tests:** pgTAP 077 (44): privileges, no permissive policy, catalogue completeness, fixed free-text list, no freely writable text column classed `code`, the fixed list of readers, argument and output catalogue of both readers (no aggregate), table reads 0 for admin, coach, nurse, operations, other organisation, anon refused; purpose refused when unknown or missing, record refused when missing or malformed; outside the team the hold reason is withheld with time, command, actor present and the text nowhere in the row; a code shown and a typed name withheld on a team record; other organisation, coach and nurse read 0 rows; a team member who is administrator reads the text; an unlisted key is withheld inside the team; each call recorded (count, purpose, 0 rows with null organisation), update and delete refused; administrators read their organisation's rows only, a coach none. pgTAP 005: former test 31 (line 121, positive check of the placeholder policy) replaced by two assertions (admin reads 0 through the table; reads the team record's two rows through the purpose reader); tests 71 and 78–98 unchanged (71 still holds; 78–98 read as the table owner, not through the table API).

### ADR-091 — LEAVE1-14: a claim lapses when the claimant's access ends; the claim inventory

**Date:** 2026-10-26 · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED in migration 0078 (in PR; CI decides) · **Brief:** `docs/agents/briefs/LEAVE1.md` LEAVE1-14 (PO 2026-10-26 02:14 CET, `changes-requested`) · **Finding:** `cra` 2026-10-26 01:26 CET CGR-62 (blocking, S1); remediation plan `docs/agents/clinical/remediation/CGR-62.md`.

**Context:** a safety-signal take by a person whose access then ended kept stopping the SIG1-5 and SIG2 steps to Operations, and the team read the signal as taken (CGR-62). LEAVE1 (ADR-087 to ADR-089) had removed her from receivers and commands, released her planned visits and refused while she held a case or a visit in progress, but had not asked, relation by relation, whether something she claimed still kept others from acting.

**Decision:**
- **One predicate, evaluated live.** `private.signal_taken_stops_step` counts only takes with `not private.access_ended(taker_id)`. SIG1-5 (`evaluate_signals`) and SIG2's immediate step (`outside_settle`) both read it, so neither needs a change; a restore makes her take count again because nothing is rewritten. Takes stay append-only; `safety_signals.taken_by` and `signal_qualified_taken_at` (SIGC/AN2) keep the historical take.
- **The team sees it as open.** `my_signals` gains `taker_access_ended` (a column, not a sentinel in `taken_by_name`, so the text stays in the app and no reason code can travel: "Übernahme beendet – Zugang beendet"). The shown take prefers a taker with open access; an ended take carries no name, time or qualification and sorts with the untaken signals. `take_signal`'s "already taken" answer orders takers the same way.
- **The administrator sees it.** `end_member_access` returns `taken_signals` (open signals she had taken; also in the change log and the audit row); `/admin` adds "n übernommene Sicherheitssignale sind wieder offen im Team".
- **Claim inventory** (every relation in which one person's row stops another person or a step from acting, and what the access end does):

| Relation | Effect at access end | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Safety-signal take (`signal_takes`) | **stops counting** (new) | 0078 predicate, `my_signals`, `take_signal` |
| Responsible member of a planned visit from today | **releases** to the team (LEAVE1-13) | 0076 |
| Responsible, covering member or performer of a visit on the way, started or paused | **refuses** `has_visit_in_progress` (LEAVE1-2) | 0074, 0076 |
| Covering member of a planned visit (COVER1, off) | **refuses** `has_planned_visits` | 0076 |
| Primary or deputy nurse of a case not closed | **refuses** `has_cases` (LEAVE1-2) | 0074 |
| Performer of an unsigned, correction-draft or returned report | not refused (LEAVE1-2); listed for the team, not billable, return `performer_unavailable` (LEAVE1-5, -11, -12); who completes it is D-30 | 0075 |
| New claims after the end: ABS1 return of released visits, PLAN2 responsible member (primary/deputy), `set_primary_nurse`, `plan_visit` responsible | **refuses or skips her** (new): `may_perform_visit` false; PLAN2 skips her (visit uncovered → existing exceptions); `not_in_team`, `responsible_not_in_team` (existing codes, no reason code) | 0078 |
| Cover offer (`cover_events.nurse_id`, COVER1 off) | none while COVER1 is off; the end path is a COVER1 switch-on item | — |
| OPS1 exception take (`exception_events` kind `take`) | **none — listed for the PO**: stops no step (P2 → P1 clock runs on, any coordinator can take over, CGR-05/-06), but Operations still reads her name | 0019, 0039 |
| Quality event `owner_id` (`in_review`) | **none — listed for the PO**: stops nobody (any team member or, if serious, coach/admin may close; OPS1 escalation continues, CGR-05) | 0020 |
| SIG2 outside delivery `sent` to her phone | **none — listed for the PO**: counts as "reached outside the app", so SIG2's immediate step waits; SIG1-5 still fires at the `signal_untaken` threshold | 0062 |
| Primary nurse of a closed case that is reopened | **none — listed for the PO**: her name stays until the team changes it (`set_primary_nurse` refuses her as the new one; PLAN2 skips her) | 0024, 0078 |

**Deviations:** (1) the inventory fixes four "new claim" paths the brief did not name (ABS1 return, PLAN2, `set_primary_nurse`, `plan_visit`): an automatic return or a pick of a leaver would create a claim nobody can act on, so they are treated "the same way as (1)" as the brief allows; the refusals reuse existing codes. (2) Four display-only relations (last four rows) are listed for the PO instead of fixed: none stops a step or a person, and fixing them touches OPS1 readers outside this item's scope.

**Not chosen:** deleting or rewriting her takes at the end (breaks append-only and the SIGC measures, and a restore could not bring them back); ending the signal (it must reach someone); carrying the text in `taken_by_name` (no type change, but a display string in a name column).

**Rollback:** re-create `signal_taken_stops_step`, `take_signal`, `my_signals`, `end_member_access`, `may_perform_visit`, `generate_case_plan`, `set_primary_nurse`, `plan_visit` from 0063/0071/0076/0046/0030/0021/0061; `my_signals` loses `taker_access_ended` (types regenerate).

**Tests:** pgTAP 078 (46), pinned to the DST1 test clock: step stopped before the end; after the end the predicate is false for her take and true for a colleague's; the colleague's reader shows it as ended and sorted with the untaken; restore before the step → her take counts again; ended again → SIG1-5 escalates once after the threshold since the signal's start; SIG2's immediate step fires; take, `taken_by` and the AN2 measure unchanged; a colleague can take it and "already taken" names her; `taken_signals` in result, change log and audit; the four new-claim paths refuse or skip her. Unit `signals-rules`, `admin-rules`.

### ADR-092 — WORK1: the working day proposed from her own visit events, confirmed and exported only by her

**Date:** 2026-10-26 · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED in migration 0079 (in PR; CI decides) · **Brief:** `docs/agents/briefs/WORK1.md` (PO 2026-10-26 00:20 CET, `approved`; builds D-23 C plus D) · **Gate:** G-19 before any real employee confirms a day (synthetic data only).

**Context:** the visits already carry depart, start, pause, resume and complete instants, yet the employer's working-time record is a second, hand-typed timesheet, and the North Star's denominator (paid working time) is not in NAVORA. D-23 (`po-decided`, `lca` compliant on terms (a)-(e)) allows a proposal only the nurse sees and completes, exported by her, and a team-level ratio.

**Decision:**
- **Proposal (WORK1-1).** `private.working_day_proposal(user, date)` reads only the person's own events (`visit_events.actor_id`, new index `visit_events_actor_idx`): each event of hers on that Zurich day opens a segment that ends at her next event of the same visit (departed → `travel`, started/resumed → `care`, paused → `visit_pause`); a last event without a successor gives an open end (null), never filled. Instants as stored (D-23 item 8a). `public.my_working_day(date)` (SECURITY DEFINER, LEAVE1-3 guard, no person argument) returns it to the caller only, and only after the WORK1-8 text is acknowledged. Output: `seq, visit_no` (ordinal), `kind, starts_at, ends_at`: no client, case, visit identifier or location.
- **Open question 1 (report segment): not derived.** A sign time has no reliable start (the draft is written during or after the visit, or signed at completion), so a derived segment would fill a gap. The proposal shows the F7 sign time as a point (`report_signed`, start = end) with the hint to enter later report time as "Administration".
- **Open question 2 (storage): one append-only table** `working_day_versions` (one row per confirmed version; entries as a validated JSON array; paid, care and break minutes computed once by the command from the instants). Plus `working_time_notice_acks` (WORK1-8). Both: RLS on, one permissive policy `user_id = auth.uid()`, the LEAVE1 restrictive policy, `select` only, no write grant, append-only triggers (also for the owner).
- **Never complete by default (WORK1-2).** `confirm_working_day(date, payload, key)` answers `questions_open` (with the list) unless `breaks` and `outside_work` are each `"none"` or a non-empty list and day start and end are given. An empty list is not an answer. Instants must carry an offset (no local time is guessed); the UI converts Zurich wall-clock input with the DST1 rule (repeated hour → earlier instant, spring gap → CET).
- **Versions (WORK1-3).** Version 1 without reason; every later call is a new version with `segment_missing | time_wrong | break_missing | other` and an optional note (≤ 200) that only she reads. Refused: a future day (`future`), access ended (`access_ended`, via `begin_command`); another person's day is not expressible (no person argument). Audited by trigger: work date, version, reason code (never the note, never a time of day). Idempotent.
- **Export (WORK1-4).** `export_working_month(month, key)` returns her latest version per day and the dates with her events but no confirmation; the CSV is built in the app (`buildCsv`, semicolon, local time with UTC offset), never stored. Audited as `working_day_exports` (month, number of days, number of unconfirmed days). A repeated key returns the data again without a second audit row.
- **Team North Star (WORK1-6).** `pilot_kpis` replaces its placeholder team row `direct_care_share` by `private.team_confirmed_care_share(month)`: care minutes ÷ paid minutes of the latest confirmed version per person and day, confirmed days only; value **and base** null with `too_few` below 3 people or 5 days (so a suppressed row does not reveal that one person confirmed). No organisation figure (the organisation row is a note), so a hidden team cannot be derived by difference.
- **Nobody else, no billing (WORK1-5, -7, -9).** The working-day tables and helpers are read only by her read models and commands and the team aggregate (catalogue in pgTAP 079); the two helpers that take a person or aggregate are not executable by app roles. A leaver reads and confirms nothing; her versions stay.

**Deviations (for the PO):** (1) **paid minutes = elapsed(day start → day end) − breaks**, not the sum of segments minus breaks: subtracting breaks presumes a span, and the record content `lca` read (ArGV 1 Art. 73) is start, end and breaks; time between visits counts as paid. If the PO wants the literal sum, it is one expression in 0079 and the ratio rises. (2) No report segment (open question 1). (3) F3 time corrections are not applied to the proposal (the brief names stored instants; she corrects in her confirmation). (4) The team of a day is her one team, else the one team of her events that day, else none (the day then counts in no team ratio). (5) Data checks that are not labour-law rules: the day starts on the confirmed date and lasts at most 24 hours, entries lie inside it, breaks do not overlap each other. (6) The audit rows (date, version, reason code; month and counts for exports) are reachable through AUD1 `read_audit_record` only with the record id, which no administrator can read; whether even that is acceptable is listed for `po`/`lca` (D-23 item 1). (7) Open question 3 (leaver's count in LEAVE1-12): not built, as the brief says.

**Not chosen:** segments as table rows (two tables in the catalogue for no reader); an employer view (out of scope); a break or rest warning (D-23 item 8).

**Rollback:** drop `my_working_day`, `my_working_day_versions`, `my_working_time_notice`, `acknowledge_working_time_notice`, `confirm_working_day`, `export_working_month`, the private helpers and both tables; re-create `pilot_kpis` from its definition before 0079 (placeholder rows). Types regenerate.

**Tests:** pgTAP 079 (68), pinned to the DST1 test clock: notice before the proposal; three visits give exactly their eight segments with the stored instants; zero rows for a colleague, her RN, a coach, Operations and an administrator; each unanswered question refused; confirm, duplicate, correct (both versions kept), note limit, future, offset-less instant; audit keys; a segment across the repeated hour is 60 minutes; export content, unconfirmed dates, audit and no identifiers; a fingerprint of every readable table and read model for five other people unchanged by her actions; policy and reader catalogues; team of 2 suppressed, 3 people with 6 days 54.2 %, latest version only; leaver. Unit `work-rules` (DST conversion, payload, CSV). e2e `work.spec` at 390 px (notice → both questions → confirm → correct → export). pgTAP 032 updated (organisation row note).

### ADR-093 — COND1-1/-2: the LEAVE1 conditions C2-C4 and the DOOR1 conditions 12-14

**Date:** 2026-10-26 · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED in migration 0080 (in PR; CI decides) · **Brief:** `docs/agents/briefs/COND1.md` (PO 2026-10-26, `approved`), specification in `LEAVE1.md` (*Conditions*, PO 06:10) and `DOOR1.md` (DOOR1-12, -13 PO 20:10/21:10; DOOR1-14 PO 22:10, CGR-61) · **Gate:** G-11, G-13 before the first real client (synthetic data only).

**Context:** the PO accepted LEAVE1 and DOOR1 with conditions that gate real data. COND1 puts them under one hand-off; COND1-1 and COND1-2 ride in one PR as `cra` 05:26 asked (CGR-64 with the DOOR1 conditions).

**Decision:**
- **LEAVE1-C2 (CGR-64).** A `pending` or `sent` outside delivery counts as "reached outside the app" only while `not private.access_ended(recipient_id)`, evaluated live (like LEAVE1-14). Both readers change: SIG2's immediate step (`private.outside_settle`) and the banner's step reading (`private.signal_step_outside_app`), so the forecast and the step read the same fact. No threshold, recipient list or step changes.
- **LEAVE1-C3 (CGR-63, option (a)).** "Recorded after her latest access end" is decided by the access-event **sequence**, not by time: `signal_takes.access_seq` holds the sequence number of the taker's latest `ended` event when she took (0 = none), set by a BEFORE INSERT trigger (`private.stamp_signal_take`, so every writer records it). `private.signal_take_lapsed(taker, access_seq, taken_at)` = her access is ended now, or an `ended` event with a higher sequence exists. Read by `signal_taken_stops_step` (both steps), `take_signal` ("already taken", the shown taker), `my_signals` (the `taker_access_ended` text) and `end_member_access` (`taken_signals`). She takes again after a restore: a new row (unique key now signal + taker + `access_seq`, nulls not distinct). *Why the sequence:* `signal_takes.taken_at` uses `now()` and the access events `private.now()`; under the DST1 test clock (and within one transaction) the two can tie or disagree, the sequence cannot. Rows from before 0080 (`access_seq` null) are judged by time (lapsed once an `ended` event exists at or after the take); only synthetic rows exist.
- **LEAVE1-C4 (deviation 15).** `operations_exceptions` and `team_visit_alerts` read an OPS1 take whose taker's access has ended as untaken (`taken` false, no `owner_name`, no `taken_at`) and add the flag `take_access_ended` (label "Übernahme beendet – Zugang beendet" in the existing flag list, no type change). `field_quality` gains `claim_ended` for an `in_review` event whose owner's access has ended; the card shows the same fixed text instead of "in Prüfung". No reader returns an owner name (unchanged). Nothing is rewritten; `handle_exception` (any coordinator can take over), the P2 → P1 clock and the QG1 closing rules are unchanged.
- **DOOR1-12.** One guard for both fields: the DOOR1-4 predicate `private.looks_like_code` (four or more digits, also with single separators) in a BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OF `access_notes` trigger on `cases` (`private.guard_case_access_notes`, 22023 "Bitte keinen Code erfassen"). A trigger covers every writer (today only `private.apply_case_facts`, reached by `convert_partner_request`, `link_returning_referral`, `update_case_intake`) and future ones. It checks only a written or changed note: no row is rewritten and an unrelated update of a case with an older note is not refused. The intake form shows the DOOR1-4 help text; the server action refuses it before the call and maps the database message. G-11: the read-only count is in `OWNER_RUNBOOK.md`.
- **DOOR1-13.** One "Zugang" line (`AccessRow`): the latest access-arrangement value (who, when, source); without one, the intake note "aus der Aufnahme" with the intake date; neither, "nicht erfasst". The intake date is `field_door_notes.intake_on`: the Zurich date of `cases.intake_consent_confirmed_at` (the conversion at intake), else of `created_at`. `field_safety_alert_history(date)` (new, same readers as the card: `private.reads_visit`, LEAVE1-3 guard) returns every value of the access arrangement of the day's cases, newest first, then the intake note as the earliest entry (`source` `referral`, `from_intake`); the card shows the history once a value exists. The intake note is never copied into the value table; `access_unknown` (0013) unchanged.
- **DOOR1-14 (CGR-61).** Check constraint `safety_alert_categories_clinical_register_ref`: a `clinical` row needs `approval_ref ~ '^CR-[0-9]{3}$'`. `node scripts/agents-check.mjs` reads every migration and fails when an insert of a `clinical` category names a register row that is not `APPROVED` (or cannot be read: fail closed). The card order is not changed (D-17 part 3).

**Deviations (for the PO):** (1) LEAVE1-C3 "after her latest access end" is implemented by event order (sequence), not by comparing times (reason above); same result in production. (2) LEAVE1-C4 for OPS1 uses the flag list for the fixed text instead of a new column (no type change). (3) DOOR1-13 intake date: the intake confirmation time, else the case's creation (the brief says "the case's intake date" without a column). (4) DOOR1-13 adds a history reader (the brief requires the history; none existed). (5) DOOR1-12 is a table trigger rather than a check in each command, so it also refuses a table-owner write (stricter than "every writer of the command layer").

**Not chosen:** comparing `taken_at` with the end time (mixed clocks); rewriting or deleting lapsed takes (append-only, SIGC measures); copying the intake note into `case_safety_alert_values` (forbidden by DOOR1-13); a CHECK constraint on `cases.access_notes` (would refuse unrelated updates of existing rows and fail the migration on an existing code).

**Rollback:** drop the triggers `stamp_signal_takes`, `cases_access_notes_guard`, the constraint `safety_alert_categories_clinical_register_ref`, the functions `private.signal_take_lapsed`, `private.latest_access_end_seq`, `private.stamp_signal_take`, `private.guard_case_access_notes`, `public.field_safety_alert_history`; re-create the 0078/0062/0071/0053/0074 bodies of the replaced functions and `field_quality`/`field_door_notes` without the new columns; restore `unique (signal_id, taker_id)` only after removing re-takes. Types regenerate.

**Tests:** pgTAP 080 (58): C2 (only sent delivery to the leaver → escalated once, Operations delivered; colleague's sent delivery holds; banner and step agree; an Operations delivery to an ended Operations user no longer counts), C4 (OPS1 untaken with flag for Operations and the team, no name or time, take-over works; quality event `claim_ended`, no name, closing unchanged), C3 stamp; DOOR1-12 (four digits and separators refused via the command and for the table owner, three accepted, older row unchanged and updatable, G-11 count), DOOR1-13 (intake only, intake plus team value, neither, no copy, Operations and leaver read nothing), DOOR1-14 ("D-17", "pending", "#396" refused, organisational unchanged). pgTAP 078: the restore block replaced by option (a). 065, 067, 072, 073, 074 catalogue lists updated. Unit: `cond1-display.test.ts`, `cond1-clinical-categories.test.ts`. e2e `door.spec.ts`: one "Zugang" line and its history.

### ADR-094 — COND1-3: WORK1-C1 (care never exceeds paid time) and WORK1-C4 (one time per visit event)

**Date:** 2026-10-26 · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED in migration 0081 (in PR; CI decides) · **Brief:** `docs/agents/briefs/COND1.md` (COND1-3), specification in `WORK1.md` (*PO review 07:15*, WORK1-C1; *PO review 10:15*, WORK1-C4, CGR-65) · **Gate:** before any team ratio is read as pilot evidence and before G-19 (synthetic data only).

**Context:** in 0079 `care_minutes` was the plain sum of the confirmed care segments, so two overlapping care segments counted twice and care inside a declared break counted as care while the break was subtracted from paid time: the North Star could exceed 100 %. And the proposal took `visit_events.occurred_at` with no F3 correction, while billing takes the latest correction (0023): one visit could carry two times (CGR-65).

**Decision:**
- **WORK1-C1.** `private.working_care_minutes(entries, day_start, day_end)` (immutable, not executable by app roles): the care entries clipped to the day, merged into islands (gaps and islands over start order), minus each island's overlap with the declared breaks. Breaks never overlap each other and every entry lies inside the day (0079 refuses both), so the result lies in [0, paid] exactly. `confirm_working_day` stores it as `care_minutes`; the segments themselves are stored as she confirmed them (overlap is never refused). Because each figure is rounded to 0.1 minute separately, the command stores `least(care, paid)` (absorbs at most a few tenths of rounding, never a real excess). Check constraint `working_day_versions_care_le_paid` (`care_minutes <= paid_minutes`), added `NOT VALID`: append-only rows written before 0081 are not rewritten (brief: no rewrite); every row from 0081 on is checked, also a table-owner insert.
- **WORK1-C4 (a).** `private.working_day_proposal` reads each of her events at its **effective** time, by the lateral join of 0023 (latest `visit_time_corrections` row by `created_at desc, id desc`, else `occurred_at`): segment start and end, segment order (`lead` over the effective time) and the Zurich date of the event. A correction that moves an event to the previous date moves its segment there. New result column `corrected` (true when the start or the end came from a corrected event); no correction reason, no corrector, no visit identifier. The result type changes, so `private.working_day_proposal` and `public.my_working_day` are dropped and created again with the same arguments and grants. `confirm_working_day`'s `from_proposal` mark now compares with the effective times.
- **WORK1-C4 (b).** `/work` keeps each visit segment's opening times (`origin`); when she changes a start or end, the segment shows the brief's text ("Die Zeit im Einsatz bleibt unverändert. Korrigieren Sie sie im Einsatz, damit Bericht und Abrechnung stimmen.") and a link "Zeit im Einsatz korrigieren" to `/field?datum=<Zurich date>&zeit=<original start instant>#einsatz-korrektur`. `/field` opens the time line (F3 correction) of the visit that has an event at that instant and anchors it. A corrected segment shows "im Einsatz korrigiert". Her confirmation is stored as she entered it; the payload carries no origin or mark.
- **WORK1-C4 (c).** Nothing in the working day writes `visit_events` or `visit_time_corrections` (no write-back; pgTAP fingerprint).

**Deviations (for the PO):** (1) **The link finds the visit by an event instant, not by a visit identifier.** WORK1-1 and pgTAP 079 fix that `my_working_day` returns no visit identifier, so `/work` passes the segment's original start instant and `/field` opens the visit whose time line has an event at that instant. When the segment comes from a confirmed version she had already edited (its start is no longer an event time), the link opens the day on `/field` without a pre-opened visit. Adding `visit_id` to her own read model would make the link exact; that changes a WORK1-1 assertion, so it is the PO's call (not built). (2) The hint text is the brief's wording verbatim ("Sie"), while `/work` otherwise addresses her as "du". (3) The constraint is `NOT VALID` (rows before 0081 unchecked, as the brief asks: no rewrite).

**Not chosen:** refusing overlapping care segments (the brief: stored as confirmed); a write-back of her change into `visit_events` or `visit_time_corrections` (`lca` 09:55: the less protective option; never built); clipping care by a plain `least(sum, paid)` without the union (would still count an overlap inside the limit); a validated constraint (would need a rewrite of append-only rows).

**Rollback:** drop the constraint `working_day_versions_care_le_paid` and `private.working_care_minutes`; drop and re-create `private.working_day_proposal` and `public.my_working_day` from 0079 (five result columns); re-create `confirm_working_day` from 0079. Types regenerate.

**Tests:** pgTAP 081 (21), pinned to the DST1 test clock: C1 (a) 09:00-10:00 and 09:30-10:30 count 90 minutes, both segments kept; (b) care 12:00-13:00 with a break 12:30-13:00 counts 30; (c) Testteam C, three people, five days whose care segments sum to 540 of 120 paid minutes each: each stores 120/120, team ratio from the stored figures and ≤ 100 %; no version with care above paid; the constraint exists and refuses a table-owner row. C4: before a correction corrected false; an event corrected twice gives the latest instant, corrected true; a departure moved to the previous Zurich date places its segment there and removes it from the original date; the result columns; confirming at the effective times marks the segments `from_proposal`; `visit_events` and `visit_time_corrections` fingerprint unchanged. pgTAP 079 column assertion now includes `corrected`. Unit (`work-rules`, `field-time`): hint and link when a visit segment changes, none when only the day start changes; link date and instant; payload without origin; focus instant parsing and the event match. e2e: not added (brief: unit or e2e).

### ADR-095 — COND1-4: FUP1-C1 (no promise in the last evaluator interval) and FUP1-C2 (no promise without a reachable Operations recipient)

**Date:** 2026-10-26 · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED in migration 0082 (in PR; CI decides) · **Brief:** `docs/agents/briefs/COND1.md` (COND1-4), specification in `FUP1.md` (*PO decision 2026-10-25 18:12 CET*: FUP1-C1 = Q16, FUP1-C2 = CGR-60) · **Gate:** before go-live (CGR-60, S3, advisory).

**Context:** before the step to Operations is made, `private.signal_step_outside_app` (0071, 0080) predicted only whether a watched period runs when the step falls due. Two cases made the banner promise "Meldung an Operations folgt" although the step would reach Operations in the app only: a step due in the last minute before a watched period ends is made by the next evaluator run, after the end (SIG2-12 then classifies it `outside_working_time`) (Q16); and the channel off, or no Operations recipient with an active subscription (CGR-60).

**Decision:** only the not-yet-made branch changes. It is true only when
- **FUP1-C1:** `watched_inside(org, due)` **and** `watched_inside(org, due + private.signal_evaluator_interval())`. The interval is one minute, named once in `private.signal_evaluator_interval()` (immutable), whose comment points to the pg_cron job `navora-sig1-signals` (`* * * * *`, 0060/0064). An operating fact, not a clinical value.
- **FUP1-C2:** `private.signal_operations_reachable(org, p_at)`: at the reading instant the organisation's channel is on (`organization_push_settings.enabled` and `enabled_since <= p_at`) **and** at least one member of `private.signal_operations_users(org)` is `private.push_active`. Both are the step's own functions (recipients 0074, active rule 0062 + LEAVE1-3), reused, not copied.
The made-step branch (follows the real delivery, LEAVE1-C2) is unchanged. The step itself, thresholds, watched hours and routing are untouched (D-28, D-32). `my_signals.operations_outside_app` keeps its name and type, so the client (`operationsFollows`) and the generated types do not change.

**Deviations (for the PO):** (1) **"Operations member" = the step's recipient set.** The brief says "at least one Operations member with an active subscription" and "the `push_active` rule used by the step itself, reused". The step sends to `signal_operations_users`, which falls back to the organisation's admins when no Operations member has open access (0074). Reusing it means an admin with an active subscription counts in that fallback case, exactly where the step would reach her. A stricter "role = operations only" reading would make the banner say "app only" while the step does reach the admin outside the app. (2) The channel check reads `enabled` and `enabled_since` only, like `outside_classify` (the step); it does not repeat `push_vendors_counted` (already required to switch the channel on, 0062).

**Not chosen:** checking the evaluator's liveness (`signal_evaluator_live`) in the forecast (not in the brief; OPEN whether the PO wants it); predicting the subscription state at the due instant (unknowable; the brief says the reading instant); a client-side change.

**Rollback:** re-create `private.signal_step_outside_app` from 0080; drop `private.signal_operations_reachable` and `private.signal_evaluator_interval`. No data, no type change.

**Tests:** pgTAP 082 (23), every instant explicit on a day seven days ahead: C2 inside the watched period, channel off → false; channel on, no Operations subscription → false; subscription stored but no send succeeded → false; one Operations member active → true; read before `enabled_since` → false; her subscription ended (unregister) → false; last send failed → false. C1 (channel on, active): watched until 20:00, due 19:59:30 → false; due 19:58 → true; due 19:59 (one interval later is the exclusive end) → false; watched until 21:00, due 19:59:30 → true; the interval is one minute. Made-step branch: a sent Operations delivery → true with the channel off and after the period. Privileges of the helpers. pgTAP 069: the FUP1-2 "watched until 21:00 → true" case now has a table-owner fixture (channel on since 2026-10-01, …904 active), removed after the two assertions; 080 unchanged (its fixture already has the channel on and an active Operations user). Unit: none (reader field unchanged, as the brief allows). Run here only against a local PG16 with stub tables (function logic, idempotency, privileges), not pgTAP; CI is the test runner.

### ADR-096 — COND1-5: WORK1-C2 (information text v2) and WORK1-C3 (a leaver's unexported days, for administrators only)

**Date:** 2026-10-26 · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED in migration 0083 (in PR; CI decides) · **Brief:** `docs/agents/briefs/COND1.md` (COND1-5), specification in `WORK1.md` (*PO 2026-10-26 08:15*, after `lca` 07:45, D-23 item 15) · **Gate:** before G-19 (synthetic data only).

**Context:** the v1 information text said nobody else, the administration included, sees her confirmations, although they are audited (ADR-092 deviation 6, accepted by naming the log in the text), and it named neither covering members, the correction trail nor the purpose and threshold of the team ratio (`lca` gaps b, c, d). A leaver's confirmed but unexported days were invisible to everybody after her access end, although the export is the only way they reach the employer's record (`lca` 07:45 point 2).

**Decision:**
- **WORK1-C2.** `private.working_time_notice_version()` returns `work1-notice-v2`; `NOTICE_VERSION` and `NOTICE_PARAGRAPHS` in `src/features/work/rules.ts` carry the v2 text (still a draft for the employer, G-19 item 2): status times also seen by members covering the team (Stellvertretung), correction reason and corrector seen by team members (b); "nobody else" replaced by: confirmations (date, version, reason code) and exports (month, number of days) are logged, never times or notes, and the log is read only for a stated audit purpose (c); why the team ratio exists (share of confirmed paid time spent on direct care, for team planning and the pilot) and that it shows only from three people **and** five confirmed days (d). Paragraph "NAVORA prüft keine Pausen-, Ruhe- oder Höchstarbeitszeiten." stays. `my_working_time_notice` and `my_working_day` already compare with the current version, so a v1 acknowledgement does not carry over without further change; `acknowledge_working_time_notice` now refuses v1 (`invalid`). v1 rows stay (append-only).
- **WORK1-C3 source.** New table `working_day_exports` (who, organisation, month, `exported_at`; no day, time, figure or content), one row appended by `export_working_month` per non-duplicate export. No privilege for any app role (her too), no permissive policy, the LEAVE1 restrictive policy, append-only and no-truncate guards. The export's audit row keeps its content; its entity id is now this row's id.
- **WORK1-C3 count.** `private.unexported_working_days(person, organisation)` (not executable by app roles): her confirmed days (latest version per day) with no export of their month whose `exported_at` is later than that version's `confirmed_at`. Both instants come from `private.now()` (one clock, the DST1 test clock in tests). One integer at the moment of the call; nothing stored.
  - (a) `public.member_unexported_working_days(person)`: the count for an administrator of an organisation of hers (`private.leave1_org_for`, as `end_member_access`); null for everybody else (her team's RNs, care assistants, coaches, Operations, another organisation's administrators). The RNs' LEAVE1-12 lists are unchanged. `end_member_access` returns `unexported_days` in its result (patched return line), never in its change-log or audit row.
  - (b) one number, no date, month, time or content; no stored or standing per-person figure.
  - (c) label "Bestätigte Tage, die sie noch nicht exportiert hat" in the `/admin` member row (LEAVE1-12 list) and in the end notice.
  - (d) `public.my_unexported_working_days()`: her own count on `/work` (export section) while her access is open, with a prompt to export; null after the end (LEAVE1-3). No export on her behalf.
  - G-19 item (4): the `/admin` end form shows, when the count is above 0, "Sie hat n bestätigte Tage noch nicht exportiert. Bitten Sie sie, diese vorher selbst zu exportieren; nach dem Ende kann sie es nicht mehr."

**Deviations (for the PO):** (1) **A new export record instead of reading the audit log.** The brief's definition needs "an export of its month was made after its latest confirmed version"; until now only the audit row held that. Reading the audit log for a product count would contradict the v2 text ("read only for a stated audit purpose") and widen the AUD1-1 reader catalogue (pgTAP 077), so the command now also stores who, month and when. That is a second copy of facts the audit row already holds; retention follows D-4 with the confirmed days. `lca` may want to confirm it (D-23 item 15 follow-up, advisory; synthetic data only). (2) **v2 also names the administrator's count** (one sentence in the log paragraph): the brief's four statements alone would leave the text silent on a count WORK1-C3 adds; `lca` term (d) of 07:45 asked for it to be named. The remaining gaps a, e (what happens at the end beyond the count) and f stay with the employer's final text (G-19 item 2). (3) The count is shown in every member row of `/admin` (including 0), not only for a person about to leave: the list is the LEAVE1-12 list, which is shown for every member. (4) The admin prompt uses "Sie" like the rest of `/admin`; `/work` keeps "du".

**Not chosen:** reading `audit_events` (see deviation 1); a column on `working_day_versions` marking export (append-only rows are never updated); a per-person figure stored at the access end (lca term b); showing the count to the team's RNs (lca term a); an export on her behalf (legal adviser, G-19 item 4).

**Rollback:** re-create `private.working_time_notice_version()` returning v1 and `NOTICE_VERSION`/`NOTICE_PARAGRAPHS` from 0079's text; re-create `export_working_month` from 0079; re-create `end_member_access` from 0078 with the 0080 patch; drop `public.member_unexported_working_days`, `public.my_unexported_working_days`, `private.unexported_working_days` and the table `working_day_exports` (synthetic data only). Types regenerate.

**Tests:** pgTAP 083 (26), pinned to the DST1 test clock: C2 version constant; with a v1-only acknowledgement `my_working_time_notice` reads v2 not acknowledged and `my_working_day` returns nothing although her visit gives a proposal; v1 refused; v2 acknowledged → the proposal (2 segments); both rows kept. C3 brief case: three confirmed days, September exported after two of them → the administrator sees 1; her team's RN, a care assistant and another organisation's administrator see nothing; she sees 1, a colleague 0; she exports October → 0; a repeated export key records no second row; a correction after the export → 1 again; export-record columns; no app privilege on the table or helper; exports stay audited keyed by their record; the RN lists carry no count; `end_member_access` returns `unexported_days` 1 and stores it in neither change log nor audit; after the end she sees none, the administrator still 1. pgTAP 079 and 081 acknowledge v2 now; the 079 reader catalogue gains `private.unexported_working_days`. Unit (`work-rules`, `admin-rules`): the four v2 statements, no "Niemand sonst", her count text, the label, the end prompt, the result reader and the end notice. e2e (`work.spec`, 390 px): the seeded v1 acknowledgement of assist.test shows v2 first; her count before and after the export; a second test confirms one more day and the administrator sees "Bestätigte Tage, die sie noch nicht exportiert hat: 1" in her `/admin` row and the end prompt. Run here only against a local PG16 with stub tables (migration twice, the count scenario, privileges, append-only guard, the end-result patch) and the pure TS helpers under node; not pgTAP, vitest or Playwright. CI is the test runner.

### ADR-097 — COND1-4a, -5a, -6, -9, -10: the fallback recipient test, the unexported count only at the access end, billing reads codes only, the access-note help text, the approval record for an APPROVED register row

**Date:** 2026-10-26 · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED in migration 0084 and the app (in PR; CI decides) · **Brief:** `docs/agents/briefs/COND1.md` (COND1-4a *PO 15:16*, COND1-5a *PO 16:20*, COND1-6 = AUD1-M1/-M2, COND1-9 = DOOR1-15 and COND1-10 = DOOR1-16 *PO 14:30*) · **Findings:** CGR-60 (4a), CGR-66 (9), CGR-68 (10), all advisory · **Gate:** before the first real client (synthetic data only).

**Context:** five small COND1 conditions, none needing a clinical value or a legal reading. COND1-8 (LEAVE1-C5, CGR-67) is the one remaining larger database change and rides in the next COND1 PR.

**Decision:**
- **COND1-4a.** pgTAP 082 gains the fallback case (082 had none: every earlier case had the Operations member …904 with open access): the channel on, …904's access ended through `end_member_access`, `signal_operations_users` = the administrator …905, false while she has no active subscription, **true** once her subscription is `push_active`, inside the watched period. No code change (ADR-095 deviation 1 as accepted).
- **COND1-5a.** The `/admin` member row no longer shows the count. `getAdminData` no longer loads `member_unexported_working_days` for every member (no standing per-person figure, not even in the page data); the new server action `memberUnexportedDays(userId)` reads it for one person when the administrator opens the access-end step (`mode === "end"`), which shows "Bestätigte Tage, die sie noch nicht exportiert hat: n" (`unexported-end-count`) and, above 0, the G-19 item (4) prompt. The `end_member_access` result and notice and her own count on `/work` are unchanged. The database reader stays as in 0083 (administrators of her organisation only); it is not narrowed to the end step, because the database has no notion of an "open end form" (display narrowing, as the PO decision).
- **COND1-6 = AUD1-M1.** 0084 re-creates `public.read_audit_record` from 0077 with one change: a `free_text` value is shown only when the purpose is not `billing` and the administrator is an active member of the record's team. With `billing` every free-text value is withheld (key present, value null, `withheld_keys`, `text_withheld` true), also inside the team. **AUD1-M2:** `READ_PATHS.md` says F-3 "closed" (0077 merged as 7f23a3b) instead of "closed in PR … on merge".
- **COND1-9 = DOOR1-15.** The guard is unchanged (`private.looks_like_code`, 0073; the `cases.access_notes` trigger, 0080): a phone number and a postal code stay refused. The help text becomes "Ort und wer den Schlüssel hat (ohne Telefonnummer), nie den Code". The category list is append-only (a change is a new version, 0073), so 0084 inserts **version 2 of `access_arrangement`**, active from 2026-10-26, same label, class and `refuses_codes`; values recorded under version 1 keep their category id and still show, since every reader matches values by code. The intake form reads the same text from `ACCESS_HELP_TEXT` (`src/features/door/rules.ts`).
- **COND1-10 = DOOR1-16 (interim part).** `scripts/agents-check.mjs` fails when a row of `clinical/CLINICAL_RULE_REGISTER.md` has status `APPROVED` and (a) the row does not name `approvals/CR-nnn.md`, (b) that file is missing, or (c) the file lacks a line `Approver role: …`, a line `G-1 reference: …` containing "G-1", or a line `Date: YYYY-MM-DD`. Today's register has no `APPROVED` row and passes. `CLINICAL_DIR` overrides the folder for tests. The required review by the named Clinical Owner (CODEOWNERS) stays with the owner after G-1.

**Deviations (for the PO):** (1) COND1-5a: the end step also shows the count as its own line "…: n" (including 0), not only inside the prompt (which appears only above 0), so the administrator sees the figure the brief names in the end step; (2) COND1-5a: the database reader of the count is not restricted to the end step (see above); (3) COND1-9: the help text arrives as category version 2 instead of an edit of version 1 (the list is append-only by design); pgTAP 073's first list assertion no longer pins the version (`access_arrangement:organisational`), its version-1 help-text assertion stays; (4) COND1-10: besides the file, the APPROVED row itself must name `approvals/CR-nnn.md` (the brief's "names no approval record", read strictly).

**Not chosen:** narrowing `member_unexported_working_days` to a pending end (no such state exists); updating category version 1 (immutable by trigger); a CODEOWNERS file (owner, after G-1).

**Rollback:** re-create `public.read_audit_record` from 0077; version 2 of `access_arrangement` cannot be deleted (append-only); a version 3 with the old text restores it; revert the app and script changes.

**Tests:** pgTAP 082 (29, +6: COND1-4a), 084 (16: AUD1-M1 billing withheld inside the team, compliance and safety_review unchanged, read record; DOOR1-15 phone and postal code refused 22023 at intake, the stated note accepted, version 2 active with the new text, version 1 kept, the record forms' reader, a version-1 value still on the card, the DOOR1-4 note refuses a phone number), 073 (one assertion adjusted); vitest `admin-rules` (count reader), `cond1-display` (DOOR1-15 pins), `cond1-clinical-categories` (DOOR1-16, incl. agents-check end to end); Playwright `work.spec` (COND1-5a at 390 px: no count in the row, the count 1 and the prompt after "Zugang beenden"), `door.spec` (new help text).

### ADR-098 — COND1-8 (LEAVE1-C5) and COND1-5b: the C3 rule for OPS1 takes and quality claims; information text v3

**Date:** 2026-10-26 · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED in migration 0085 and the app (in PR; CI decides) · **Brief:** `docs/agents/briefs/COND1.md` (COND1-8 = LEAVE1-C5, *PO decision 14:30*, CGR-67; COND1-5b *PO 17:15*, `lca` 16:42 on D-23 point (2))

**Context:** LEAVE1-C4 (0080) judged a leaver's OPS1 take and quality claim by her access *now*, so a restore brought her name back (CGR-67), unlike the signal take (LEAVE1-C3, option (a)). The PO chose one rule for every claim. Separately the PO asked for information text v3 before the first real day (rule P-11: the next version after v3 is the employer's final text).

**Decision:**
- **COND1-8.** `exception_events` and `quality_event_log` gain `access_seq` (bigint, nullable), set by one BEFORE INSERT trigger `private.stamp_access_seq()` to the actor's latest `ended` access-event sequence (`private.latest_access_end_seq`, 0080; 0 = none; null for an automatic close without actor). Both tables stay append-only. The OPS1 readers `operations_exceptions` and `team_visit_alerts` judge the latest take by `private.signal_take_lapsed(actor, access_seq, created_at)` (the C3 predicate, unchanged); `field_quality` judges an `in_review` event by `private.quality_claim_lapsed(event, owner)`, which applies the same predicate to the newest `in_review` transition of the event in `quality_event_log`.
