**Date:** 2026-10-28 · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED in PR (CI decides) · **Brief:** `docs/agents/briefs/COORD6.md` · **Issue:** #597

**Context:** README rule 9 and `DECISION_PACKAGES.md` *Frozen packages* (STR-14, STR-17 F1, F2) existed only as text and were broken repeatedly on their first day (`csa` STR-17). The brief asks for a format check in `agents-check`, run in CI before merge. No clinical, care, signal or priority rule is involved.

**Decision:**
1. New module `scripts/agents-coordination/freeze.mjs` (pure functions plus one git reader), wired into `scripts/agents-check.mjs`. It reuses COORD5's `readPackageIndex`, `packageStatusOf`, `AWAITING` from `parse.mjs`.
2. **COORD6-1** runs on the files as they are: every INDEX row at `awaiting-approval: <role>` needs `frozen since YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM` directly after the status link (an optional ` · ` between them, the form the PO uses).
3. **Base = merge base** of `AGENTS_BASE_REF` (default `origin/recovery/main`) and `HEAD`, not the base tip: a branch that is behind its base is compared with the commit it started from, so newer base changes are not taken for the branch's own. In CI the PR's merge commit has the base tip as its merge base, so both are the same there. Base texts are read with `git show <rev>:docs/agents/<file>` only; the working tree is the head side (uncommitted edits count locally).
4. **"Frozen" = `awaiting-approval` on the base** (COORD6-2 (c), (d)): a package escalated in the same diff is not frozen yet.
5. **COORD6-2:** a frozen package's file that differs from the base needs an *added* line matching `^Unfreeze: [1-5] \S` (added = multiset line difference, so a line already on the base does not unfreeze a new change).
6. **COORD6-3:** a frozen package's INDEX row or `OPEN_DECISIONS.md` *Open* row whose text differs from the base needs a **new** `Unfreeze: [1-5] ` reference in the row (more occurrences than the base row), or an added `Unfreeze:` line in the package file. A row that left *Open* (COORD6-6) is satisfied by the package line or by a new reference in its *Decided* row.
7. **COORD6-4:** journal entries are `## ` sections; an entry is *added* when its heading is not on the base (entries are never edited, README). An added entry containing "no new evidence" (any case) and a frozen ID as a whole token (`D-3` does not match `D-38`) is a warning before 2026-11-04 00:00 Europe/Zurich (`2026-11-03T23:00Z`, CET) and an error from then. `AGENTS_NOW` (ISO time) replaces the clock; an invalid value is an error.
8. **COORD6-5:** if the folder is not in a git work tree, or the ref or merge base does not resolve, checks 2 to 4 are skipped with one warning on stderr naming the reason; check 1 still runs.
9. **CI (brief's open question):** the `app` job checks out with `fetch-depth: 0` (repository about 20 MB) and sets `AGENTS_BASE_REF` to `origin/<base_ref>` on `pull_request`, `HEAD` on `push` (already merged; nothing to compare). The hourly watcher (`agents-coordination.yml`) is unchanged: it checks out `recovery/main`, so its diff is empty or skipped.

**Deviations from the brief:** (1) COORD6-3 requires a *new* reference, not only "contains": a row that once carried `Unfreeze:` would otherwise accept every later edit without a trigger. (2) COORD6-4 identifies an added entry by its `## ` heading. Both stricter or equal to the brief's tests; po may relax.

**Not done (out of scope):** judging whether a named trigger is true (the reviewer's job); scheduled-task prompts (F7, owner); the `csa` weekly measure (F6).

**Checked by me (not CI):** `node scripts/agents-check.mjs` on this branch: ok; replayed against the last 15 merges on `recovery/main` (each merge against its first parent): the 10 after #596 pass; #596 (which introduced the markers by editing every frozen row) and the merges before it would fail, as expected. The four open PRs' heads #611, #604 pass; #589, #582 fail on their stale INDEX only as a branch, not as a merge commit. The new unit tests were run by me with Node's test runner through a small `vitest` stand-in (npm registry unreachable from this sandbox), 22 of 22; vitest in CI is the authority.

**Tests:** vitest `tests/unit/coord6-freeze.test.ts`: COORD6-1 (with/without marker, marker not directly after the link), COORD6-2 (a)-(d) plus a base-only line and malformed lines, COORD6-3 per file and via the package line, COORD6-4 on both sides of the date and negatives (old entry, unfrozen ID, ID prefix), COORD6-5 (unresolvable ref, folder outside git, check 1 still runs), COORD6-6 (decided with and without the line; po-decided and inputs-requested by row references), and end to end through `agents-check.mjs` in a temporary git repository holding a copy of the real layer.

**Rollback:** revert the PR (no data or schema; only `docs/agents`, `scripts/`, the test and `ci.yml`).

### ADR-111 — VIS1: field actions happen on the visit's day (`cra` CGR-87)

**Date:** 2026-10-28 · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED in PR (CI decides) · **Brief:** `docs/agents/briefs/VIS1.md` · **PR:** #624 · **Issue:** #617 · **Remediation plan:** `docs/agents/clinical/remediation/CGR-87.md`

**Context:** `depart_visit`, `start_visit`, `complete_visit` (0007, `private.transition_visit`), `record_no_show` (0024, 0072) and `end_visit_not_completed` (0042) changed status by state only, and `/field` offered "Einsatz starten" on any `planned` visit. A visit dated next week could be worked today: its overdue/missed signal never fired on its real date (H-01), the record named a day on which no care was given (H-05), and billable facts follow `visit_date`. The rule is a visit/schedule fact (visit date vs. the Zurich calendar day, DAY-1); no clinical level is set.

**Decision:**
1. **Database (migration 0089):** `private.visit_day_refusal(command, visit)` (SECURITY DEFINER, team members of the visit only via `private.visit_for_team`) returns `not_today` for depart and start of a visit whose `visit_date` is after `private.at_zurich(private.now())::date`; `complete_visit` gets `started_before_date` when the visit is dated after today or its latest `started` event lies on a Zurich day before its `visit_date`. `record_no_show` and `end_visit_not_completed` check the date inline after their own visibility check. Order in every command: `begin_command` (duplicate, LEAVE1 access end) → visibility (else the command's own `rejected`) → the date rule → the state rule. Pause and resume are unchanged. The commands are patched from their own current definitions (marker `VIS1 / CGR-87`, idempotent), as in 0056/0074.
2. **Refusal audit:** `private.write_visit_refusal` writes one `audit_events` row (table `care_visits`, entity = the visit, the command, `changed = {outcome: {old: null, new: <code>}}`, actor and role). **The idempotency key is not stored on that row** (deviation of form, not of substance): `begin_command` treats a stored key as "already applied" and the app maps `duplicate` to success, so a retry of the same key after the visit was moved to today would otherwise be shown as done without a start. `outcome` is catalogued as `code` in `private.audit_value_classes` (AUD1-1); `write_visit_refusal` is added to the writers-only list of pgTAP 077 and `READ_PATHS.md`.
3. **App:** `fieldActionsOn(status, visitDate, today)` returns no action after today (VIS1-3); `VisitLifecycle` hides no-show and "not completed" there. On a future day with visits, one sentence under the day heading, `info` (`data-tone="info"`, stone-700 on stone-50; VIS1-3a), wording of the brief. A planned, not suspended future card shows only "Heute machen" (`action-outline`), which opens one confirmation (today's date fixed, the card's times prefilled and editable, an empty required reason, 3-300 characters as J3) and calls the existing `rescheduleVisit` → `reschedule_visit` (ABS1-C4, ABS1-C5, CGR-35, audit, `moved` row). On success she lands on `/field` (today); the move never starts the visit (VIS1-3b). `not_today` and `started_before_date` have German messages in `toCommandResult` and `LIFECYCLE_MESSAGES`.
4. **Who can move (brief question 2):** `reschedule_visit` (current definition after 0056) requires `private.is_org_member` and `private.is_team_member` of the visit's team, for any qualification. `/field` shows a nurse her teams' visits (`scoped_read`), so every nurse who sees a future card of her team can move it herself; a coordinator outside the team who opens `/field` gets the existing refusal "Nur Mitglieder des zuständigen Teams planen Einsätze." (it says who can). No new permission.
5. **Jobs (brief question 1):** no job or command in the database calls these commands (search of every migration: PLAN2 generation, COVER1, ABS1 release, LEAVE1 return only insert, release or move visits).
6. **PLAN2 after a move (`cra` C-2 (b), asked again in CGR-89; answer added 2026-10-28 22:15):** PLAN2 does **not** re-create a generated visit that the team moved off its planned date. `reschedule_visit` changes only `visit_date` and the times (0012 §2, patched 0014/0050/0052/0056), so the visit keeps `plan_slot_date`/`plan_slot_start`; the `moved` row makes it touched (`private.plan_visit_untouched`, 0025, `taken` added by 0046); `private.generate_case_plan` (0030, patched 0046/0061/0078) finds the slot's visit by `plan_slot_date`/`plan_slot_start`, keeps it (`kept`) and inserts nothing for that date. A slot removed by hand is remembered in `plan_slot_skips` and not re-created either. So after "Heute machen" the planned date has no visit for that slot and no overdue or missed signal can fire for it there, which is the fact behind CGR-89. VIS1 does not change this (IMPLEMENTED behaviour, pinned by pgTAP `089_vis1_c2b_plan_slot_after_move`, CI decides). Whether such a gap needs a replacement is a clinical question (CGR-89 (3), Clinical Owner, UNKNOWN); no re-creation or "replacement needed" logic is coded. CGR-89 (1) (the confirmation states whether another visit for the client is planned on the date the visit leaves) is **not built in this PR**: it waits for the PO to place it (OPEN).

**Tests (VIS1-2, VIS1-5, VIS1-6):** pgTAP 089 (41) at a pinned clock: tomorrow refused (start, depart, no-show, not completed, complete), status and events unchanged, one audit row each without a key; a coordinator outside the team gets `rejected` and writes no row; the moved visit starts with the same key as the refused start (`ok`, not `duplicate`) and completes; today and yesterday unchanged; a visit started yesterday and dated today cannot be completed but can be ended as not completed; Zurich midnight (23:50 refused, 00:05 ok); no interval or tolerance in the rule. Existing pgTAP that worked future visits now use visits dated today (pinned clock or same-day fixtures): 014, 023, 036 (moves first), 039, 044, 062, 065, 067, 069, 078, 080; 077 lists the new writer. vitest `vis1-rules`. Playwright `vis1.spec` (VIS1-4, VIS1-3a, VIS1-3b at 390 × 844); `field.spec` moves seed visit 508 (to 11:00) and 509 to today with "Heute machen" before working them; `lifecycle.spec`, `signals.spec` and the UX1-27 e2e plan the visits they start for today; `lifecycle.spec` C1 asserts "Heute machen" instead of "Losfahren" on its future visit; UX1-6/UX1-27 plan their own open visit ten days ahead instead of relying on 508.

**Deviations from the brief:** (1) no idempotency key on refusal rows (reason in 2). (2) The sentence is the brief's singular wording also on a day with several visits; a plural is a `uxa`/`cra` wording question. (3) `complete_visit` also refuses a started visit dated after today (it was necessarily started before its date), so a status written by maintenance without an event row is covered.

**Not done (out of scope, UNKNOWN):** any tolerance before the date (CGR-87 (4)); any rule for late documentation of a past visit (CGR-87 (3)); a prefilled move reason (VIS1-3b, until `cra` decides).

**Checked by me (not CI):** the migration chain 0001-0089 and the seed on a local PostgreSQL 16 with Supabase auth stubs; 0089 applies twice; every pgTAP file under a minimal pgTAP stand-in: 88 of 88 green (`fk_ok`, `col_default_is`, `trigger_is` are skipped by the stand-in), also with the database clock set to 22:30, 23:05, 23:30, 23:58 and 00:20 Zurich and the same result as `recovery/main` there. The pure rules were run by me in Node (assertions of `vis1-rules`). vitest, typecheck, lint, build and Playwright **not run** (npm registry unreachable from this sandbox); CI decides.

**Rollback:** revert the PR. The migration only re-creates three functions from their definitions with an added guard and adds two private functions and one catalogue row; no data changes.

### ADR-112 — VIS1 follow-up: the day rule on every write path, what a move leaves behind, the move at the door finished (VIS1-3c, -3d, -3e, -8, -9)

**Date:** 2026-10-28 · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED in PR (CI decides) · **Brief:** `docs/agents/briefs/VIS1.md` (po 2026-10-28 22:00 and 22:15) · **Remediation plans:** `docs/agents/clinical/remediation/CGR-89.md`, `CGR-90.md` · **Builds on:** ADR-111 (#624)

**Context:** the PO accepts VIS1 only with VIS1-3c (UXR-30: prefilled times never in the past), VIS1-3d (UXR-31: the release shown on `/field`), VIS1-3e (UXR-32, UXR-33: focus and a count-aware day line), VIS1-8 (`cra` CGR-89: the move states whether the client still has a visit on the date it leaves) and VIS1-9 (`cra` CGR-90: the day rule also for a direct `UPDATE` of `care_visits.status`), in one follow-up PR.

**Decision:**
1. **VIS1-9 (migration 0090):** the check sits in `private.enforce_visit_transition`, through a SECURITY DEFINER helper `private.visit_status_day_refusal(visit, date, old, new)`, in the trigger's `authenticated` branch after the state-pair and suspension checks and before the CGR-12 qualification check. A change out of `planned`/`on_the_way` into `on_the_way`, `started`, `no_show` or `interrupted` on a visit dated after today (Europe/Zurich) raises SQLSTATE 23514 with message `not_today`; a change into `completed` of a visit dated after today or started on a Zurich day before its date raises `started_before_date`. The statement fails, so no row, event or note changes. The date checked is the later of the old and new `visit_date`. **The column grant stays** (the brief's other option): `private.transition_visit` (0007) is security invoker and writes status through that grant, so withdrawing it would break depart, start, pause, resume and complete. The commands keep their 0089 check first, so through a command a refusal still returns its outcome and writes its one audit row; a refused direct `UPDATE` writes no audit row (the failing statement would roll it back).
2. **VIS1-8 (migration 0090, `/field`):** `public.visit_move_leaves_other_visit(visit)` (SECURITY DEFINER, stable, returns one boolean) answers for a team member of the visit (`private.visit_for_team`) whether another visit of the same case is on the visit's current date, whichever team holds it, counting every status except `cancelled` (removed visits are deleted, 0014); anyone else gets null. In NAVORA the client is the case (no cross-case identity, D-9). The answer carries no time, team, name or count, so another team's visit is counted without being shown and the sentence can never say "kein Einsatz" while one exists; no new read permission. `/field` asks once when "Heute machen" opens (server action `getMoveLeavesFact`) and shows "Am <Datum> ist danach kein/noch ein Einsatz bei dieser Person geplant." as one `info` line in the confirmation; no answer → no sentence. Nothing is blocked, no extra tap, no threshold, count or replacement rule. Not a set-returning reader and no `case_id`/`visit_id` result column, so it is outside the RLS2 catalogue in pgTAP 067; listed in `READ_PATHS.md`.
3. **VIS1-3c:** `prefillMoveTimes(nowMinutes, cardStart, cardEnd)` prefills the start with the next full 5 minutes after now (Zurich wall clock, `zurichMinutesNow`) and the end with start + the card's planned duration; if the start or end would be after 23:59, both stay empty and required. A card without a valid duration gets the start only. Computed when the form opens. No change to `visit_overdue`, its threshold, the signal job, `reschedule_visit` or any permission. The optional "start at or before now" `info` line is **not built**: its wording needs `cra` confirmation in VIS1-7 first, as the brief says.
4. **VIS1-3d and VIS1-3e (1):** after a successful move `/field` opens today's view with `?verschoben=<visit>` (`&offen=1` when `reschedule_visit` returned `released: true`, `&hinweise=<flags>` for its planning flags). `parseMovedNotice` accepts only a UUID and known `PLAN_FLAG_LABEL` keys; the page shows, above the moved card (or above the list when the card is not on that day), one `role="status"` line "Einsatz auf heute verschoben", the /team release text (now one constant `RELEASED_ON_MOVE`, used by both pages) as `role="status"` with tone `attention`, and the flags as on /team; focus moves to the moved card. A crafted link can only show a notice; it changes nothing. Opening "Heute machen" focuses "Von"; "Abbrechen" returns focus to "Heute machen".
5. **VIS1-3e (2):** `futureDayNote(day, count)`: one visit keeps the VIS1-3 wording; several get the PO's plural wording. ADR-111 deviation 2 is thereby closed.

**Tests:** pgTAP `090_vis1_follow_up_day_rule_every_path_and_move_fact` (34) at a pinned clock (14:02 Zurich): direct start, depart and complete of tomorrow's visits refused with the outcome, status and events unchanged; pause unchanged; today and yesterday as before; the command still returns `not_today` with its audit row; the move fact for none / own team / another team's visit (which she cannot read) / cancelled / outside the team; the move itself unchanged; VIS1-3c: a visit moved with the prefilled 14:05 raises no `visit_overdue` on `/team` (`team_visit_alerts`) and no signal (`evaluate_signals`), while the control moved with the card's earlier time raises both. vitest `vis1-follow-up` (prefill 14:02 → 14:05, 14:05 → 14:10, 23:57 → empty, midnight edges, Zurich clock in CET and CEST; count-aware line for 1 and 3; sentence choice; notice link round trip). Playwright `vis1.spec` at 390 × 844: prefill against the pinned browser clock, focus on open, cancel and success, the "kein Einsatz" and "noch ein Einsatz" sentences, the plural day line, the release notice.

**Deviations from the brief:** (1) VIS1-3c e2e pins the browser clock at the real now instead of a fixed hour: the server and database clocks are not pinned in e2e, and a fixed earlier hour would make the moved visit overdue on the real clock; the overdue evaluation at a pinned now is proven in pgTAP 090 (the brief allows pgTAP or e2e). (2) The VIS1-8 e2e uses day +45 ("kein Einsatz") and day +10 ("noch ein Einsatz") instead of tomorrow, because seed visit 508 (same client) is tomorrow and other specs plan visits for that client on near days (doctor.spec: +9). (3) The VIS1-3d e2e relies on the absence of assist.test recorded by `field.spec` "ABS1-C6" (that file runs earlier: one worker, files in name order). (4) A card without a valid planned duration prefills the start only. (5) AUT-17 (placeholder *why today*) not added (allowed, not required).

**Not done (UNKNOWN, not coded):** whether a moved visit should be exempt from the overdue signal (rule question, P-27); whether a day left without a visit needs a replacement (CGR-89 (3), Clinical Owner); any tolerance before the date (CGR-87 (4)).

**Checked by me (not CI):** the migration chain 0001-0090 and the seed on a local PostgreSQL 16 with Supabase auth stubs; every pgTAP file under a minimal pgTAP stand-in: 88 of 90 files green at about 23:30 Zurich, the two others (`007`, `053`) only need `col_default_is` / `trigger_is`, which the stand-in lacks; 089 (41), 089_vis1_c2b (8) and 090 (34) green. The pure rules were run by me in Node (type stripping). vitest, typecheck, lint, build and Playwright **not run** (npm registry unreachable from this sandbox); CI decides.

**Rollback:** revert the PR. 0090 re-creates the trigger function with one added check and adds two functions; no data changes.

**Addendum 2026-10-29 (VIS1-3f, VIS1-3g; po 00:15 from `uxa` UXR-34, UXR-35):**
6. **VIS1-3f:** the release notice carries a fixed id (`MOVED_RELEASED_NOTICE_ID`); the moved card (the element VIS1-3e focuses) gets `aria-describedby` pointing at it only when the move returned `released: true`. A move without `released` sets no description. Text, position and tone of the notice are unchanged (VIS1-7 gate `cra` 01:30).
7. **VIS1-3g:** the notice belongs to the move. Today's view still opens with the move's parameters (decision 4), but once it has rendered, `FieldClient` removes `verschoben`, `offen` and `hinweise` from the address with `history.replaceState` (`withoutMovedNotice`; Next.js keeps its router in step with the native history API). A reload, the refresh after the next action (e.g. "Einsatz starten") and a copied link therefore no longer show it; Back returns to the future day as before. The `router.refresh()` after the move's `router.push` is removed: today's dynamic view is loaded fresh by the push, and a queued refresh would run on the stripped address and drop the notice before it is seen. Nothing is kept in storage. The `moved` row and `/team` stay the lasting record.
   - *Rejected:* client state (context or module variable) consumed once: it outlives a refresh, so "after Einsatz starten" would need a second rule on the visit's status; the address is the only place the notice lived.
   - **Tests (CI decides; not run by me, no npm registry here):** vitest `vis1-follow-up` (`withoutMovedNotice`); Playwright `vis1.spec`: focused moved card described by "Der Einsatz ist jetzt offen im Team." after a released move, no description without it, address without the parameters, notice gone after reload; `field.spec`: notice gone after "Einsatz starten" on the moved visit. The four e2e waits on `?verschoben=` now wait for the notice instead.

### ADR-113 — e2e suite on one Zurich day: run-day guard and calendar-day arithmetic (issue #635)

**Date:** 2026-10-29 · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED in the PR for #635 (CI decides) · **Owner:** `eng`

**Context:** the PO (#635, 2026-10-29 00:15) reported e2e failures at night: PR #632 (docs only) red twice around 23:25 Zurich in `work.spec.ts:99`; PR #633 red with 13 failures in a run from 23:56 to 00:06. The e2e job keeps the real clock (ADR-081): `supabase start` seeds rows dated by the Zurich day at that moment, while the app (`todayInZurich()`) and the specs (`zurichDay()`) read the Zurich day whenever they run.

**Two causes, reproduced by me (Node, pinned `Date.now`):**
1. **Crossing midnight (13 failures on #633):** a run seeded before 00:00 and tested after it looks for "today's" visits on a day the seed did not fill. No spec-side change can fix this alone: the app itself moves to the new day.
2. **24-hour steps across the DST change (`work.spec.ts:99`, 23:25):** `work.spec` computed "N days back" as `now − N × 24 h`. At 23:25 CET on 28.10.2026, `−4 × 24 h` lands at 00:25 CEST on 25.10, the same Zurich day as `−3`. The first test had already confirmed that day, so the second could not confirm it and timed out. It happens only in the last hour before midnight during the four days after the autumn change (and the mirror case in spring).

**Decision:**
- `work.spec.ts` uses calendar-day arithmetic on the Zurich date, as `field.spec.ts` already does (ADR-068). No assertion changed.
- `scripts/ci-e2e-day-guard.sh wait` runs in the e2e job **before the seed**: if fewer than `E2E_DAY_MARGIN_MIN` (default 20) minutes are left until midnight Europe/Zurich, it waits until 30 s after midnight. A green e2e job runs about 4 minutes from seed to end (run 40782620429: seed 23:12:03 UTC, Playwright done 23:16:40 UTC), so 20 minutes is five times that. Job timeout 25 → 50 minutes (at most 20.5 minutes of waiting).
- `scripts/ci-e2e-day-guard.sh check` runs after Playwright (`if: always()`) and annotates a run whose Zurich day changed anyway (diagnosis only; it never fails the job).

**Rejected:** pinning the database clock in e2e as the `db` job does (ADR-081): the Next.js server and the browser read the real clock, so database and app would disagree all day instead of only at midnight. A fake clock inside the app is a production code path for tests; not worth it. Re-running at night: forbidden as a root cause.

**Not changed (OPEN, other specs):** `intake`, `planning`, `lifecycle`, `journey` build dates with `toISOString()` (UTC) plus whole days; between 00:00 and 01:00/02:00 Zurich these are one day earlier than meant. All are future dates (+1 to +90 days) entered into forms, so none is known to fail; left as they are to keep this change small.

**App check (PO ask 3), finding for `po`, not a test fix:** the database commands read the day when they run (`private.now()`), so no visit dated after the real today can be worked (VIS1 holds). But `/field` computes "today" once when the page renders. A page opened before midnight on a future day and used after midnight sends "Heute machen" with the **old** date, which is now yesterday: `reschedule_visit` accepts a past date (a documented "Nachtrag", ADR-063/ABS1-C5), so the visit is moved to yesterday while the form said "Heute". The form shows the date, but the outcome is a visit on a past day that no longer appears on today's view. Likewise, field actions on a card dated "today" at render are, after midnight, actions on a past-dated visit (allowed today; late documentation is CGR-87 (3), UNKNOWN). Reported to the PO as an issue; not built here.

**Tests:** `tests/unit/ci-e2e-day-guard.test.ts` (vitest; clock replaced through `E2E_GUARD_NOW`, no sleeping): wait at 23:50 CET and 23:45 CEST, no wait at 23:40, 00:00:30 and 14:00, configurable margin, `check` flags a changed day and stays quiet otherwise. **Run by me:** the same cases through bash on this container; vitest, lint, typecheck and Playwright **not run** by me (no npm registry here); CI decides.

**Rollback:** revert the PR.

### ADR-114 — ADM2 part (a): the team admits its colleagues; "Vertrag erfasst" is the employer's only step

**Date:** 2026-10-29 · **Status:** part (a) IMPLEMENTED in the ADM2 PR (CI decides) · part (b): ADR-115 · **Owner:** `eng`

**Context:** brief `docs/agents/briefs/ADM2.md` (po 2026-10-27 03:10, amended 04:10 and 06:10), D-20 po-decided with the `lca` 00:50 protective shape. Part (a) is ADM2-1 to ADM2-10; part (b) (ADM2-11 to ADM2-15, contract end ends access on time; `cra` CGR-72) follows in the same PR, so the PR stays a draft until then.

**Decision (migration 0091):**
1. **Switch (ADM2-1):** `team_admission_settings` (one row per organisation; no row = off), written only by `set_team_admission` (administrator, audited, idempotent). Off: every ADM2 command returns `admission_off`. ADM1 commands are not touched (pgTAP: `add_team_member` in both states).
2. **Three records (ADM2-3):** `team_admission_proposals` (head: team, proposer, time) and `team_admission_events` (consent / objection with reason code / objection withdrawn / outcome, who and when) are the append-only decision record; neither has a text column (kind and reason are enum types `team_admission_event_kind`, `team_admission_objection`). `team_candidates` holds the only candidate data (display name, claimed qualification, Pensum band `20-40 | 40-60 | 60-80 | 80-100`); it is deleted in the same transaction as the outcome (ADM2-8, 0 days, D-4 addendum). Its audit rows carry the proposal reference only, never the name. `team_decisions` (J2) is not used.
3. **Consent rule (ADM2-5), organisational product rule (D-20, not clinical):** constants `private.adm2_consents()` = **2** and `private.adm2_lapse_days()` = **14**. Admitted when at least `least(2, other active members)` members other than the proposer consent and no objection stands. A member's stance is her newest consent / objection / objection-withdrawn event; members who joined after the proposal take part; members whose access ended do not count. An open proposal lapses at the same Zurich local time 14 calendar days later (`private.at_zurich` both ways, DST-safe). **Engineering question 2:** one event row per member act (the J2 idempotency pattern through `begin_command`), the tally reads the newest per member; no unique row per member is needed.
4. **Lapse evaluation:** no hosted job exists (D-2), so every ADM2 command and reader of the organisation calls `private.adm2_sweep(org)` first (the OPS1 page-load pattern). The lapse row's actor is the nil UUID (system).
5. **"Vertrag erfasst" (ADM2-6):** `record_member_contract(proposal, e-mail, start, end?)`, administrator only, for an `admitted` proposal. The account is made by hand (D-26 default A); the administrator names it by its **e-mail** (case-insensitive), because `/admin` must not list accounts outside the organisation. It creates the organisation membership with role `nurse` when she has none (an existing role is never changed, ADM2-7), the team membership **without qualification**, her ADM1 display name from the candidate record (only when she has no profile), and a `member_contract_events` row (dates only). No judgement field.
6. **Qualification (ADM2-6; engineering question 3):** `team_memberships.qualification` becomes nullable; only ADM2 writes a null. `set_member_qualification` (0018, patched by ABS1 0046) works **unchanged**: it reads the old value (null), `null = x` is not true, so it updates and logs old null → new. Until it is set she is never responsible or covering: `private.has_qualification` in `may_perform_visit` (take, ABS1 return), PLAN2 generation (primary and deputy are skipped, the visit stays uncovered as for an absent nurse), `plan_visit` (new code `qualification_missing`), `cover_team_for` (offers and accept); plus a BEFORE trigger on `care_visits` (`responsible_user_id`, `covered_by`) as the floor on every other path (raises 42501 `qualification_missing`). She may still be named primary nurse (J6 unchanged, ADM2-7); generated visits then stay uncovered and alert as today.
7. **Reads (ADM2-4):** no direct table read for `authenticated` on the three admission tables. `team_admissions(team)` returns the open and admitted proposals to the team's active members and the organisation's administrators (null to everyone else, including a person whose access ended); `admin_team_admissions(org)` lists every admission with team, outcome and date for administrators (ADM2-9; candidate fields only while the record exists). A pgTAP catalogue check proves no other function (AN1, AN2, OPS1, AUD1, export) reads these tables.
8. **ADM2-7:** no ADM2 command has a role, primary/deputy or qualification parameter (pgTAP on the signatures); `record_member_contract` adds only the proposing team.
9. **Decline (ADM2-8):** the brief names decline, lapse and contract. Engineering reads "decline" as the proposer's **withdrawal** (`withdraw_proposal`); an objection that stands ends in a lapse. OPEN for `po` if a separate team "decline" outcome is wanted.

**Deviations and OPEN points (for `po`):**
- **OPEN:** a team with **no other active member** admits on the proposal itself (`least(2, 0)` = 0 consents, the literal rule). The pilot teams have at least two members; the PO may want a floor of one consent.
- **Changed existing test:** pgTAP 018 asserted `col_not_null(team_memberships.qualification)`; now it asserts that no seeded member lacks one (the column is nullable by this brief). The LEAVE1 suites (074-078) and ADM1 suites (035-037) are unmodified and green on my local run.
- **Start date:** recorded; access **before** the start date is not yet withheld. Part (b) evaluates the contract window in `private.access_ended` (engineering question 1) and will cover both ends with one predicate.
- **e2e:** the seed's Testteam Nord has two members with passwords, so one consent admits there (the fewer-than-two rule); the two-consent path is in pgTAP 091. The outsider check uses the coach (org A, in no team); the "nurse of another team" refusal is in pgTAP 091.
- Seed: one unconfirmed, membership-less account `aufnahme.test@navora.test` (…958) for the e2e; pgTAP 002 counts confirmed accounts only.

**Tests:** pgTAP `091_adm2_team_admission.test.sql` (83 assertions: switch both states, ADM1 unchanged in both, proposal refusals, schema of the candidate record, no text column on the decision record, append-only, reads and refusals for six roles, catalogue of readers, two consents, objection blocks, late joiner, objection withdrawn then next consent, lapse one minute before / at 14 days, DST lapse, withdrawal, deletion on withdrawal/lapse/contract, no name in audit, contract recorded with role nurse and null qualification, planning / take / backstop refusals, the existing qualification path, role unchanged, signatures, administrator list, switch off again). Vitest `tests/unit/admission-rules.test.ts`. Playwright `tests/e2e/team-admission.spec.ts` at 390 px. **Run by me:** migrations 0001-0091 and the seed on local PostgreSQL 16 with an auth shim and a pgTAP stand-in: 091 green (83/83), the suite otherwise unchanged against `recovery/main` (the same 8 stand-in gaps red on both: 001, 007, 008, 009, 011, 053, 071, 077); 0091 applies twice. Vitest, lint, typecheck, build and Playwright **not run** (no npm registry here); `database.types.ts` edited by hand for the new functions (the types bot regenerates it). CI decides.

**Rollback:** revert the PR; 0091 only adds tables, functions and one trigger, and makes one column nullable (no row is changed by it).

### ADR-115 — ADM2 part (b): the recorded contract end ends access on time, and her responsibilities reach the team

**Date:** 2026-10-29 · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED in the ADM2 PR #647 (migration 0092; CI decides) · **Owner:** `eng`

**Context:** brief `docs/agents/briefs/ADM2.md` ADM2-11 to ADM2-15 and the Negative (po 2026-10-27, answers 2026-10-29 06:10); `cra` CGR-72 (blocking for ADM2 acceptance, S2), remediation plan `docs/agents/clinical/remediation/CGR-72.md`; `lca` 03:50 and 06:05 on part (b). Builds on LEAVE1 (0074-0078, 0085, 0087) and ADM2 part (a) (0091, ADR-114).

**Decision (migration 0092):**
1. **Input:** the contract record `member_contract_events` (0091; append-only, dates only, the newest row per organisation membership is current). New command `set_member_contract_end(person, end date | null, key)`: an administrator of her organisation (`private.leave1_org_for`, as LEAVE1), switch on, for ADM1 members too (their row has no start date). Refused: `self`, `in_past` (an earlier end than today needs `end_member_access`, G-23), `access_ended` (restore first), `no_contract` (nothing to remove), `last_admin` (as LEAVE1), `has_planned_visits` (a planned visit she covers for another team after the end, as LEAVE1-13; COVER1 is off). Audited (contract row trigger, `change_log`, `audit_command` with `contract_end_date` and `released_visits`); returns `{result, released: {team: n}, access_ends_on}` and never a count (ADM2-14).
2. **ADM2-13, engineering question 1: read time, no job.** `private.access_ended` (LEAVE1-3's single predicate) is also true from the start of the Zurich day after her current end date (`private.at_zurich(end + 1, 00:00)`; 00:00 is never ambiguous, so the two nights of the clock change need no special case: pgTAP 092 at 21:59/22:00 UTC on 24 October and 22:30/23:00 UTC on 25 October), unless an access event of that membership was recorded at or after that instant. Cost: one more probe of `member_contract_events_user_idx` per call; every RLS policy calls it once per statement (`(select …)`). Every LEAVE1-3 path (restrictive policy on every readable table, membership helpers, SECURITY DEFINER read models, `begin_command`, `push_active`, SIG1 receiver sets, `cover_team_for`, `may_perform_visit`) already reads the predicate, so the end is never late and needs no hosted job (D-2). `access_ended_at` and `my_access_state` return the contract instant; `signal_take_lapsed` (LEAVE1-14, LEAVE1-C5) also lapses a take made before the contract end. `restore_member_access` restores a contract-ended access (its `restored` row is after the instant); a changed end date before the instant moves it; visits already released stay released. The same predicate withholds access **before the contract start date** (ADM2-6 "active from the start date", po 06:10 (4)); `my_access_state.starts_on` lets the access screen name the date.
3. **ADM2-11:** `private.adm2_in_contract(org, person, date)` (start and end inclusive; true without a contract record) is read by `plan_visit` (`outside_contract`), `take_open_visit` (`outside_contract`), `team_open_visits.may_take`, PLAN2 generation (primary and deputy skipped, the visit stays uncovered), `cover_team_for` and the ABS1 return in `change_absence`; the 0091 BEFORE trigger on `care_visits` refuses every other path (42501 `outside_contract`, also when a visit date is moved). Recording the end date releases her planned visits after it, per team, by the one release rule `private.release_member_visits` (LEAVE1-13), text "Vertragsende", in the command's context.
4. **ADM2-12 / ADM2-15 (a):** `contract_end_handover()` lists, from 14 Zurich days before the end date (constant `private.adm2_handover_days()`) until her access ends, each leaver with end date, access-end date, her open cases as primary or deputy nurse (`case_id`, case reference = last six hex digits of the case id, team, role) and her responsible or covered visits from today up to and including the end date (reference, date, team, role). Readers: the organisation's administrators (all teams) and the registered nurses of a team she belongs to (that team's entries); not another team's nurse, a care assistant, a coach or Operations (pgTAP). Her open reports by kind come from the unchanged LEAVE1-12 list `member_open_reports` (same readers), fetched by the page for each listed leaver. No client name, address, health content or count.
5. **ADM2-14:** (a) `my_contract_end()` returns her end and access-end dates in the same window; `/work` shows "Dein Vertrag endet am …. Ab … hast du keinen Zugang mehr zu NAVORA. Exportiere vorher deine bestätigten Arbeitstage." above the unchanged count and export prompt. (b) the administrator's list entry has "Austritt vorbereiten", which calls the recorded `prepare_member_access_end` (0087, unchanged; one audit row per read, no count stored). No new reader of the count (pgTAP: none of the four ADM2 functions reads the working-day record).
6. **ADM2-15 (b):** `private.successor_needed(org)` lists open cases (assigned, lifecycle not closed) whose primary or deputy nurse's access has ended (any cause). `team_successor_items()` shows them to every active member of the case's team on `/team` ("Bezugspflege neu bestimmen" / "Stellvertretung neu bestimmen", resolved on the case card by `set_primary_nurse`, J6 and its 0078 guard unchanged); `private.derived_exceptions` gains one OPS1 exception per case and role, `successor_needed:<case>:<role>:<person>`, so take, comment, close and the sync's auto close apply unchanged and it disappears with its cause (a new nurse named, or a restore). NAVORA names nobody and does not promote the deputy (who holds the care-process responsibility is UNKNOWN, D-12, D-25).
7. **ADM2-15 (c):** nothing in 4-6 conditions or delays the access end.
8. **ADM2-8 clarified (po 2026-10-29 06:10, answer 3):** `private.adm2_sweep` also lapses an admitted proposal without "Vertrag erfasst" 14 Zurich days after its admission (same constant), deleting the candidate data in the same transaction; `record_member_contract` then returns `not_admitted`.

**Deviations and OPEN points (for `po`):**
1. **No row in the access record for a contract end** (brief ADM2-13: "with actor `system` and reason `contract_end` in the access record"). Any function that writes `member_access_events` would be a new entry in LEAVE1's catalogue check (pgTAP 074: the fixed list of functions that read the record), and the brief's Negative says the LEAVE1 suites pass unmodified. The end is therefore derived from the contract record at read time; it is deterministic and audited there (who recorded which end date, when). The administrator's member row shows "Zugang beendet seit … (Vertragsende)" (reason label `contract_end`, actor: none, i.e. the system) and offers "Zugang wiederherstellen". **OPEN for po:** if a row is wanted, the alternative is one sweep (`private.adm2_end_due_access`, run on page loads as the lapse sweep) and one more name in pgTAP 074's list.
2. **Push subscriptions** are not deleted at a contract end (no writer runs at the instant); `push_active` already returns false for her, so nothing is sent, and after a restore she needs no new subscription. LEAVE1 deletes them at a manual end.
3. **OPS1 priority:** `successor_needed` is P2 (OPS1's continuity class), no threshold, no escalation clock; the administrator can override it per organisation (ADM1-R). OPEN for po if another class is wanted.
4. **Switch:** recording the end date needs team admission switched on (`admission_off` otherwise), so LEAVE1 with the switch off is unchanged; an end date recorded while it was on keeps its effect if the switch is later turned off (privacy first).
5. **Case reference:** the brief names a "case reference" that the data model does not have; the last six hex digits of the case id are shown as "Fall-Ref. …". The team reads the case card itself; the reference is only the pointer.
6. **Changed existing tests:** pgTAP 019 and 061 count the operating rules (19 → 20); pgTAP 002 counts the seeded confirmed accounts (6 → 7, teamc.test); pgTAP 091: `set_member_contract_end` added to the catalogue of admission-record readers (it reads the switch only), and the lapse case D is proposed one day earlier so the admitted proposals keep their 14 days (point 8). The ADM1 (035-037) and LEAVE1 (074-078) suites are unmodified and green on my local run.
7. **Seed:** a third organisation, "Testorganisation C" (one team, one case, three synthetic nurses: teamc.test signs in, contract end in 14 days; vertrag.test, primary nurse of Testfall C1, contract ended the day before yesterday; bald.test, deputy, ends in 10 days), so the e2e sees day 14 and "the day after" without a clock and no org-A or org-B count changes.

**Tests:** pgTAP `092_adm2_contract_end.test.sql` (100): who may record (switch off, RN, coach, Operations, another organisation, self, past, nothing to remove); release in the same command with its audit rows and fixed text; dates only; unchanged and duplicate; planning, take, "Offen im Team" and the backstop refuse after the end date, the end date itself allowed, cover / PLAN2 / ABS1 read the window; day 15 nothing listed, no notice; day 14 listed to the team's RN and the administrator with both cases (reference and role keys only) and the visits up to the end date, not to a nurse of another team, a care assistant, a coach, Operations or another organisation, not to herself; her own notice; the recorded 0087 read; no count anywhere; 23:59 open, 00:00 ended without a command; no reads, commands refused, state with the contract instant and no reason code, take lapsed, no signal receiver, no access-record row; the day after: both items to Nord's members, not to another team or a coach, one OPS1 exception per case, P2 without threshold or clock; take, `set_primary_nurse` clears X's item, sync auto-closes its exception, Y's stays, the deputy is not replaced; restore clears Y's item and exception; the manual `end_member_access` still refuses `has_cases`; a moved end date moves the access end and released visits stay released; the two nights around the clock change; no access before the start date; ADM2-8 admitted proposal lapses at 14 days. vitest `contract-end-rules`. Playwright `contract-end.spec.ts` at 390 px: the `/work` notice on day 14 and the `/team` item the day after (plus the handover entry). **Run by me:** migrations 0001-0092 and the seed on local PostgreSQL 16 with Supabase auth stubs and a minimal pgTAP stand-in; 0092 applies twice; every pgTAP file: 092 (100/100) and 091 (83/83) green, the suite otherwise identical to `recovery/main` (the same six stand-in gaps: 001, 007, 008, 053, 071, 077). The pure rules checked in Node with type stripping. vitest, typecheck, lint, build and Playwright **not run** (no npm registry here); `database.types.ts` edited by hand (the types bot regenerates it). CI decides.

**Rollback:** revert the PR; 0092 adds functions, one rule row and one trigger column, and replaces function bodies (no row is changed by it).

**Addendum 2026-10-29 09:15 (ADM2-15 (c), po 08:15 from `uxa` UXR-42):** (1) the case card on `/team` shows one secondary line "Fall-Ref. <ref>" under the client name; `<ref>` is computed in the app from the case id the card already has (`caseReference`, `src/features/contract-end/rules.ts`), the same expression as `private.case_reference` (last six hex digits, upper case). Chosen over a new `case_ref` column in `team_cases`: no read path and no type change; the equality is pinned by the same two ids in pgTAP 092 (tests 101-102) and vitest `contract-end-rules`. (2) Each successor item carries one link "Fall öffnen" (outline, `min-h-11`) to `#kontinuitaet-<case id>`, the id of the card's "Kontinuität" section (`tabIndex=-1`); on click the section is scrolled into view and focused (the plain anchor still scrolls without JavaScript). The item's text, position, tone and colour are unchanged; no client name is added to the item or the leaver lists. A case not on the caller's `/team` (not expected: the item is shown to the team that holds the case) leaves the link as a no-op anchor. Tests: Playwright `contract-end.spec` "day after" at 390 × 844 (link, focus, in viewport, one "Fall-Ref." per card, item without client name).

### ADR-116 — VIS1-10: the move to today lands on the database's today, or is refused and offered again (`cra` CGR-91)

**Date:** 2026-10-29 · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED in PR (CI decides) · **Brief:** `docs/agents/briefs/VIS1.md` VIS1-10 (po 02:15, amended 03:10 from `aia` AUT-18 and 04:10 from `uxa` UXR-39) · **Issue:** #638 · **Remediation plan:** `docs/agents/clinical/remediation/CGR-91.md` · **Builds on:** ADR-111, ADR-112, ADR-113 (finding) · **Numbering:** ADR-114/-115 and migrations 0091/0092 are taken by ADM2 (draft PR #647), so this PR uses ADR-116 and 0093.

**Context:** `/field` computed "today" when the page rendered and "Heute machen" sent that date to `reschedule_visit`. Confirmed after 00:00 on a page rendered before it, the date was yesterday; `reschedule_visit` accepts a past date (the /team *Nachtrag*, ADR-063 / ABS1-C5), so the visit moved to yesterday while the form said "Heute" (ADR-113 finding, CGR-91 S2, blocking for VIS1).

**Decision:**
1. **Database (migration 0093):** a new command `public.move_visit_to_today(visit, shown_date, start, end, reason, key)` (SECURITY DEFINER, `search_path = ''`, `authenticated` only) is the only call behind "Heute machen". Order: no user → `rejected`; `begin_command` (key required, LEAVE1 access end, a retry of an applied move is `duplicate`); visibility through `private.visit_for_team`; then the day rule: if the shown date is not `private.at_zurich(private.now())::date`, the result is `day_changed` with `today` (the database's date) and the visit's unchanged `visit_date`, nothing changes, and `private.write_visit_refusal` writes one audit row (command `move_visit_to_today`, outcome `day_changed`, no idempotency key, as ADR-111 (2)). Otherwise it returns exactly `reschedule_visit(visit, <database's today>, …)` with all its checks and its `moved` row. A caller outside the team gets `reschedule_visit`'s own `rejected` (no date in the answer, no refusal row).
2. **`reschedule_visit` is unchanged** (signature, grant, behaviour): the /team move to a past date still returns `ok`. Wrapping instead of adding a parameter keeps the /team path and ADM2's 0092 untouched.
3. **App:** `moveVisitToToday` (server action) sends the date the form shows (a hidden `shown` field). On `day_changed`, `afterDayChanged(today, visitDate)` keeps the confirmation open when the visit is still dated after the new today: the sentence `dayChangedNote(today)` ("Inzwischen ist <Wochentag>, <Datum>. Die Zeiten sind neu gesetzt. Prüfe sie und bestätige noch einmal.", tone `info`, `tabIndex=-1`, focus on it) at the top of the form, "Heute, <Datum>" and the hidden field now the database's today, "Von"/"Bis" re-mounted with `prefillMoveTimes` for the new now (VIS1-3c), the typed reason kept, the VIS1-8 line and the confirm label unchanged, no success line. A malformed answer or a visit no longer after the new today gets the reload sentence (`DAY_CHANGED_RELOAD`, brief wording) with a link to today's view. After the second confirm the existing push to `/field` shows today's view with the VIS1-3d/-3e notice.
4. **Logging:** `command.refused` with `op` and `code` only (TD-17 allowlist).

**Tests:** pgTAP `093_vis1_10_move_to_today_lands_on_today` (29) at a pinned 00:05 Zurich: yesterday's shown date refused (`day_changed`; date, times, responsible unchanged; no `moved` row; one audit row without key; `today` and `visit_date` returned), the same key with today's date → `ok`, stored on today with one `moved` row naming today, retry → `duplicate`; a shown date after today refused; a coordinator outside the team → `rejected`, no refusal row; no user → `rejected`; the /team `reschedule_visit` to yesterday → `ok`; key required; grants (`anon` none, `authenticated` execute), definer, no tolerance in the source. vitest `vis1-10-day-changed` (sentence with weekday, no internal code, confirm-again vs. reload, malformed answers). Playwright `vis1-10.spec` at 390 × 844: browser clock pinned at 23:58 the evening before when the form opens, moved to the real now before the confirm; refused; the sentence focused (`document.activeElement`), `info`, the new date, the re-derived times, the reason kept, no success line; the second confirm stores today; nothing on yesterday or on the planned day.

**Deviations from the brief:** (1) the e2e cannot pin the server or database clock (ADR-081, ADR-113), so the page's pre-midnight date is simulated by setting the form's `shown` field to yesterday; the database's refusal at a pinned 00:05 is proven in pgTAP 093. (2) The e2e's second confirm types 22:40–23:10 after asserting the re-derived values, so the visit never shares its times with vis1.spec's prefill-based visit on today's list (issue #650). (3) VIS1-11 is not in this PR (allowed, not required; UX2-17). (4) The audit row's command is `move_visit_to_today` (the command she ran), not `reschedule_visit`.

**Not done (UNKNOWN, not coded):** any tolerance around midnight; whether field actions on a card that became past-dated at midnight need a rule (late documentation, CGR-87 (3), Clinical Owner).

**Checked by me (not CI):** migrations 0001-0090 plus 0093 and the seed on a local PostgreSQL 16 with Supabase auth stubs and a minimal pgTAP stand-in; 0093 applies twice; pgTAP 093 29/29; every other pgTAP file gives the same result as `recovery/main` on the same stand-in (89 of 90 green, `007` needs `col_default_is` with an untyped literal, which the stand-in lacks). The new pure rules and the vitest cases run by me in Node (type stripping, a minimal `expect`); `rules.ts` type-checked alone with `tsc --strict`; `field-client.tsx` and `commands.ts` syntax-checked with the TypeScript transpiler. vitest, lint, full typecheck, build and Playwright **not run** (npm registry refused here); CI decides. `database.types.ts` edited by hand; the types bot regenerates it.

**Rollback:** revert the PR. 0093 only adds one function; no data changes. Without it "Heute machen" falls back to the ADR-113 defect, so a revert needs the PO's agreement.

### ADR-117 — ADM2-16: a member without a recorded qualification performs no visit (`cra` CGR-92)

**Date:** 2026-10-29 · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED in PR #647 (CI decides) · **Brief:** `docs/agents/briefs/ADM2.md` ADM2-16 (po 10:15) · **Finding:** `cra` CGR-92 (blocking for ADM2 acceptance, S2; comment on #647, section in PR #662) · **Remediation plan:** `docs/agents/clinical/remediation/CGR-92.md` · **Builds on:** ADR-111 (refusal row), ADR-112 (VIS1-9 direct path), ADR-114 (ADM2-6) · **Numbering:** migration 0093 and ADR-116 are VIS1-10 (merged), so this uses 0094 and ADR-117.

**Context:** 0091 made `team_memberships.qualification` nullable for a member admitted by her team. ADM2-6 keeps her from being responsible or covering, but `start_visit`/`complete_visit` run as the member (`private.transition_visit`, security invoker), any team member may write `care_visits.status` (0003 column grant), and `private.enforce_visit_transition` checked qualification only for category B and needs assessments (CGR-12). She could depart, start and complete every other visit of her team.

**Decision:**
1. `private.actor_may_perform_visit(team, cover_team, covered_by)` (SECURITY DEFINER, `authenticated` execute): the caller has a recorded qualification in the visit's team, or she is the covering nurse (`covered_by`) of a covered visit and has one in the cover team. No user parameter: it answers only about `auth.uid()`, so nobody can probe a colleague's qualification.
2. **Command path:** `private.visit_qualification_refusal(command, visit, to)` is patched into `private.transition_visit` after the VIS1 day rule and before the update (marker `ADM2-16 / CGR-92`). Into `on_the_way`, `started` (start and resume) or `completed` without a qualification → `qualification_missing`; nothing changes; one `audit_events` row through `private.write_visit_refusal` (outcome code, no idempotency key, so a retry is evaluated again). Order: begin_command → visibility → day → qualification → state; a caller outside the team keeps the command's own `rejected`.
3. **Direct path:** `private.enforce_visit_transition` (same body as 0090) refuses the same transitions with `qualification_missing`, errcode 42501 (as the ADM2-6 backstop in 0091). The statement is rolled back, so there is no audit row (as VIS1-9).
4. **App:** `toCommandResult` maps `qualification_missing` to "Deine Qualifikation ist noch nicht erfasst. Einsätze führst du durch, sobald die Administration sie eingetragen hat." (`QUALIFICATION_MISSING_MESSAGE`). No screen change; the buttons stay and the database decides.

**Deviations / OPEN:**
1. `record_no_show` and `end_visit_not_completed` are not gated: they are SECURITY DEFINER and do not write through the trigger's `authenticated` branch (brief: "when they write through it"), and they record that a visit did not happen or was interrupted, not that care was given. With (2)/(3) such a member can never have departed or started a visit herself. OPEN for `cra`: if recording either fact also needs a qualification, it is a one-line addition to each command.
2. Pause (`started` → `paused`) passes: it is not performing. Resume is refused.
3. The sentence is Engineering's wording (no clinical content); `uxa`/`cra` may change its words.

**Not done (not coded):** billability of a visit already performed by such a member stays **D-29** (owner, legal adviser). No clinical rule added: the criterion is ADM2-6's own ("a recorded qualification"), not a skill rule.

**Tests:** pgTAP `094_adm2_16_no_visit_without_qualification` (32): a member admitted through ADM2 (switch, proposal, consent, "Vertrag erfasst") without a qualification: `start_visit` on an unassigned category-A-only visit dated today → `qualification_missing`, status and `visit_events` unchanged, one audit row without key; same key again → refused again; `depart_visit` refused; direct `update … status` to `started`, `on_the_way`, `completed` → 42501 `qualification_missing`; on a visit a care assistant started: `complete_visit` refused, `pause_visit` ok, `resume_visit` refused; cover check for the covering nurse only; no user parameter; after `set_member_qualification(…, 'care_assistant')` start and complete → `ok`; exactly five refusal rows. pgTAP 038 (CGR-12) and 090 unmodified. vitest `command-result` (the mapping).

**Checked by me (not CI):** migrations 0001-0094 and the seed on local PostgreSQL 16 with Supabase auth stubs and a minimal pgTAP stand-in; 0094 applies twice (the patch once); pgTAP 094 32/32 with 0094 and 18 red without it; 038 (38), 089 (41, 8), 090 (34), 091 (83), 092 (102), 093 (29), 076 (22) green; of all files, 13 stop at stand-in gaps (`has_table`, `function_privs_are`, …) before their assertions, the same as without 0094; all others green. `result.ts` type-checked alone (`tsc --strict`) and the mapping run in Node. vitest suite, lint, full typecheck, build, Playwright **not run** (no npm registry here); CI decides. No change to `database.types.ts` (private schema only).

**Rollback:** revert the commit; 0094 adds two private functions, patches `transition_visit` and replaces the trigger function; no data changes. Without it CGR-92 is open again.

### ADR-118 — OBS1: every observation shows who wrote it, and whether her qualification was recorded when she wrote it (`cra` CGR-93, `lca` 13:57)

**Date:** 2026-10-29 · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED in PR #673 (CI decides) · **Brief:** `docs/agents/briefs/OBS1.md` (po 14:35) · **Finding:** `cra` CGR-93 (advisory S2, G-23; section 13:40 in PR #666), `lca` 13:57 D-15 re-check (`decisions/D-15.md`) · **Remediation plan:** `docs/agents/clinical/remediation/CGR-93.md` · **Builds on:** ADR-117 (ADM2-16 rule), ADR-083 (RLS3-4 column grants), CSR1 (signed snapshot immutable) · **Migration:** 0095.

**Context:** IMPLEMENTED before 0095 (read in a database built from 0001-0094): `record_observation` (0040) accepts an observation from any active team member; `author_id` is stored but no qualification fact. `field_notes` (0009) returns only `own`; HAND1 (`private.handover_entries`, 0033) returns a member code ("Teammitglied 3"); the snapshot (`private.visit_report_snapshot`, 0016) holds no author. Since 0091 an author may have no recorded qualification.

**Engineering questions of the brief:**
1. *Column or derivation?* **Column** `visit_observations.author_qualification_recorded`, set by a BEFORE INSERT trigger from the ADM2-16 rule (`private.has_qualification` in the visit's team, or for the covering nurse in the cover team), for the stored author. A trigger instead of the command alone so that every insert path (command, seed, a later import) records it and a passed value is overwritten; the 0009 trigger already forbids updates, so the value is fixed at write time. Derivation from the 0018 change log was rejected: it would need the full qualification history per team, including ADM2's null rows, at every read.
2. *Every path that writes a snapshot:* only `private.visit_report_snapshot`. Callers: `create_report_draft`, `update_report_draft`, `sign_report` (stores the fresh snapshot whose hash the signer saw), `correct_report`, `return_report`; `field_reports` shows a draft's fresh snapshot. Changing the builder covers all of them; no signed row is written.

**Decision:**
1. **OBS1-1:** the column (null = written before 0095; no backfill, the table forbids updates and no history is guessed; nothing is deployed, D-1). No observation is refused.
2. **OBS1-5:** the column is **not selectable through the table API**: `authenticated` loses the table-wide select on `visit_observations` and keeps column grants on the twelve existing columns (the RLS3-4 pattern of 0070), so nobody lists or filters the marker per person. It is read per observation only through `private.observation_qualification_missing(id)` (SECURITY DEFINER, private schema, answers only for a member of the observation's team, null otherwise), which the INVOKER reader `field_notes` calls.
3. **OBS1-2:** `field_notes(date)` gains `author_name` (ADM1 display name through `member_profiles` under its own RLS; null without a name) and `author_qualification_missing` (true only when recorded as missing). HAND1 observation entries carry `author_name` and `author_qualification_missing` in `detail` (patch of `private.handover_entries` with a marker; other entry kinds keep the member code).
4. **OBS1-3:** the snapshot builder adds per observation `author_id`, `author_name` (at the time of the snapshot; a draft refreshes it until signed) and `author_qualification_missing`. Signed snapshots are untouched.
5. **OBS1-4 (app):** visit card (`/field`, "Beobachtungen"), HAND1 ("Seit deinem letzten Einsatz") and the report facts show the author's name per observation ("von dir" for the reader's own) and, where set, one line "Qualifikation war beim Schreiben nicht erfasst" in `info` tone (stone-700). A version signed before OBS1 shows "Wer dies geschrieben hat, ist in dieser Fassung nicht gespeichert", never a name. A member without a display name: "Teammitglied ohne Namen". The countersigning registered nurse now sees the report facts above "Gegenzeichnen" (before, the countersign view showed none).
6. `READ_PATHS.md`: `field_notes` listed under RLS3-4 with the task "authorship of a clinical entry (ZH GesG § 13, `lca` 13:57)"; pgTAP 071's RLS3-4 list gains exactly `field_notes` (its source filter now also matches `public.visit_observations`).

**Deviations / OPEN:**
1. All three on-screen texts are Engineering's PROVISIONAL wording; `uxa` owns them (OBS1-3, OBS1-4). No clinical or legal content.
2. Observations recorded before 0095 carry no marker value (null) and show none. Synthetic data only; no real observation exists anywhere.
3. A covering nurse cannot record an observation today (`record_observation` requires membership of the visit's team); the cover branch of the rule is in place for when that changes.
4. Playwright covers the signer (brief); the countersigner's view is covered by pgTAP 095 (the registered nurse reads author and marker in the signed version) and the component change, not by a browser test.
5. OBS1-6: `lca` (G-18, D-23 term b) and `cra` (CGR-93 gate) read the PR before merge. Engineering does not merge before both reads.
6. **`lca` 16:05 (D-23 re-check, residual):** the marker is closed only on `visit_observations`. `visit_reports.snapshot` stays selectable by the team through the table API (0016, unchanged), so the author id and marker inside new snapshots can be filtered per person by a direct query; `visit_observations.author_id`/`recorded_at` and `visit_reports.signed_by`/`signed_at` are row-readable as before. `lca`: compliant on synthetic data and not blocking the merge; closing these like RLS3-4 is compliant, as built needs the owner before G-18/G-24. Engineering tried the column revoke on `snapshot`: pgTAP 016, 026 and 049 read the snapshot as `authenticated` through the table, so closing it changes three existing suites; left for the PO to place (no app path reads `visit_reports` directly).

**Not done (not coded):** whether such observations are allowed, need a countersignature or a review (Clinical Owner, D-15, D-10); a legal determination of ZH GesG § 13 (adviser question 9; D-11); authorship of other entry types.

**Tests:** pgTAP `095_obs1_observation_authorship` (47): admitted member without qualification (ADM2 flow) records an observation on a visit a care assistant started → `ok`; stored marker false, a qualified colleague's true; a passed value is overwritten; the marker cannot be updated; column not selectable by `authenticated` (42501 through the table API), other columns are, anon none; `field_notes` gives a colleague her name and the marker on the visit and in the next day's handover, `own` kept, no marker for qualified authors; HAND1 entries carry name and marker; the performer's draft and the stored snapshot carry author id, name and marker; signed and read by the registered nurse with both; after `set_member_qualification` a new observation has no marker, the old one keeps it, the signed snapshot is byte-identical; a signed snapshot without author keys is read back and stays byte for byte unchanged; a nurse of another team, the coach, Operations and the administrator read no author; the helper answers nothing outside the team; fixed list of callable readers of observations; only four private functions read the marker. pgTAP 071 (RLS3-4 list +`field_notes`). vitest `obs1-authorship`. Playwright `obs1.spec.ts` (390 px; seed: Testteam C, today's 07:00 visit, `notiz.test` without qualification when she wrote).

**Checked by me (not CI):** migrations 0001-0095 and the seed on local PostgreSQL 16 with Supabase auth stubs and a minimal pgTAP stand-in; 0095 applies twice; pgTAP 095 47/47 with 0095, stops at the missing column without it; all other files give the same result with and without 0095 (91 green; 001, 007 and 053 stop at stand-in gaps both ways), including 011, 016, 033, 038, 040, 041, 066, 067, 071, 089, 090, 094 green. `authorship.ts` type-checked alone (`tsc --strict`) and run in Node. vitest suite, lint, full typecheck, build, Playwright **not run** (no npm registry here); CI decides. `database.types.ts` edited by hand for `field_notes` and the new column (db-types-sync corrects it if it differs).

**Rollback:** revert the commit; 0095 adds one nullable column, a trigger, two private functions, re-creates `field_notes` and replaces the snapshot builder and the HAND1 function; restore the table-wide select with `grant select on public.visit_observations to authenticated`. Signed snapshots written meanwhile keep their author keys (CSR1).

### ADR-119 — OBS1-C1 (OBS1-7): authorship and signer columns only through the per-visit read paths; ADM2-C1: the sweep ends an ended access's push subscriptions

**Date:** 2026-10-29 · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED in its own PR (CI decides) · **Briefs:** `docs/agents/briefs/OBS1.md` OBS1-7, condition OBS1-C1 (po 19:40); `docs/agents/briefs/ADM2.md` condition ADM2-C1 (po 14:35) · **Findings:** `lca` 16:05 D-23 re-check on #673 (its most protective option, `po-decided`); `cra` 17:30 condition on OBS1-7 (section in PR #680: pgTAP for performer, countersigning RN, team, F6R; Playwright for report view and handover; `cra` reads the PR before merge) · **Migration:** 0096 · **Tests:** pgTAP `096_obs1_c1_per_visit_read_paths_adm2_c1` (39); 011, 016, 026, 049, 066, 095 read through the functions; 077 writer list.

**Context (IMPLEMENTED before 0096, read in a database built from 0001-0095):** `authenticated` held a table-wide select on `visit_reports` (0016) and column grants on `visit_observations` that included `author_id` and `recorded_at` (0095). A team member could therefore select `snapshot` (since 0095 with every observation's author id and OBS1-1 marker), `signed_by`, `signed_at`, `author_id` and `recorded_at` row by row and filter them per person (D-23 term b). Every reader of these columns is SECURITY DEFINER except `public.field_notes` (INVOKER). No RLS policy and no view reads them; the app reads neither table directly. `private.adm2_sweep` lapsed admission proposals only; an access ended by the contract end (ADM2-13, evaluated at read time) kept the person's push subscriptions, while `end_member_access` deletes them.

**Readers checked (OBS1-7 (a), "the PR names each reader it checked"):** SECURITY DEFINER, unaffected by the grant: `field_reports`, `field_countersign_queue`, `field_returned_reports`, `report_returns` (F6R), `sign_report`, `countersign_report`, `correct_report`, `return_report`, `create_report_draft`, `update_report_draft`, `withdraw_report_correction`, `visit_correction_history`, `billing_preview`, `generate_billable_facts`, `final_report_visits`, `pilot_kpis` (AN1), `pilot_hypotheses` (AN2), `private.derived_exceptions` (OPS1), `private.working_day_proposal`, `private.handover_entries` (HAND1, `field_since_last`), `private.visit_report_snapshot`, `private.build_billable_fact`, `private.billable_fact_follow_hold`, `private.reads_visit`, `private.visit_facts_locked`, `private.open_reports_of`, `private.visit_open_return_performer`. INVOKER: `field_notes` only (changed, below). No export function reads the snapshot or observations (OBS1-5 fixed list); the billing path reads the snapshot hash as owner (pgTAP 022, 043, 049 unchanged and green locally).

**Decision:**
1. **OBS1-7 (a):** `authenticated` loses the table-wide select on `visit_reports` and keeps column grants on every column except `snapshot`, `signed_by`, `signed_at`; on `visit_observations` every column except `author_id`, `recorded_at` and `author_qualification_recorded`. The RLS3-4 pattern of 0070 (column grants, not a view or a policy: a policy cannot hide a column, and a view would add a second read path to keep in step). `anon` keeps nothing on either table.
2. `field_notes(date)` reads author and time of each observation through `private.observation_authorship(id)` (SECURITY DEFINER, private schema), which answers only for an active member of the observation's team, the same rows the table policies give her, and nothing otherwise. Rows, signature, output and order are unchanged; the 14-day handover window is applied to the time it returns.
3. **ADM2-C1:** `private.adm2_sweep(org)` calls `private.adm2_end_push_subscriptions(org)`, which deletes the push subscriptions of every member of the organisation whose access has ended (`private.access_ended`: a recorded end or ADM2-13), with one `audit_events` row per subscription: actor null, role `system`, `{"deleted": true, "reason": "contract_ended"}` (keys already classed `code` by AUD1). The sweep runs on the ADM2 page loads and commands (no hosted job, as ADM2-5/-8). A restore (LEAVE1-7) needs a new opt-in, as after a manual end. No access record is written, so the LEAVE1 074 catalogue is unchanged.
4. **OBS1-7 (b), (c):** `READ_PATHS.md` lists the five columns as closed under RLS3-4 and gains the inventory *Remaining person-linked column grants* (no change by it; the PO decides).

**Trade-offs:** a direct Data API read of these columns by a team member now fails with 42501; nothing in the app does it. New columns of the two tables are not selectable until granted (as for `visit_events` since 0070). The sweep deletes on the first ADM2 page load or command after the access-end instant, not exactly at it; nothing is sent meanwhile while `push_active` is false (ADR-115 (2)); whether a queued push checks `access_ended` was not re-checked here (UNKNOWN). The audit row has no actor because the end is the system's act, not that of the viewer whose page load ran the sweep.

**Deviations / OPEN:**
1. Test reads of `snapshot`, `signed_by`, `signed_at`, `author_id`, `recorded_at` as `authenticated` in 011, 016, 026, 049, 066, 095 now go through `field_reports` / `field_notes` (or, where a test asserts the stored value, as the table owner), with the same content asserted (OBS1-C1 named 016, 026, 049; 011, 066 and 095 also read them). 066 no longer orders two rows by `recorded_at` (the first sort key already separates them). 095's OBS1-5 grant assertion now expects `author_id` closed. 077's writer list gains `adm2_end_push_subscriptions` (a writer, not a reader of `audit_events`).
2. `cra` 17:30 asked for Playwright that the report view and the handover render for a nurse and a care assistant. No new spec: the existing `field.spec.ts` (F4/HAND1 handover and F6/F7 report as the nurse; the care assistant signs, F6R return and countersignature), `obs1.spec.ts` (nurse) and `lifecycle.spec.ts` run unchanged against 0096 in CI. A care assistant's handover view in the browser is covered by pgTAP only (095 `field_since_last`/`field_notes` as a teammate, 096). `cra` decides whether a browser test for that view is needed.
3. Inventory (c) only lists; closing any further column is the PO's call.

**Not done (not coded):** no clinical rule, no change to who may read a report or note (RLS unchanged); the live project is not touched (D-1).

**Checked by me (not CI):** migrations 0001-0096 and the seed on local PostgreSQL 16 with Supabase auth stubs and a minimal pgTAP stand-in; 0096 applies twice; every pgTAP file green with 0096 except 007 (one stand-in gap, same without 0096); 096 39/39 with 0096, 11 red without it (the five refusals, `select *`, count per author, grants, ADM2-C1 deletion and audit); the changed 011, 016, 026, 049, 066, 077 also green without 0096. vitest, lint, typecheck, build, Playwright **not run** (no npm registry here); CI decides.

**Rollback:** revert the commit; restore `grant select on public.visit_reports to authenticated` and the `author_id`, `recorded_at` column grants on `visit_observations`; `field_notes` from 0095; `adm2_sweep` from 0092. Deleted push subscriptions are not restored (a new opt-in, as after LEAVE1).

### ADR-120 — AIG1 part (a): AI governance foundation in the database (register, append-only ledger, per-value confirmation, field classes, stop rule, per-value confirmer reads, look-back)

**Date:** 2026-10-29 · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED in its own PR (part (a), database; CI decides) · PLANNED part (b): confirm screen, ticked list and health list UI with Playwright, vitest for the screen strings · **Brief:** `docs/agents/briefs/AIG1.md` (AIG1-1 to AIG1-13; AIG1-5 amended 2026-10-27 15:20 and 18:10, AIG1-11 added 2026-10-29 21:15, AIG1-12 added 2026-10-29 22:10, AIG1-13 added 2026-10-29 23:15) · **Findings considered:** `cra` CGR-75 (4), CGR-78 (a)/(b) (met at brief level, close on the builds), CGR-95 (= AIG1-12); `aia` AUT-1 (#543), AUT-22 (#686), AUT-23 (= AIG1-13, #695); `lca` 20:40 and 21:45 (D-35 terms 9 and (i)-(iv)); Hazard Log H-20 (automation bias), H-01 · **Migration:** 0097 · **Tests:** pgTAP `097_aig1_ai_governance_foundation` (110); 071 (+2, AIG1-11 (4) catalogue), 019 and 061 (21 OPS1 rules), 077 (writer and reader lists).

**Context (IMPLEMENTED before 0097):** no table, command or reader for machine proposals. The existing patterns used: idempotent audited commands (`begin_command`, `write_audit_event`), append-only guards (0091), no-grant tables read through SECURITY DEFINER readers (ADM2, AUD1), RLS3-4 column closing (0070, 0096), OPS1 exceptions derived from state (`derived_exceptions`, `exception_rules`), the LEAVE1-3 guard, AN1's rule of three.

**Decision:**
1. **Ledger shape (brief's open question 1): one generic ledger, one row per proposed value.** `ai_suggestions` holds agent key and version, organisation, team, case, the target record (`entity_type`, `entity_id`), the field, input references (`[{table, id}]`, a check function refuses any other key, so no content can be copied), the proposed value as `jsonb` or `not_found`, and the time; the rows of one proposal share `proposal_id`. `ai_confirmations` holds one row per confirmed value: outcome (`confirmed`, `corrected`, `rejected`, `not_found`), the value written (`final_value`), `bulk`, confirmer and time. A re-check after a stop is a new confirmation row of the same suggestion; the newest counts. Both are append-only for every role (BEFORE UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE triggers, so the table owner and `service_role` are refused too); no deletion path exists (D-4). Typed per-record ledgers were rejected: every consumer (PLAN3, A1, A4, A7) would repeat the guards, grants and readers.
2. **Register (AIG1-1):** `ai_agent_definitions` (written by migrations only: key, name, level, kind, version, provider and region (both null for `rule`, both required for `ai`), fixture, evaluation bar, brief) and `ai_agent_settings` per organisation (state, the written threshold, who switched it on and when, version/provider/region at the switch-on). `set_agent_state` (organisation administrator, audited, idempotent) is the only writer. Refusals return `result: refused` with a stated `reason` and a German `detail`: `ai_not_allowed` (D-35 rule A; hard-coded, so lifting it needs a migration and the owner's stage-1 package), `level_not_allowed` (D-34), `threshold_missing` (AIG1-8), `no_fixture` (AIG1-9).
3. **Field classes (AIG1-5):** `ai_agent_fields` with class `record` by default. A trigger accepts `schedule` only for `care_visits.responsible_user_id` and only with `class_brief` and `class_cra_ref`; it refuses safety-alert tables (`case_safety_alert_values`, `safety_alert_categories`), `care_plan_items`, medical-order columns (`cases.medical_order_*`, `case_gp_notices`), assessment records (ASM1's future `assessment_records`/`needs_assessments`), and a visit's date, time, team, cover team, minutes, assessment flag or services (CGR-78 (a)). The one field classed `schedule` is the test agent's `responsible_user_id` (cra 2026-10-27 17:35, #555). `ai_one_by_one_fields` is the Clinical Owner's list (D-17 part 3): empty, no command until the role exists (G-1, OPEN).
4. **Confirm (AIG1-3, AIG1-4):** `confirm_suggestion(id, outcome, value, key)` for one value; `confirm_suggestions(ids[], key)` for a ticked list. A value reaches the record only through `private.aig1_apply`, which calls the consuming command's checks per value; for the visit's responsible member they are those of `take_open_visit`/`plan_visit` (J3): `may_perform_visit` (membership, access, qualification, RN for B or assessment), contract dates (ADM2-11), absence (ABS1-C4), visit still open, and a stale proposal (someone assigned the visit meanwhile) is refused `already_taken`; the change is logged in `visit_plan_changes` (`taken`, "Bestätigter Vorschlag (AIG1)", actor = confirmer). A refused value returns its reason and writes neither record nor confirmation. A `not_found` proposal is confirmed only as `not_found`, writes nothing into the record, and cannot be turned into a value or an empty value (22023). A correction needs a non-empty value different from the proposal.
5. **Bulk (AIG1-5):** the whole list is refused (42501) unless every value is `schedule`, from a level-L `rule` agent, not on the organisation's list, not `not_found`, **and the agent is `on`** (engineering choice: a stopped or switched-off agent's open proposals are confirmed one by one; the brief is silent, this is the more protective reading, the PO may relax it). Each value is then applied separately; values the consuming command refuses are returned with their reason and stay open (PLAN3-9); each accepted value is its own confirmation row with confirmer and time. The ticked list's UI rules (unticked, no select-all, count on the button) are part (b).
6. **Scope (AIG1-6):** the synthetic test agent's command `propose_visit_responsible(visit, key)` raises 42501 and writes nothing unless the caller is an active member of the visit's team in its organisation (the rows RLS2/RLS3 give her); it reads only that visit and its case. No new read path.
7. **AIG1-11 mechanism:** no table grant at all on the seven AIG1 tables for `authenticated` and `anon` (stronger than column grants: every direct select, `select *` and a count grouped by confirmer is 42501, administrators included); RLS is on with no policy as a second wall. The confirmer is read (a) per value by `value_provenance(entity_type, entity_id, field)` (at most one row: the newest suggestion for that value and its newest confirmation; "vorgeschlagen von <Agent> / bestätigt von <Name> am <Datum>", status `offen`/`bestätigt`/`geändert`/`abgelehnt`/`nicht gefunden`) for the active members of the record's team, and (b) per record by the existing `read_audit_record('ai_confirmations', id, purpose)` (the confirmation's audit row: actor, outcome). No other function returns a confirmer (pgTAP 071 and 097 catalogue). `open_suggestions(team)` lists undecided proposals with the proposed member's name, never a confirmer.
8. **AIG1-7 suppression (the PO's small-team question):** yes, in a team of three a cell for a field that only one person confirmed that month would show that one person's accept/correct pattern.
